**What shows it's right.** `apps/server/test/network.test.ts` (16), `chatslack.test.ts` (14), `prefs-lists.test.ts` (4) in a server suite of 548 with none failing; the client suite 248; `e2e/chatform-sidemenu.spec.ts` (2), `chatform-slack.spec.ts` (4), `network.spec.ts` (3), and the older ChatForm, one-interface and Today checks re-run. Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-20_chatform-sidemenu/`, `2026-11-20_chatform-inbox-slack/`, `2026-11-20_server-on-network/`.

### 0.15 Whole-app usability, legibility, spacing & motion pass (Josh, 2026-11-22)

**The ask.** Apply [`jakubkrehel/skills`](https://github.com/jakubkrehel/skills) (`better-interface` across accessibility, layout, writing, typography, colors, UI polish and motion) across all of Foundation without changing its warm-paper / graphite / federal-blue / violet-agent look, feel or features — improving readability, spacing, keyboard usability, and motion hygiene.

**1. What a top professional adds.** Fix the root system before touching leaves (`packages/ui/src/tokens.css`, `packages/ui/src/index.ts`, `App.css`, `physics/spring.ts`, `App.tsx`) and lock the gains into permanent automated ratchets (`apps/client/test/usability.test.ts` alongside `design.test.ts`). Measure every foreground/surface token pair mathematically against WCAG 2.2 AA (`≥ 4.5:1`) and tune lightness only (preserving hue and warmth) so every caption, badge and status passes in both Day and Night. Honor `prefers-reduced-motion` across all three layers (CSS reset, `<MotionConfig reducedMotion="user">` for `motion/react`, and synchronous settlement inside `physics/spring.ts`). Remove `user-select: none` from `<body>` and scope it strictly to `.grid-viewport` and drag handles so prose, release notes, settings, messages and errors are selectable. Eliminate keyboard dead ends (`outline: none` without `:focus-visible`, hover-only toolbars unreachable via `:focus-within`, `<div/span onClick>`, missing `aria-modal`/Escape/focus-restore, missing Skip-to-content / `<main>` landmark, missing `scroll-padding-block-start` under sticky chrome, and uncontained scroll chaining into the grid behind).

**2. Rawest material.** The raw `jakubkrehel/skills` domain rule files (`better-accessibility`, `better-layout`, `better-writing`, `better-typography`, `better-colors`, `better-ui`, `better-motion`), Foundation's executable contract (`packages/ui/src/tokens.css`, `packages/ui/src/index.ts`, `DESIGN.md`, `apps/client/test/design.test.ts`, `apps/client/src/App.css`, `apps/client/src/physics/spring.ts`), all 35 stylesheets and 153 TSX files, mathematical WCAG luminance calculations on declared hexes, and live rendered Chromium DOM / accessibility tree / Day & Night screenshots.

**3. Methods and tools.** Static & mathematical contrast/a11y scanners, 4-worker parallel multi-model swarm (`tokens-core`, `shell-chrome`, `grid-panels-today`, `chat-settings`) in isolated git worktrees with zero file overlap and coordinator review/rework gates, permanent `node:test` ratchets (`usability.test.ts` + `design.test.ts`), `npm run check:security`, and real Chromium Playwright verification (`e2e/usability.spec.ts` plus existing `today`, `look`, `chatform` regression specs).

**4. What shows it's right.** Every ink/state/accent token in `tokens.css` passes WCAG 2.2 AA (`≥ 4.5:1`) across all four surfaces (`void`, `abyss`, `panel`, `raise`) and `--color-on-fill` passes across all solid fills in Day and Night (`usability.test.ts` 11/11 + live computed readouts on `#/design` → *Usability & contrast*); `design.test.ts` stays green (0 raw colours added, 0 `background`/`color`/`all` transitions); `:focus-visible` rules rose 31 → 217, `overscroll-behavior: contain` 11 → 92 across 89 scrollers, `scroll-padding-block` 0 → 61, `text-wrap` 2 → 136, `@media (hover: hover)` 0 → 63, `100vh` 7 → 0; `e2e/usability.spec.ts` passes 5/5 in Chromium with Day, Night, Room, CommandBar, ChatForm, Settings, and reduced-motion verification. Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-22_usability-pass/`.

**What is not verified.** Physical touchscreen hardware (gated via `@media (hover: hover)` and tested in Chromium), Windows High Contrast / `forced-colors` OS mode, screen-reader speech output (NVDA/VoiceOver), and the packaged Tauri desktop shell (verified in web Chromium).

### 0.16 The Mac: the top-left corner on the panel line, and the Character studio at speed (Josh, 2026-11-24)

**The ask.** "The top left today, chatform and expander icon is not lining up with the panel below … the tabs are too far out on mac and should be brought back to that panel line." And: the Character studio is slow on the Mac; progressive loading, cached images of the character videos and any videos or images, faster loading and navigating, "30-60FPS at all times, even with very heavy boards like the character panel."

**What was found.** *The corner*: the group over the open side menu was sized `paneW − 16` (the bar's inset on Windows), but a Mac's bar starts 84 px in for its lights, so the fold and its line sat 68 px past the menu's edge; at the menu's least width (220 px) it overflowed on Windows too. *The studio*: the Mac app draws in WebKit (Tauri's WKWebView), and `e2e/frames.spec.ts` measures Edge (WebView2) only, so the studio had never been measured in the engine it's used in. Measured in Playwright's WebKit 27.2 at 2× on a copy of this Mac's own data (11 characters, 30 inspiration pictures, 30 wires): pan at the opening zoom 60 fps, but **zooming 4.7 fps (frames to 740 ms) and panning after a zoom 1.7 fps**, where Chromium held 90–120 fps on the same board. Bisected by hiding one thing at a time on fresh pages: not the images, canvases, shadows or cards, but **the port-wire layer**: every live wire's pulse is an animated `stroke-dashoffset` under an SVG tunnel mask, on a layer the size of the grid, and WebKit re-renders masks and that animation whenever anything above them moves (Chromium composites them). Two smaller WebKit costs: every character card re-rendered as it passed the screen's edge (two states in `useOnScreen`), and **the persistent image cache never hit on a Mac**: v2 opened Cache Storage per read and write, WebKit answered racing opens with a new cache of the same name (79 called v2 after one load), and the next launch opened an empty one, so every launch fetched every picture again. The mesh avatar (2 MB) was fetched again on every launch too: WebKit didn't answer that `fetch` from its HTTP cache though it's marked immutable.

**What was built.** *The corner*: one inset for the bar (`--bar-inset`: 12 px, 84 px on a Mac), and the group's width is the menu's less that inset, so its line lands on the menu's edge on every computer at every width (`TopBar.tsx`, `spaces.css`); where that leaves too little room, ChatForm keeps its mark and then Today keeps its Shiba (container queries; the words stay the buttons' names). *Wires* (`GridViewport.tsx markCamera`, `AgentLayer.tsx`, `shell.css`): the tunnel mask goes on only a wire that passes under another; the pulse rests and the mask comes off while the board is drawn below 35 % (`FAR_ZOOM`) and while the camera moves, back 240 ms after it rests; the marks go on the wire layer only and are written only when they flip (on the viewport, each flip restyled every card and fed itself). *Cards*: `useOnScreen` keeps one state (near and big enough), checks only when the zoom or fit changes (a pan changes neither), only for cards near the screen, and reads every card in one pass a frame before writing (`PanelView.tsx`). A character card fetches its pictures and its set's inspiration once it has come within a screen (`CharacterView.tsx useSeenNear`); a seat's still is the 512 tier, not the original (`Avatar.tsx`). *Videos*: an autoplaying video plays only while on screen and not drawn small, and holds its frame when paused (`MediaView.tsx`). *Caches*: the image cache is opened once per page and is now `foundation-image-cache-v3`, the scattered v2 copies let go (`imageCache.ts`); a character's mesh avatar and clips are kept between launches in `foundation-byte-cache-v1` (keyed by file, 120 entries / 256 MB, oldest out, wiped at sign-out with the image cache; `lib/byteCache.ts`, `roster.ts`).

**What shows it's right.** WebKit, the studio from this Mac's data, 1440×900 at 2× (`docs/evidence/2026-11-24_mac-studio-perf/`): zooming **4.7 → 44–46 fps**, panning after a zoom **1.7 → 60 fps**, panning at 12 % **2.6 → 40–41 fps**, pan at the opening zoom and at rest unchanged at 60; Chromium on the same board 107–120 fps throughout (no regression). First load 94 requests / 4.3 MB → 46 / 3.0 MB in WebKit (Chromium 99 / 14.9 MB → 46 / 3.0 MB); **a relaunch with a persistent WebKit profile fetches 0 bytes of pictures or avatars** (was all 46 and the 2 MB avatar). Pulses run and the tunnel is cut at rest, paused and unmasked when far, day and night. `test/imageCache.test.ts` 10/10 (a new check: racing reads and writes open the cache once, with a stand-in that makes a cache per open as WebKit does). `e2e/today-corner.spec.ts` now also holds a Mac's corner: the line on the menu's edge at 220, 272, 340 and 420 px, Today clear of the lights, ChatForm before the fold, its name kept. Real-browser specs run with it (Chromium): `today-corner`, `character-studio`, `progressive-images`, `pointer`, `collections`, `council`, `fitted-frames`, `perf`, `sub-grid`, `card-chrome`: 30 passed, 4 failed, and the same 4 fail on `main` without these changes (§7).

**Not built, or not verified.** **Zoomed all the way out, panning in WebKit is ~40 fps, not 60**, and a zoom's frames reach ~100 ms at their worst: what's left is WebKit painting the studio's sections and wire layer as they move (each hidden alone gives ~45); the next step is drawing the wires at far zoom into one canvas, or culling cards' DOM off screen (nothing on the board is virtualised). **WebKit isn't in the repo's harness**: the numbers above came from scripts kept beside the evidence, not a spec (plan: *webkit-frames*). **Not run in the installed Mac app itself** (WKWebView, the production build): Playwright's WebKit is the same engine, not the same build. No inspiration videos are on this studio, so the video change was checked by reading, not by a played clip. The corner at 272 px on a Mac shows ChatForm as its mark alone: a wider menu shows the word.

### 0.17 The council at work, kinds of agent, and code mode (Josh, 2026-11-24)

**The ask.** The agents' actions and thinking over their heads on the grid, not in a strip above the toolbar; the text box above the head too, as one bar with the agent's name, mark, cloud or local and status; the box not transparent and not under the figure; no agent cut off at its sides; one silhouette, asleep, with a much smaller one behind it for adding. "The agents don't seem to be working": a loading animation, a small card of the plan as Claude Code makes, and the reasoning, at the avatar's top left and in the agents panel. A Claude API key "seemed like it was going through T3": T3 as its own kind, and three kinds of Claude (federated identity, an API key with help choosing the latest model, the subscription), with how this computer's Claude Code is connected said plainly. Harnesses for agent control of the grids, over MCP or code mode (universal-tool-calling-protocol/code-mode). All of it on section councils too.

**What was found first.** A Claude Code session a person runs is not connected to Foundation at all (the repo's `.mcp.json` lists only `details`); the *subscription* agent is the server starting `claude -p` on its computer's Claude sign-in. "Claude (API key)" in the add flow was a T3 preset, so the key did go through T3. Runs were finishing (the desktop app's own data: 13–15 s, `read_board` and `search`, then a refusal: "I can only see the first couple of lines of each digest note, and none of my tools can read the full text") but nothing showed it: the cursor stayed on *reading the ask*, the answer was only in the session tab, no reasoning was emitted, a failed MCP connection was ignored, the child had no timeout, and its MCP address was built from the listen address (`0.0.0.0` is no address to connect to). The text box went see-through from a global `.is-held { opacity: .35 }` its own class picked up, inside a blurred group the avatars' canvas painted over. Seats were a fixed 104 px; a mesh avatar draws 142.

**What a professional adds.** What a seat is belongs to the server (`agentkinds.ts`: the kind decides the runtime; a request's `runtime` is ignored, an unknown seat is 404, and no kind falls back to another's credentials). The plan and the reasoning ride the crew's awareness, so the card is the same for everyone on the grid, and the reasoning stops being shared the moment a run reads anything beyond the board (a DM reaches only its members). A run that says nothing for 90 s ends and says why. Model-written code runs in a real isolate (QuickJS in WebAssembly, in a worker thread), not Node's `vm` as the library does, and reaches only `runTool` with the caller's own context. The tool list's cost is measured.

**What was built, and what was not.** Built: the council's one bar, sizing, silhouette and run card (`characters/Council.tsx`, `RunCard.tsx`); the strip above the bar goes for seated agents and a person's own ask opens the grid's council; `thinking` events, `plan`/`thought`/`started` on awareness, the silence watchdog, one ending per run, a forced kill; four kinds (`claude-account`, `claude-api` with a sealed key and Anthropic's live model list, `claude-federated` on Anthropic's workload identity exchange, `t3`) and their routes; the add flow re-cut by kind with a model picker, a federation form and *Connect Claude Code or another MCP client*; `read_card`, `search_tools`, `run_code` and `/mcp?…&mode=code`; `FOUNDATION_AGENT_TOOLS=all|code` (default `all`). The contract is `docs/agent-harness.md`. **Not built, or not verified** (said here rather than implied): federated identity has only run against a stand-in token endpoint, never Anthropic's; the API-key way has only run against the stand-in (no real key was spent); no real turn called `set_plan`, so the plan card has been seen only from the scripted runtime (the prompt now asks for a plan on any ask of more than one step); a real run's reasoning goes quiet on the grid as soon as it calls `search` or `company`, which most do; `run_code` is bundled into the desktop server (`codemode-worker.mjs`, 0.9 MB) but was not exercised in the packaged app; code mode was measured on one real turn, where it was correct but slower (14.3 s and 4 calls against 11.6 s and 3), so it is not the default; the sleeping eyes are faint on a picture silhouette; ChatForm's "Claude" follows the `claude` key, so a server with only an API key shows it as off; T3 runs leave no plan or reasoning; the server on :4410 was not restarted, so the running app serves none of this yet.

**What shows it's right.** A second person's browser sees the run card with its plan over the same seat (`e2e/council.spec.ts`). Measured in the browser: the text box at opacity 1 with an opaque ground and `elementFromPoint` inside it, typing and held, grid and section, day, night and room; every figure inside its seat and none overlapping; the bar centred on its figure within 2 px; one silhouette and one small add; the run card above the window's foot at 1440×900 and 1280×700. `apps/server/test`: `codemode.test.ts` (escape attempts, a loop cut at the deadline with the server answering, a memory bomb, the 41st call, a viewer's write refused inside a script, `confirm` still needed, one write for one `request_id`, the real `/mcp` route in both modes), `agent-kinds.test.ts`, `agent-harness.test.ts`, `agent-claude-routes.test.ts` (the key sealed in SQLite and in no response, log, URL or child environment; agent bearers and the service token refused; the watchdog; reasoning withheld after a private read). An independent review found ten defects (a retried script replaying another call's result, `mode=code` not wired to the route, reasoning that could carry a DM to a grid's viewers among them); all were fixed and each has a test. Two real Claude Code turns through the new server finished with the right table (`docs/evidence/2026-11-24_council-harness/REAL-TURN.md`). The tool list an agent reads: 82 tools, about 14,100 tokens; in code mode 7 tools, about 1,270 (`node scripts/measure-tools.mjs`). 637 server tests, 290 client tests, typecheck and `check:security` pass; the affected browser specs pass (council 9, section-agents 5, agent-setup, crew 6, build 6, one-interface 5), and the council was driven by hand in a browser on a throwaway instance with the starter characters. Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-24_council-harness/`.

### 0.18 The Character grid at rest: kept pictures, a wave of arrival, and what plays (Josh, 2026-11-25)

**The ask.** "Performance improvements for all the grids, particularly the Character Grid. We should cache the images of the 3D characters and be much smarter on progressively rendering them, using fading or animations to make the transition appear smoother. Also … fundamental performance improvements to help render things."

**What was found** (a copy of the real data, a production build, headed Edge on the RTX 5080 at 2×; `docs/evidence/2026-11-25_character-grid-perf/notes.md`). The tiered pictures and the two-level cache (§0.13, §0.16) were already working once the merge brought them: a cold studio is 57 requests and 2.99 MB with no originals, a reload 0 requests. An ordinary grid holds 60 fps. What dropped frames on the studio was the characters: **every card on screen played**, and opening a mesh avatar is **one 170–555 ms task** in the runtime's loader (it reads its portrait's pixels and builds its mesh on the page). Cards started as they came on screen and were destroyed as they left, so arriving at the characters ran at 38 fps (six long tasks), panning over them 47, zooming 42, with 31 WebGL contexts made and thrown away in one pass; at rest five live avatars kept the main thread 70 % busy. Around that: pictures on the cards popped in (no stand-in, no fade; a grid picture's blur jumped to sharp), a character's pictures were kept under an address with no version (a sheet drawn again would have shown the old one from the cache), stills were cut on the page into `data:` addresses every launch, and every card was two compositor layers.

**What was built.** *Rest and play* (`characters/stagePolicy.ts`, `panels/CharacterView.tsx`): a card rests on a picture and plays when the pointer is on it (and 1.4 s after) or when it is drawn at 80 % of its size or more, started only while the camera is calm, as many as the stage budget allows; the picture cross-fades to the live character and back on opacity (`Avatar.tsx` `PosterAvatar`). *The 3D character's still is the model, drawn once* (`characters/portrait.ts`): the first frame of its idle clip under the stage's own lights and camera on a clear ground, kept in the image cache in memory and between launches under the character, the clips file's id, the framing and the size (theme is not in the key: a clear ground and fixed lights look the same day and night); one renderer, one at a time, only for cards that have come near. *A mesh avatar that stops is kept ready* (three at most, the runtime's manual mode, advanced only by the card showing it): its second wake is instant. *Stills* are cut in a worker and kept in the image cache under their versioned address (`characters/cutout.ts`, `cutoutCore.ts`, `cutoutWorker.ts`). *Versions* ride in a character picture's address (`imageCache.ts withVersion`, `roster.ts characterFilePath`). *Arrival* (`lib/arrival.ts`, `progressive-image.css`): a cold picture fades in from its well, its stand-in comes into focus instead of jumping, and each waits a few steps by its distance from the middle of the screen (half of `--dur-press` a step, six at most), so a section fills from the centre outward: grid pictures (`MediaView.tsx`), a card's pictures and picks, collection tiles, stills; opacity, blur and a 3–4 % scale only, off with reduced motion. *Layers*: a card inside its anchor is no longer a layer of its own at rest (`App.css`).

**What shows it's right.** Same data, same steps, before and after: arriving at the characters **38 → 54 fps** (long tasks 6 → 1), panning over them **47 → 60** (3 → 0), zooming out and in **42 → 59** (8 → 0), panning after a zoom **47 → 60**; script at rest over the characters **1,409 → 381 ms** per 2 s; WebGL contexts made in the pass **31 → 4**; `data:` pictures 1.2 MB → 0; layers 432 → 366 (the company board 168 → 152); cold and warm requests unchanged (57 / 2.99 MB, then 0). `PERF_GPU=1 … frames` passes on the build from before and the build after, within a frame or two of each other. `test/characterRest.test.ts` (9: the keys and what changes them, made-once, the still-or-live rule, the wave's order, the cut); client tests 336/336, server `imagelod` 8/8, typecheck clean. `e2e/council.spec.ts` 10/10 with a new check: a 3D card at rest has no canvas, shows its model's 640 px still on a clear ground kept under `derived:model-still/<character>/<clips file>/full/640`, plays under the pointer and goes back. `character-studio` 9/9, `collections` 6/6, `card-chrome` 7/7, `perf`, `renders`, `card-use`, `fitted-frames` pass; day and night pictures mid-load and loaded beside the notes; `#/design` → Progressive cached images shows the wave.

**Not built, or not verified.** **A mesh avatar's first wake is still one 170–555 ms task** (once per character per page now, when you rest on it or zoom in to it): the fix is in `mesh-avatar` (a worker, or a mesh made ahead in the `.mavatar`). The board's first render is still one long task (§7). **WebKit was not measured** with these changes. The real studio holds only mesh avatars, so the model's still was checked on the stand-in's GLB, not on a real model. Two e2e checks fail for reasons outside this work (§7).

### 0.19 A run's trace on the council, the silhouette that adds, and CAD cards fed by an app (Josh, 2026-11-25)

**The ask.** No second silhouette: the + goes on the one random silhouette. When there are agents, that silhouette is much smaller and peeks out from the side, as if around a wall. More padding in the line to an agent. Pointing at an agent on the council should open the panel on the right with the agent's profile minimised at the top, its chain of reasoning, and whether it succeeded: "one of the agents tried and failed, but there was no trace of what he tried and what happened afterwards". Then the real task behind that failure: an agent goes into BomForm, takes the STL files, and makes new 3D CAD nodes in the section to its right. Each node is wired to BomForm so it updates when BomForm does; cut the wire to orphan it, or cut and paste it to work on it another way.

**What was found first.** The failed run was in the desktop app's own data (`agent_runs`, 2026-11-26 00:34 UTC). It set a plan, called `app_tools` on "Airform BOM" ("has no MCP address on its card"), read the card, found the agent browser closed, tried `find_repo` from `run_code` (GitHub not allowed on that board), and said so in its answer. It ended `done`, so nothing marked it as failed, and the only place it could be read was the session tab. Runs weren't findable by seat: `agent_runs` didn't record the name the cursor wore. "Airform BOM" is PartForm (`partform.apps.normallab.co`, on HyperForm). It signs people in through OnShape, its parts' geometry lives in OnShape, and it has no MCP and no public API (only server functions behind its sign-in). The agent browser refuses downloads by design (`hostbrowser.ts`). `call_app_tool` dropped any file an app returned (`[resource]`). A file card picked its viewer by the card's name, so a card named for its part drew no model. The grid had no copy and paste of cards.

**What was built.** *Council* (`characters/Council.tsx`, `council.css`): one silhouette, and it is the button, with the + on its chest. Alone it is full size. Beside agents it is half size, tucked behind the last agent's right side and leaning out round it. The line to an agent is at least 304 px wide and 38 px tall, with 13 px text and room around it. *Pointing at an agent* (260 ms) shows its pane as a sheet over the grid, so nothing under the pointer moves; ⇥ or a press keeps it open (`AgentInfo.tsx`). Its profile is a 52 px bust with name, maker, model and where it runs, and the facts fold under *About*. *Its runs, step by step* (`characters/RunTrace.tsx`): what was asked; how it ended (Working, Done, Failed, Stopped, or *Didn't finish: k of n steps* when its plan was left undone, which is how the BOM run above reads); then each thought, each tool with its arguments and its answer (failures in red), what it said, and the error it ended on. ‹ › steps through its last three runs, and the pane keeps an agent that has left the council. The runs come from the window's live stream, or from `GET /api/agent/seat-runs?board=&name=`: the asker's own runs only, matched by `agent_runs.crew_name` (new). *CAD fed by an app* (`apps/server/src/cadfeed.ts`, `docs/cad-feed.md`): `call_app_tool` keeps a file an app returns (an MCP embedded resource with bytes, at most 64 MB) in the grid's files, archived and never deleted. `add_cad` makes a 3D card from a file id, or from an app card's tool, wired from the app's `cad` port to the card's `model` port. `refresh_cad` and `POST /api/boards/:id/cad/:panel/refresh` ask again, and so does a 10-minute timer while the board is open and the wire is there; new bytes swap the model and the old file stays. What a card asks, and in whose name, is the server's record (`cad_feeds`), never the card's `feed`, which only displays it: a copied or hand-written feed is never asked. *The fed card* (`cad/CadFeedStrip.tsx`, `feedWords.ts`): where its model comes from, when it changed and was checked, what it waits on or what went wrong, and Refresh. Cut the wire and it reads "Unlinked: keeps its last model". The model fills the card, and a card named for its part takes its viewer from its type. *Copy, cut and paste* (`lib/cardClipboard.ts`, `state/cardClip.ts`, `store.putCards`): ⌘C, ⌘X and ⌘V on the selected card. A copy brings no wires, so a pasted CAD card is an orphan. Cards with server state of their own are refused. A file card pastes only on the grid it came from. Files and pictures on the OS clipboard still paste as files. The agents' instructions say how to take a part's model out of an app, and what to say when the app can't be reached.

**Not built, or not verified.** **No real STL has come out of PartForm**: it has no MCP or API. What it needs on its side (an MCP with `part_model` returning the STL as a resource, from OnShape's export) is in `docs/cad-feed.md` §"What the app has to offer". Until then, an agent asked to do it says so. The agent browser still refuses downloads; letting it download into the archive is a security decision left to Josh. The CAD path was checked against a stand-in app, in tests and in the browser. Copy and paste takes one card, because the board has a single selection. Reasoning in the trace is the asker's alone, and others on the grid see only the run card. The pane sheet was checked with the scripted runtime, not a real Claude turn. Nothing here has reached the server on :4410 until the desktop app updates.

**What shows it's right.** `apps/server/test/cadfeed.test.ts` (5) tests a stand-in BOM app over real MCP:
- Before a person's Allow nothing is called and no card is made.
- After it, the STL is kept, the card is wired, the same `request_id` calls nothing, the same bytes change nothing, and new bytes swap the model with the old file kept.
- A part gone leaves the card's model and says why.
- A cut wire is a 409 with no call.
- A forged `feed` in the board (someone else's name, wired by hand) is never asked.
- A viewer is refused.

`seat-runs.test.ts` checks that runs are the asker's own, by name, newest first, failures included, and that LIKE's wildcards are escaped. The client's `card-clip.test.ts` (7) covers what a copy keeps and drops, and where a paste lands. In the browser, `e2e/cad-feed.spec.ts` (2) runs: a person saves the app, the agent's add_cad asks, Allow, two 3D cards beside the app wired from it, Refresh takes a taller part, then the wire is cut and the card is unlinked with no Refresh (a 409), in day and night. `e2e/council.spec.ts` checks one silhouette with the + on it at full size; half size and overlapping the last agent once one is seated; pointing shows the sheet with the small profile; and a run's steps in the pane while it works and after it has left. Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-25_council-trace-cad/`.

### 0.20 Zooming out without the flicker; ChatForm at the top right, a conversation pinned beside the grid, and dragging both ways (Josh, 2026-11-25)

**The ask.** "The progressive loading flickers a bit when I zoom out of the grid … it has trouble re-loading things … It should be very focused on loading things in the view port, and starting the progressive load as the scroll goes in and out." And: "Chat Form should be able to pin a conversation on the right panel, perhaps chatform is actually a top right level tool … the ability to drag things in from foundation into the chatform chat box and vice versa."

**What was found.** Zoomed out, a grid picture's level drops to 64 px, and a 64 px copy was always drawn as the *blurred stand-in* (blur 8 px, 3 % scale): every picture went soft as the zoom passed, then "came into focus" on the way back. Pictures loaded only within one screen of the view *now* (an IntersectionObserver, 100 % margin), so a zoom out of more than 3× brought in cards that hadn't started or had shed to their 64 px copy. Levels also swapped down mid-gesture (a decode and a new texture each).

**What was built.** *Loading for where the camera is going* (`lib/viewAhead.ts`): GridViewport publishes its springs' goal with every frame; one pass (every 90 ms while moving, rects read together) marks the cards in the view the camera is heading for, plus half a view around it, so they start as the gesture begins. A zoom in loads the level for the goal's scale; a zoom out keeps its level until the camera has been calm 320 ms. A 64 px copy is blurred only while a cold picture waits for its first real level, never when it is the right size or shed (`MediaView.tsx`). *ChatForm at the top right*: one pill in two halves heading the tools: the word opens the whole of ChatForm, the panel half opens the conversation pinned in the right panel (`chat/ChatButton.tsx`). *Pinned*: a pin on ChatForm's head and on a chat card's head (`chat/PinRightButton.tsx`, `chat/pinRight.ts`, kept on this device) puts the conversation in the tools pane's ChatForm tab (`chat/PinnedChat.tsx`, the chat card's live conversation, now `ChatConversation`), with a switch and an unpin; an unread dot on the panel half. *Drag in* (`chat/chatDrop.ts`): a card carried off the grid (`lib/carry.ts`) or a search result (`lib/pickup.ts`) held over a conversation rings it; let go, the card stays and goes into that composer as a draft: a picture as the file itself, any other card as a link (a message of type "link"; on a chat server of its own, words that say where). *Drag out*: a message's gutter is its handle; dropped in a section it becomes a note with who said it and when, its files coming with it from Foundation's archive.

**What shows it's right.** `test/viewAhead.test.ts` (5: the ahead rect at rest, for a zoom out, a zoom in, a glide; a link in words); client 341/341, server 664 passing, typecheck clean. In a browser on a copy of the data (1440×900, night): the pill and the panel half at the top right, the picker, #general pinned beside the grid, a card link dropped into its composer and sent, rendered as a link card (`docs/evidence/2026-11-25_chat-panel/`).

**Not verified.** The zoom-out fix was not measured or watched in a browser (no frames trace, no before/after); a real pointer carry onto the panel and a message dragged out onto a section were not driven, only the composer's drop event. Day was not looked at. No e2e spec yet.

**Follow-up (2026-11-26, Josh: "a pill with just the chatform icon and a small number icon if there are notifications … channels should be collapsable to icons").** The top right's ChatForm is now one pill holding the mark alone, with the count of what's waiting beside it (the word stays the button's name, out of sight); it opens ChatForm, and a conversation goes to the right panel from the panel button in its own head. ChatForm's channel list folds to a 56 px column of icons from a control at its top (kept on this device): each conversation's mark with its name as a tip, its count or an unread dot on the mark's corner, hairlines between the sections, Unreads left out. Checked in the browser on a copy of the data (night, 1100×700): the pill, the list folding to 56 px and back to 256 px (`docs/evidence/2026-11-26_chat-pill-icons/`). Not looked at: day, a live count on the pill, the column with many unread rows.

### 0.21 The timeline, voice and video notes, menus that rise, and a chat card without its paper (Josh, 2026-11-25)

**The ask.** "The menus for notes should be about 20% larger, and should actually animate up, not moving the text down, keep it steady, same with the chat window. I think the chat window should also have a feature to make the background invisible. I'd like to add a timeline feature to every grid that also maintains its history of adding and deleting things, and chats that were had during that time frame … connected to a larger timeline of the whole grid, but you can temporarily scrub the timeline of a section. You should also see the agents that are working on the grid, and people's interaction with the grid, you should see video notes that people leave, and audio notes. There may in fact be agent work that is done behind the scenes of a section … terminal level commands … harness prompt, or agent council which should be a top meta level header for every section and every grid. Remember a section is a grid or a view and link to a grid. Actually have we implemented audio and video notes yet … construct a good architecture to support this for the on-going Foundation data model, and to support more primitives. Let's design the timeline nicely, something that has a time icon in the section and grid header."

**What was found first.** Only the present was kept: a board's update log is squashed every 500 writes and the document garbage-collects, so nothing could say what a grid held on Tuesday. Activity is 400 lines inside the document with no section on a line. A Replay take keeps a grid's changes, but only while a huddle records. Agent runs had no section; the crew, a T3 thread's section and a terminal's screen lived in memory. Voice and video notes were never in Foundation: they were Flowform's `video_note`, which Foundation only imports as video cards. And the card's bar pushed its content down 38 px on purpose (2026-11-16), which is what this ask reverses for notes and chat cards.

**What a professional adds.** One model for all of it, written down before any code (`docs/timeline.md`): a *place* (a section, or `*` for the grid, the council's own word), a *meta layer* every place has (council, timeline, and two parts reserved and not built: a harness prompt and a terminal of its own), and two records the server alone keeps: **history** (the document's changes with their times, on the take's own wire, so `TakePlayer` plays it) and **events** (one line per thing that happened, in a lane, by kind; a new primitive adds a kind and nothing else). Who did a thing is the session's or the transaction's to say. Words that belong to a conversation are read from the conversation for each reader, never copied. An independent review after the build, whose findings were fixed before this was written (below).

**What was built.**
- *Menus that rise* (`ObjectFrame.tsx` `barRise`, `RichText.tsx`, `App.css`): a note's and a chat card's bar is 36 px (was 30) and sits just above the card, rising 6 px into place; the formatting toolbar is 40 px (was 34) and hangs under the card's foot. The card's content does not move or resize when either shows. Other cards keep the bar that makes room.
- *A chat card without its paper* (`PanelDoc.bare`): the note's Background button on the chat card's bar; the conversation stands on the section, its composer still on raised paper.
- *History and events* (`apps/server/src/timeline.ts`, `packages/protocol/src/timeline.ts`): every board change kept in 2 s bursts with who made it, checkpoints so a moment is rebuilt from the nearest one, pointers once a second for 14 days, a budget per grid (256 MB) past which the oldest history is dropped and the timeline says where it now begins. Events in six lanes: People, Agents, Behind the scenes (tool calls, scripts, machine commands: work with no cursor), Changes, Chat (asks, answers, a conversation's messages, huddles), Voice and video. `GET /api/boards/:id/timeline`, `GET /api/boards/:id/history`, and the tool `read_timeline`.
- *The timeline* (`apps/client/src/timeline/`): a clock in the grid's tab and beside every section's name; `H`; a band along the grid's foot with the lanes and one playhead; scrubbing draws every section as it was, for this person only, with pointers where they were; what was said around the moment and the notes left then, playable there even after their card is gone; a section's own bar that takes only that section into the past, shown on the grid's band, with *Follow the grid*. A section that is a grid opens that grid's timeline.
- *Voice and video notes* (`apps/client/src/notes/`, `MediaDoc.note`, `media: "audio"`): a microphone in a section's tools, the section menu and ⌘K; the microphone or camera opens only on the press of Record; review, record again, discard, leave it; a voice note is a player with its drawn wave, a video note rests on its first frame and plays with sound. Agents read a note as what it is.

**What the review found, and what was done.** Eight defects, three proven by failing tests: a card with a malformed name lost its whole burst and left every later change unplayable (history is now written apart from events, each event derived alone, and the document read as untrusted); a failed tool call's request id swallowed its successful retry; a subsystem's write on a timer was credited to whoever's request armed the timer (now only a request's or a tool's own write is credited to its person or agent); any viewer could make the server rebuild a whole history per request (checkpoints, a small cache, and a cap that answers 413); an agent's name could come from the request (`?as=`), a note's author from the card, a viewer's withheld ask was answered in the open, and a kept pointer trusted its window's name. Each has a test.

**What shows it's right.** `apps/server/test/timeline.test.ts` (21) and `media-notes.test.ts`; `apps/client/test/timeline.test.ts` (16) and `media-notes.test.ts` (18); in real browsers `e2e/timeline.spec.ts` (8: add, add, remove, scrub between them and see the removed note drawn formatted while the live one is hidden; a section alone; a second person sees none of it; a viewer scrubs; notes recorded with fake devices played from the moment after their cards were removed; 860 px wide, 700 px tall; an older server's 404 said plainly), `e2e/media-notes.spec.ts` (5), `e2e/card-chrome.spec.ts` (a note's rects identical before, during and after its bar and toolbar). 382 client and 695 server tests, typecheck and `check:security` pass. Driven by hand on a throwaway server: a note's first line at the same pixel with its bar up; a deleted note back in its section at 23:06, formatted, with the banner; events credited to the right person. Measured: a 20-edit burst's flush 0.47 ms; a moment in a 28 MB history 21–69 ms; a scrub at 16.7 ms a frame (headless, a small grid). Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-25_timeline/`, `2026-11-25_media-notes/`, `2026-11-25_menus-rise/`.

**Not built, or not verified.** A place's harness prompt and its own terminal; restoring anything from the past; a purge of history; transcripts; a note left *for* someone. Not verified: WebKit and the Mac app for recording (an `Info.plist` now declares the microphone and camera, unbuilt and unsigned here); the timeline on a busy board or with `PERF_GPU=1`; ChatForm on a chat server of its own (the moment reads back 200 messages there; never exercised); the desktop app. The rest is §7.

### 0.22 The timeline, live and whole; a note and a chat card as one box (Josh, 2026-11-26)

**The ask.** "Perhaps the timeline should come from under the top menu of the section since that's where you call it. I think the notes card shouldn't have a double outline when it's selected and editing … the menu up top should be an extension of the box, it just doesn't need to push the text down, it just pushes the box up … the formatting should be in the box at the bottom, but you should be able to right click format selections, in addition to other things to the note. The same goes for the chat box menu … The very fine print below the text input in the chat box should be removed. The text box for chat shouldn't be rendered as a rounded [box] inside the box, simply divide the chat area from the input with a line … The video and audio note might not actually work. None of the actions that I took, making notes, creating tables, putting in tables, moving objects around, creating audio notes, video notes, were captured by the timeline. All of these actions should be captured by the timeline and can be played back again, every user interaction, every agent interaction … make the primitives better, and also ensure the timeline is robust and comprehensive. Follow best practices in deterministic architecture."

**What was wrong, found.** (1) An open timeline read itself again only every 45 s, so what you had just done wasn't on it while you looked. (2) Dragging or resizing a card inside its section was not an event at all (only a move to another section was); the history held it, the timeline didn't show it. (3) A card's first stretch of edits was folded into its add for five minutes, so filling a table you had just made, or typing in a note you had just made, left no mark. (4) **Determinism:** an event was stamped when the server saw its change, but the change was kept with its burst's later time, so jumping to an event could draw the board a moment before it happened; events at one millisecond came back in a random order. (5) Every event line's quotes were broken characters (a curly quote saved as three wrong ones in `timeline.ts`). Where the edits behind the report went is not known: no database on this computer holds them (the dev data's last board change was 2026-11-21, the app's own 2026-11-23), so that session was on another server or copy.

**What was built.**
- *Live*: whenever the server keeps a burst or records an event it nudges every window on the grid over the board socket (message type 2: a time, nothing else; each window reads again as itself). An open timeline at now reads again within 1.5 s, at most once per 1.5 s; one looking back keeps what it has and reads again on returning to now; a grid the window isn't joined to is read every 20 s. Measured in a real browser: a person's typing is on the open band about 2.9 s later (the 2 s burst, then the nudge).
- *Whole*: `card.place`, one per card and actor for each stretch of dragging or resizing (20 s quiet), "moved" or "resized". A card's making is one event: what it is filled with in the same burst (an agent's `add_note` writes the card, then its words) is part of the add; anything after is its own `card.edit`, one per card and person until they pause a minute (was five).
- *Deterministic*: an event from the document is stamped with the time of the burst that keeps its change (never earlier), so the board rebuilt at any event's moment holds that event's change; events at one moment are ordered by when the server recorded them (the row's place), the same for every read and every reader. Tested by replaying a person's whole session and comparing the board after each step with the rebuilt one.
- *The section's timeline* unfolds from under its header, where its clock is, over the top of its body (was: along its foot).
- *Edges resize* (2026-11-26, Josh: "Most of the edges of notes and other panels should be re-sizable, using the highlight animation on the bottom right corner"): every `ObjectFrame` card (notes, images, videos, panels) resizes by its right and bottom edges as well as the corner, and a floating one by its left edge and bottom-left corner too (its right side stays put); each edge gleams like the corner under the pointer, pulse while pulled. The top is the card's bar. Maps keep their corner only. Checked by pointer events on a throwaway copy (right +65, bottom +52 at 15 % zoom, left grows with the right edge fixed) and on `#/design`; not yet an e2e. Evidence `docs/evidence/2026-11-26_edges-format-more/`.
- *A note being written is one box*: its bar is the box grown up (flush on the top edge, the card's paper and edge, a hairline between), its toolbar the box grown down (flush on the foot; one line, and what a narrow note can't fit waits under **More**, a small sheet below it, last buttons first; 2026-11-26), both unfolding from the card's edge; one selection ring round all of it, none on the words. **Right-click in the words**: the selection's formatting (✓ on what it has), styles, lists, quote, Link…, Clear, Cut/Copy/Paste, text size, then the note's own card menu.
- *A chat card's field is its foot*: a hairline over it, no box, radius, shadow or ring of its own; nothing under it (who is typing is a line over the timeline's foot, only while someone is; the "Voice needs Foundation's own server" line is gone, the missing headset says it).

**What shows it's right.** `apps/server/test/timeline.test.ts` (22: a new test drives a person's note, table, cells, drag, resize, move, video note and removal through their socket and checks each is one event in order, the board at each event's moment is exactly the board after that step, a second drag is the same event, two reads agree, and the window was nudged with a bare time). In real browsers `e2e/timeline-live.spec.ts` (2, run twice: with the band open and never reopened, a typed edit, a filled cell and a drag each appear credited to the person; scrubbed before the drag the note is where it was; a note being written is one box in day and night, its first line still, one ring; right-click, Italic writes `_word_`), and `timeline` 8/8 and `media-notes` 5/5 again (voice and video recorded on Chromium's fake devices, played by a second person, played from the timeline after the card is gone). Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-26_timeline-live/`.

**Not built, or not verified.** Voice and video notes are verified in Chromium with fake devices only: not with a real microphone or camera, not in the desktop app's WebView2, not on the Mac. "Every interaction" means every change to the grid and every agent tool call, run and script: a pointer that only moves is kept as cursor samples (once a second), not as events; what happens inside a hosted app (a page, a machine's screen) is not. History is thinned to 400 steps per answer, so on a long stretch an event's moment can draw a step that also holds its neighbours.

### 0.23 The grid behind the grid: how the council thinks, and what T3's agents work on at once (Josh, 2026-11-26)

**The ask.** "Integrate https://github.com/sodiumsun/agenttrail to show how the agent council is thinking, even opening up a hidden grid to show what they are thinking and working on … which can be seen by expanding their thinking module. Also place this behind the T3 Agents … T3 agents that are spawned by in-built T3 [should] have underlying access to Foundation Grids, and understand sections in which they are working on and modifying the data structure and code for … visualized somewhere that you can look at to see what they are working on concurrently."

**What was found first.** agenttrail (MIT) is not a trace viewer: it is a live map of *where* each coding agent is working in a repo. Components are cards in columns along their `needs`, each with a status ring; every agent's run is a card of its todos and last tools with their durations; a dotted trail in the agent's colour goes through the components it touched; a handoff is drawn where one agent picks up after another; an unfolded card hangs its tasks on a dashed tether. It reads Claude Code and Codex logs and hooks, and it never runs an agent. Foundation already had the parts it lacked: the council's run trace (§0.19), and T3 threads that reach the grid through Foundation's MCP as a crew member (`t3threads.ts`). What Foundation didn't have was a record of a T3 run. The trace came from `agent_runs`, which T3 threads never write ("T3 runs leave no plan or reasoning", §0.17), and nothing recorded which sections and cards a run touched or which files a T3 agent wrote in its folder. An agent also had no way to know another was working in the same section.

**What was built.**
- *The work trail* (`apps/server/src/worktrail.ts`). Every tool call by a crew member goes through `runTool`, so every Foundation run and every T3 thread with board tools records the same things:
  - the few words its cursor already shows;
  - the section and card it acted on (a card it made counts);
  - whether it worked, and how long it took;
  - its arguments and a line of what came back, kept for the person who asked.

  A council run also keeps each stretch of its reasoning and of what it said, also for the asker only. A T3 thread's folder is watched (`fs.watch`, recursive) for the files it writes, by path and size, never their contents. The pass file, `.git`, `node_modules` and build output are left out. The trail lives in memory for three hours (`FOUNDATION_TRAIL_KEEP_MS`); the council's full runs stay in `agent_runs`. `GET /api/boards/:id/trail` answers anyone who can see the grid, and nobody else (404). It only reads.
- *Who sees what.* This is the invariant that an agent's reasoning is not shared with a grid once its run has read beyond the grid.
  - Everyone on the grid sees the shape: which step, where, whether it worked, and the files by name.
  - Reasoning, a step's arguments and what it answered go only to the person who asked.
  - Once a run has read beyond the grid (`PRIVATE_READS`), others see only "reading" or "changing the grid" and the sections it stood in: no step words, no cards, no file names.
- *T3's agents know where they are, and who else is at work* (`concurrent_work`, a read tool). It returns:
  - the caller's own home section and the sections it has touched;
  - for each other agent at work (Foundation's own or T3's): its section, what it is doing, the cards it has changed and the files in its folder.

  It never returns another run's words. A T3 agent's brief now names the tool, says to call it before changing anything and again before leaving its section, and says that its steps and files show on the board's work trail.
- *The grid behind the grid* (`apps/client/src/trail/`). It opens over the grid from two places:
  - **Expand ⤢** on an agent's thinking module: its pane's run trace, now under a *Thinking* head;
  - **See their work** in the agents pane's T3 block.

  It is agenttrail's map on Foundation's tokens (`trail/model.ts`, `TrailGrid.tsx`, `trail.css`):
  - **Sections** are cards, laid out in columns along the wires between their cards.
  - **The status ring** on each card shows an agent's violet arc while one is in it, a check once written, and ! when the last step there failed.
  - **Busy and shared sections:** *Editing*, *Revising* or *Retrying* shows when a section changed in the last minute. A warn outline and *Two at once* show when two agents write in it within two minutes.
  - **Agents' paths:** a dotted trail in each agent's crew colour runs through the sections it went through. A handoff bar ("A ⇢ B") shows where one agent picked up after another had finished.
  - **A T3 agent's folder** is a card of its files.
  - **Run cards** on the right show what each agent is doing, its plan, and its last five steps with how long each took.
  - **Following an agent:** pressing its run card dims the others and hangs its chain under where it stands: the ask, then each thought, tool and answer.

  Wheel zooms and drag pans. Near, a section lists the cards changed in it; far, only the names show. Esc goes back. The map is on `#/design` → The council → *Behind the grid*.

**Not built, or not verified.**
- **A T3 agent's own reasoning isn't on the trail.** T3 keeps its transcript and Foundation reads only its thread list. Its steps on the grid and its files are on the trail, and the chain says so.
- **A T3 agent without board tools** (Codex, or a launch line a person changed) shows only its files and its home section.
- The trail is in memory: a server restart forgets the last three hours of T3 runs (the council's are in `agent_runs`, but the map reads only the memory).
- The trail is read every 1.5 s while open, not pushed.
- `fs.watch` on a folder with very many files hasn't been measured.
- Not checked with a real T3 agent or a real Claude turn: only the stand-in T3 (`t3-standin.mjs`, now with `T3_STANDIN_WORK`: read the board, `concurrent_work`, two files, a failed read, a note) and the scripted council runtime.
- The server on :4410 serves none of this until it restarts.

**What shows it's right.** - **Server, `apps/server/test/worktrail.test.ts` (5).** It runs against the stand-in T3 end to end and checks that:
  - a T3 thread's MCP steps land on its lane with section, card, time and outcome;
  - its folder's files show by path and size, never the pass file or `node_modules`;
  - the asker sees arguments, answers and reasoning, and someone else sees only the shape;
  - `concurrent_work` names the other agent's section, cards and files and never its words;
  - once a run reads beyond the grid, others see no words, cards or files;
  - reading never changes the board's state vector;
  - an ended run is kept and no longer watched.
- **Client, `apps/client/test/trail.test.ts` (7).** It checks:
  - columns along wires, safe on a loop, five to a column;
  - the rings and the Editing, Revising and Retrying words;
  - contested sections and handoffs (not when too late, a read, or a failed write);
  - trails and folders;
  - edges and file kinds.
- **In the browser, `e2e/trail.spec.ts` (4) and `e2e/council.spec.ts` (10, now with Expand from the agent's pane): 14 passed.**
- **Full suites.** Server: 700 of 701 tests pass. The one that failed under load, `pagecard`'s real browser, passes alone in 8.8 s. Client: 389 of 389 pass, and typecheck is clean.
- **Evidence:** `docs/evidence/2026-11-26_work-trail/`.

### 0.24 The updates panel: the whole release history, the version large (Josh, 2026-11-27)

**The ask.** "Keep a strong sense of history in the updates panel, and keep the version number prominent in that panel and the latest changes."

**What was found first.** The Changes tool's Releases face showed only the hand-written posts in `docs/blog`: 0.2.0 and 0.3.0. Since 2026-11-13 every push to `main` has been a CI release (0.3.2 … 0.3.56 on 2026-11-27, fifty tags), each with notes written into its own build and never committed, so an installed app knew 0.2.0, 0.3.0 and itself, and a checkout's client called itself 0.3.0 (the checked-in base). The version was nowhere large.

**What was built.**
- `docs/releases.json`: every `v*` tag with its date, commit and changes (the subjects since the tag before; plan syncs, merges and bumps left out), written by `node scripts/release.mjs history` and again by each `ci build` with the version it builds on top, before the client is built, so every installed app carries the whole history up to itself.
- The client is told where it stands: `vite.config.ts` bakes in `git describe --tags --long`; `settings/history.ts` `standing` reads a CI build as its stamped version and a checkout as *the tag it is ahead of, +n*.
- The Releases face (`settings/ReleaseHistory.tsx`): this app first, its version in display 40 with when and from which commit it was released, then **What's new** (a checkout first lists what it hasn't released, from the server's commits); then **History**: the tally, a strip of releases per day, and every release by day, each opening to its changes and, where a person wrote one, its post. The version also sits as a pill at the faces bar's right on every face, and the Code face marks the commit each release was cut from.

**Checked.** `apps/client/test/release-history.test.ts` (5: the file, an entry, git describe, standing for a CI build / a checkout / a tag / nothing, by day, the tally, cuts, the unreleased); e2e `bottom-bar.spec.ts` (the tools test: the version, more than 40 releases, 0.3.0's post opening in its row, cuts on the Code face, `docs/evidence/2026-11-17_bottom-bar/10_changes*.png`); `#/design` → Release history by day and night (`docs/evidence/2026-11-27_release-history/`). **Not checked**: inside the desktop app (a CI build carries it from the next release on); Settings → Releases still lists posts only.

### 0.25 Pings: a sound, the bell, straight to the card; Ping in the right-click; who has worked on a grid (Josh, 2026-11-27)

**The ask.** "Referencing someone in chat pings them with a sound and adds to their notifications that they can go and access the part of chat or the board that they were referenced at. A ping command in the right context menu … someone that we've recently pinged, or someone who is working in that board or has items associated to them. Keep meta data like this: who has been working in the board, whether they are human or agent."

**What was there.** An @mention already reached the person's bell (chat/notify.ts), and a desktop notification if they'd turned that on, but there was no sound, and a notice always opened ChatForm. A chat link to a card only switched to its grid. The board's `activity` log had people's and agents' lines by name, bounded at 400, leaving out typing.

**What was built.**
- **Who worked on a grid** (protocol `workers`, `WorkerDoc`, `noteWorker`, `workersOf`). One row per party: user id for a person, `agent:<name>` for an agent (the same id a chat mention uses). Each row has its name, kind, first and last time, a count, and for an agent the person it worked for. The server keeps it alone (it's in `SERVER_MAPS`, so a socket's forged row is put back). A person is noted from any change on their socket, typing included, at most once a minute (gridlog.ts). An agent, or a person at the HTTP door, is noted from their tool calls (tools.ts `touch`). The client reads it as `board.workers()`.
- **Ping in the right-click.** A card's menu leads with *Ping someone about this…*, and a section's with *Ping someone about this section…*. Either opens a picker at the press (chat/PingPicker.tsx) with a field and four groups (chat/pingLogic.ts): *Recent* (whom I pinged, this device's), *On this* (the card's maker or assignee), *Here now* (on this grid now), and *Worked on this grid*. Each party appears once, never me. An agent ChatForm doesn't know is shown dimmed. Picking one sends, by that press, a direct message that @mentions them and links the place (a message of type "link"). For an agent ChatForm knows, it goes to my conversation with it. Recent is updated.
- **Their end.** A mention or direct message they aren't looking at sounds two soft notes (chat/pingSound.ts, Web Audio, at most one per 1.5 s; never for @channel or @here; Settings → General → *A sound when you're pinged*, on by default). The bell row shows where it points (↗ card · grid). Pressing it opens that grid with the camera on the card (lib/openPlace.ts). Recent mentions in ChatForm still opens the message. A link in a conversation now frames the card too, not only the grid.

**What shows it's right.** A server test (activity.test.ts) covers people from their socket (typing too), agents with whom they worked for, most recent first, and a forged row put back. Client tests (ping.test.ts) cover the groups, the filter, Recent, the words, and when it sounds. In the browser, on a copy of the data, at night, with a second person signed in through the API, I checked that:
- Kai, after adding a note, is offered under *Worked on this grid*;
- Enter sent the ping, and Kai's mentions list has it, with the @mention and the place;
- Kai's ping back rang both notes and counted on the bell;
- the bell row showed the place, and pressing it from Today opened the grid with the note in view, the bell cleared (`docs/evidence/2026-11-27_ping/`).

**Not verified.** The sound wasn't heard: the check counted its two tones. A second real browser window wasn't used. Day theme, pinging an agent, a section's ping, and a chat server of its own weren't tried. On such a server the place is folded into the words, and agents can't be pinged.

## 1. What Foundation is

| | |
|---|---|
| **For** | Everyone at Form Industries, and their agents |
| **Is** | A shared, infinite, spreadsheet-like grid of sections. A section holds cards: notes, tables, maps, media, and **panels**: live apps, web pages, machines, files and CAD, company connectors, agent jobs, and asks to people |
| **Above the grid** | The **agent layer**: wires between app ports, rings where agents are working, and an activity feed of who did what |
| **Agents can** | Work in the open as **the crew** (named cursors, §0.3), and run the whole interface through one tool surface (§4): make sections, notes and tables, organize, open and wire apps, bring a person to what they made, find repos, read company systems, brief Vision, ask people for CAD (Hermes), queue reverse-engineering (REA) |
| **Runs** | Locally with one command (`npm run dev`), on macOS, Linux or Windows. Desktop via Tauri (`npm run desktop`: the server and the window together). The packaged desktop app carries its own server and Node, and starts it with the window (2026-11-09) |

### 1.1 Architecture

```
                 ┌────────────────────────── apps/client (React 19 + Vite, :1430; Tauri shell) ──────────────────────────┐
  people ───────▶│ Login (ASCII wind) · Side pane · Grid (springs, WebGL paper) · App sections · Agent layer · ⌘K · Dock │
                 │ store.ts (60 fps local model) ⇄ sync/board.ts (Yjs mirror, diff by identity) ⇄ WebSocket /sync/:board  │
                 └───────────────▲──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────▲──────────────────────────┘
                                 │ postMessage bridge (foundation:input/output/ports)          │ HTTP /api /auth /files, SSE /api/agent
                     embedded apps (iframes)                                                     │
                 ┌───────────────────────────────────────── apps/server (Node 22 + TS, :4410) ───┴──────────────────────────┐
                 │ boards.ts  Y.Doc per board · update log + compaction · awareness · FTS indexing                         │
                 │ auth.ts    Google via flowform (flowform.ts) / dev login · hashed sessions · service token                        │
                 │ files.ts   content-addressed uploads · range streaming · text, STEP/IGES headers, documents · archive    │
                 │ search.ts  FTS5 (board, files) + Slack + GitHub + apps, local-first                                      │
                 │ tools.ts   the board tool surface ──▶ agent.ts (Claude Code subscription | Anthropic API)                │
                 │                                    ──▶ mcp.ts (/mcp — Claude Code, Codex, Vision attach here)            │
                 │ connectors.ts  FactoryForm (:3001 REST) · BomForm (remote MCP) · Vision (T3 :3783, Archive :63727) …     │
                 │ hermes.ts  asks + nudges (board activity, Slack DM)   jobs.ts  REA reverse-engineering (person presses Run)│
                 │ machines.ts  Kiri or Docker over the Engine API socket · a shared terminal (a person presses Start)        │
                 └─────────────────────────────────────── SQLite: data/foundation.db · files: data/files/<sha256> ─────────┘
```

### 1.2 Repo map

```
apps/client      React grid, shell, panels, login, design page (src-tauri for the desktop build)
apps/server      the Foundation server (src/*.ts, test/*.test.ts)
apps/client/src/characters   the council and the avatars (3D on three.js, mesh on mesh-avatar)
apps/rune        the Rune renderer (Rust → wasm, its own Cargo workspace): the board drawn on the GPU, beside the web renderer
packages/protocol  board schema (Y.Doc layout, PanelDoc, LinkDoc, AgentEvent) — one schema, no copies
packages/ui      tokens.css + TS tokens, springs, night engine (Vision's language, Foundation's additions)
docs/evidence    screenshots per slice
SPEC.md DESIGN.md README.md CLAUDE.md
```

---

## 2. The board document (packages/protocol)

One Y.Doc per board, with these top-level maps:
- `grid.tracks`: `{cols, rows}`
- `sections`: id → `{name, c0, r0, c1, r1, host?}` — `host` is the app (panel id) the section hosts: it fills the section and its ports sit on the section's edges
- `objects`: id → table | map | note | media | **panel**
- `cells`: tableId → `{"c,r": raw}`
- `links`: id → `{from:{panel,port}, to:{panel,port}, label}`
- `huddles`: panel id → `HuddleState` (who's in a huddle now, whether it records), written only by the server
- `machineAsks`: machine panel id → `MachineAsk[]` (2026-11-08): the commands agents asked to run there, waiting, running or answered; written only by the server, off the card, the last six per machine
- `meta`: `schema: 6`, name
- `activity`: bounded array (400) of `{at, who, role, text, objectId, for?}`: `role` is who really acted (a person, an agent, a service); an agent's line names the person it worked for in `for` (2026-11-08)

**Panel kinds:** `app`, `link`, `file`, `machine`, `agent`, `connector`, `ask`, `stage`, `huddle` (a video portal; its live state (who's in it now, whether it records) is in `huddles`, kept by the server), `slack` (a channel; `channel` is its id — a huddle opened for a channel carries it too). A `machine` is a web desktop reached by `url` (noVNC, ttyd, code-server) or, with `machine` set, a Linux machine on Kiri or Docker whose state (`MachineState`: name, image, engine, state, ports, who proposed and started it) the server keeps from what the engine says. Each kind has optional `url`, `repo`, `mcp`, `fileId`/`mime`, `ports[]`, `outputs{portId: value}`, `status`, `assignee`, `sessionId`.

**Rules:**
- A section never scrolls: it fits its cards to its body (the wheel always belongs to the grid). How it's fitted and whether it's expanded are local to the window, never shared.
- `blob:` URLs never enter the document.
- The schema version lives in `meta.schema`. flowform shipped unversioned fields and paid for it; bump it and migrate on change. Migrations are numbered steps in `apps/server/src/migrate.ts`, run at boot. **2:** sections host apps — a section of only apps (the company board's six connectors) becomes one section per app where its card was, other sections shift, nothing overlaps; a section holding one app hosts it (`apps/server/test/hosting.test.ts`). **3:** a note's text is a `Y.Text` in `noteText` (keyed by note id), written as the smallest edit (`setNoteText`, `putNote`); the card in `objects` no longer carries `text`, so typing never rewrites its position or size (`apps/server/test/notes.test.ts`). **4:** each table's cells are a `Y.Map` ("c,r" → raw) per table in `cells`; edits write only the cells that changed (`setTableCells`, `tableCellsOf` reads either form) (`apps/server/test/tables.test.ts`). **5:** every table has its cell map from the start, made in the same change as the table: a map made lazily by two people at once is two maps, and one is lost (found by the two-browser test). **6:** a huddle's live state moves off its card into `huddles` (panel id → `HuddleState`), which only the server writes (`huddleStateOf`, `setHuddleState`); the card no longer carries `huddle`, so a move made as someone joins can't overwrite it (`apps/server/test/huddle.test.ts`, `e2e/huddle.spec.ts`).

**Machines (2026-11-15).** `MachineState` gains (all optional, no migration): `wantDesktop`, `desktop { state: installing | ready | error, detail, by, at, w, h }`, `agentsMay { by, at }`, `agentAsked { by, for, at }`, `agentActs[]` (the last five: `by`, `for`, `did`, `at`), `io { inPreview, inBytes, inAt, outPreview, outBytes, outAt, error }`. A `LinkDoc` end may name a note (port `text`) or a table (port `rows` or a cell) when the other end is a machine's `in` or `out`; the server reads and writes those ends (`machineio.ts`). New socket: `/machines/:board/:panel/vnc` (RFB bytes only; tickets as for the terminal). New table: `person_prefs (user_id, key, value, updated_at)`.

**Grids of grids, councils and Foundation's own cards (2026-11-15).** A board row has a `parent` (the grid it sits inside). The board's `agents` map (place → `PostedAgent[]`; a place is a section id or `*` for the grid) is the server's alone, like `huddles` and `characters`. Three panel kinds: `grid` (`board`: the child grid it stands for), `kanban` and `design` (`source`: what it shows), all hostable. A crew member's presence carries `via` (which agent it is).

**Collections (2026-11-17).** No migration: everything is optional. A panel kind `collection` (ports `collect` in and `items` out, type `any`), and a server-only map `collections`: panel id → `CollectionState { items: CollectionItem[], into?, laid?, intoBy?, intoAt? }`, where an item is `{ key ("file:<id>" | "chr:<character id>"), kind: image | character, name, src?, fileId?, w?, h?, character?, panel?, form?, from, at }` and `laid` maps an item's key to the card laid for it in `into` (kept when that card is moved or removed, so it isn't laid again). `CharacterState.copyOf` names the original of a character card's copy. A pipeline `output` node has an output port, `characters` (`OUTPUT_PORT`; older studios get it from `upgradeStudio`).

**The timeline, and notes left to be played (2026-11-25).** No migration: everything is optional and the schema stays 6. `MediaDoc.media` gains `"audio"`; `MediaDoc.note { by, byName, at, ms, fileId, mime, peaks? }` marks a recorded voice or video note (who it says it is by is the card's claim; the timeline's event says who left it). `PanelDoc.bare` takes a chat card's paper away. `agent_runs` gains `section_id`. History and events are not in the document: SQLite tables `timeline_base`, `timeline_updates`, `timeline_cursors`, `timeline_events`, `timeline_boards`, written by the server alone (`docs/timeline.md`; the kinds are `TIMELINE_KINDS` in `packages/protocol/src/timeline.ts`).

**Sub-grids (2026-11-17).** No new fields. `extract_section` turns a section into a board whose `parent` is this one; the section then hosts a `grid` panel (`board`: the new grid). A `grid` panel whose board's `parent` is not the board it sits on is a link left behind (`grid_leftovers` in SQLite lists them); nothing on the card says so, the outline does.

### 2.1 The Foundation bridge (apps ↔ grid)

An app inside a panel talks to the grid with `postMessage`:

| Direction | Message |
|---|---|
| app → grid | `{type:"foundation:ports", ports:[{name, dir:"in"\|"out", type}]}` declares its ports |
| app → grid | `{type:"foundation:output", port, value}` emits a value; wires carry it on |
| grid → app | `{type:"foundation:hello", panel, ports}` on load |
| grid → app | `{type:"foundation:input", port, value}` whenever an upstream value changes |

An app can also publish `/.well-known/foundation.json` (`{name, description, mcp, repo, ports}`). Opening its URL then gives it a name, ports, an MCP badge and its repo automatically.

---

## 3. Feature status

This computer (2026-11-20, Josh: "visualize automatically in a Grid all the projects that are running … previews in different sections of changes and progress … indicate that these are being generated locally"; after Kris Puckett's Rounds, built on Foundation's own server, grid and Rune instead): **implemented / Review**. Settings → Places → **Connect this computer** (an admin, in person) makes the members-only grid *This computer* and starts a watch in the server (`apps/server/src/localdev.ts`, shipped inside the Tauri desktop app with the server). It reads Claude Code's transcripts (`~/.claude/projects`, the desktop app's Code tab and the CLI alike), T3 / Vision Code's thread store and git, never writing a project, and lays a section per git repository (worktrees folded in) with three `local` cards: **Preview** (the newest screenshot a session saw, archived; the dev server `.claude/launch.json` declares, knocked on), **Progress** (each session's plan steps, wrap-up, PR, state) and **Changes** (branch, ahead/behind, changed files, last commits). Cards wear the *On this computer* pill; the grid's tab and side-menu row wear the computer mark and the watch's dot; a section's header carries *Local* / *n working here* beside its council chip, and the grid's council button a computer badge. Rune draws the grid like any other (the cards are web content over its canvas). Checked: `apps/server/test/localdev.test.ts` (6), `#/design` → This computer in day and night (`docs/evidence/2026-11-20_this-computer/`), and the flow on an isolated server against this machine's real transcripts (9 projects, 27 cards, screenshots and live plans; not kept as evidence: it shows private work). Not yet checked in the packaged desktop app.

Native full OS VMs (2026-11-20, Josh): **implemented / Review; real QEMU runs on Josh's host (2026-11-21), a WildOS boot is still unverified**. A section tool, right-click item and command add a WildOS VM setup card. Archived ISO imports stream to disk; native QEMU starts behind a separate person-only press, with its own persistent qcow2 disk, embedded authenticated desktop, guest-agent command runner, bounded RAM/CPU, explicit software emulation, shutdown, confirmed power-off and archive/restore. Configuration stays in SQLite, not the writable card. WSL/Docker are not required. See `docs/wildos.md`; host helper remains `npm run wildos`. Tests use simulated transports (no real boot); typecheck, client build and token checks passed. On 2026-11-21 QEMU 11.1.0 (winget) was installed on the host; WHPX initialises. `local-vms.spec.ts` passed with real QEMU: setup screens day and night, then a blank ISO9660 test disc imported, started under software emulation and streamed SeaBIOS's real framebuffer to the card's Desktop; the guest command reported no guest agent (the disc has no OS). Evidence: `docs/evidence/2026-11-20_native-vms/`.

| Area | Feature | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **The grid behind the grid** (2026-11-26, Josh: agenttrail for the council's thinking and T3's agents at work at once) | `worktrail.ts` records each crew member's steps (section, card, ok, ms; arguments, answers and reasoning for its asker only) and a T3 thread's files; `GET /api/boards/:id/trail`; `concurrent_work`; `trail/` draws it: sections in columns along their wires, status rings, trails, handoffs, folders, run cards, a followed agent's chain | Review | §0.23. Opened by Expand on an agent's thinking or See their work in the T3 block. Checked with the stand-in T3 and the scripted council only |
| **Monorepo** | npm workspaces, `npm run dev` runs both, Tauri still builds the client | done | Client moved to `apps/client` with `git mv` (history kept). Port 1430: Vision owns 1420 |
| **Auth** | Google through flowform-server: flowform's own client, PKCE and signup allowlist. Foundation verifies the hand-off at `/auth/me` (email_verified), keeps flowform's bearer encrypted for downloading the person's Flowform files and nothing else (since 2026-11-11, Josh's call; a refused sign-in's is revoked), and mints its own session. The web path is bound to the browser that started it; the desktop path uses the system browser and the window polls. Also dev login, an optional own Google client, hashed 30-day sessions, and the service token. **Dev login is off unless `FOUNDATION_DEV_LOGIN=1`** (2026-11-18, ADR-001 G0), and even on it refuses when the allowlist is empty, when the request isn't from this computer (or comes through a proxy), and while Foundation's T3 runs here; the server's boot line and the sign-in screen say how to turn it on when nothing else is offered. **A person, not an agent** (2026-11-18, ADR-001 G1, G2, G9, G10, G12): running a job, asking the agent (`POST /api/agent`), making an agent token (now 7 days, was 30), probing an app's address and saving an app are `requirePerson`, so an agent's bearer and the service token get 403; probing is no longer exempt from the viewer write gate. **Two transports** (2026-11-08): the cookie when the page and server are one origin; otherwise (the app on another site's server) sign-in returns an `app` bearer, sent only as Authorization, and each socket opens with a one-time 30 s ticket (`POST /api/ws-ticket`, `sockets.ts`). **Only our own pages use the cookie** (2026-11-18, `origins.ts`): CORS answers exactly the client (`FOUNDATION_CLIENT_URL`), the desktop window (http://localhost:31440, tauri://localhost) and `FOUNDATION_CORS_ORIGINS`, no longer any localhost port; a change (non-GET) or a socket signed in by the cookie must carry an Origin on that list, or no Origin with `Sec-Fetch-Site` same-origin or none (a bearer or a ticket needs none); and every request must name the server in `Host` (loopback on its or the client's port, the public or client address's host, or `FOUNDATION_HOSTS`), else 421: the DNS-rebinding fence, `/auth/dev` included. **Roles need a person** (2026-11-18): changing who may edit a board is an admin in person; an admin's agent token is refused | done: `test/flowform.test.ts`, plus the desktop app signed in for real through server.formresearch.ai (`docs/evidence/2026-11-02_flowform-signin/`). Users are keyed by flowform account; an address flowform hasn't verified can create a user but never claim an existing one. The person gate and dev login: `test/person-gate.test.ts`, over HTTP against the real server (`docs/evidence/2026-11-18_person-gate/`). Tickets: `test/tickets.test.ts` on real sockets, and a browser on one origin synced live through a server on another (`docs/evidence/2026-11-08_ws-ticket/`) | Flowform admins become Foundation admins. Viewers are refused unless `FLOWFORM_ADMIT_VIEWERS=1` |
| **Sync** | Yjs board docs, y-protocols wire, update log + compaction, reconnect with backoff, presence/awareness | done | Agent edits verified streaming live into an open browser |
| | Peer cursors | done (2026-11-07) | Two browsers each see the other's named pointer (`e2e/together.spec.ts`) |
| **Grid** | Original Foundation grid (sections, tracks, springs, tables with formulas, maps, notes, media) | done | Now night-aware and on tokens. Layout memoised per state |
