| **A second renderer: Rune** (2026-11-12, Josh: "a fundamentally GPU rendered interface side by side with the Tauri app UI layer … don't write over the existing rendering engine … slowly build an even faster rendering engine for Foundation on the side") | [Rune](https://github.com/anomalyco/rune) (a Rust GPU UI framework: its own layout, text, springs and renderer) draws the board on a canvas through WebGPU, beside the web renderer and never instead of it. **Settings → Renderer** picks the engine for this device (`foundation-engine`; `?engine=rune` for one page load); the web renderer is the default and loads nothing of Rune's. `apps/rune` is its own Cargo workspace (Rune pinned at a `dev` rev with its four patched crates; toolchain 1.99.1), built to wasm by **`npm run rune:build`** into `apps/client/public/rune/` (10.7 MB, not checked in; `build:desktop` runs it first, and goes on without it where there is no Rust toolchain). **The seam:** the store stays the board's one writer and the one source of geometry. `src/rune/scene.ts` hands Rune the board already laid out by the store's own functions (the rects the wires and cursors read), with colours resolved from the tokens; Rune (`apps/rune/src/view.rs`) draws it and reports what the person did (`bridge.rs`: a press, a card let go, a note's text), which `src/rune/host.ts` turns into the store's own actions, so sync, viewers and the invariants are untouched. **One camera for both engines:** the grid's springs, wheel, pinch, throw, framing and keys are the same code; Rune gets the camera as two live values (meant to redraw without re-running its view; measured 2026-11-13, Rune runs it again on about four frames in ten of a pan: §7). The web layers that aren't Rune's yet (wires, cursors, all the chrome) draw over its canvas. Today Rune draws the paper, the grid frame and labels, sections (paper, header, the selected look), empty cells with + Section, notes, images, an agent's ✦ pill and the selection ring; cards move within their section; **a note opens for writing in Rune's own text field** (caret, selection, undo, IME and clipboard through Rune's hidden textarea), each keystroke to the board. **Web content stays web, as islands** (second slice, 2026-11-12): apps, pages, machines, huddles, Slack, connectors, files, maps and video are the web renderer's own components (`src/rune/RuneIslands.tsx`), placed over Rune's canvas inside a shell of each section (the web's own markup, nothing of the section drawn), so their styles, drag, ports and wires work unchanged; `scene.ts` `isIsland` is the one rule both sides read, so every card is drawn by exactly one engine. **A table is Rune's at rest** (header, column and row heads, ruling, and what each cell shows, from the workbook) **and the web's in hand**: pressed, the live sheet takes its place for cells, formulas, ranges and menus, and Rune draws it again, with the new values, when it's let go. **Sections and cards answer to the pointer in Rune** (third slice, 2026-11-13): a section's toolbar on hover (the web toolbar's list and groups, decided in `src/rune/tools.ts`; Rune draws line icons and says which was pressed), rename in Rune's own field, drag by the header to another cell or past an edge (the section travels with its cards and its islands, and the landing cell rings), remove behind its confirmation, *n more · Expand*, and Open as its own grid; the right-click menu is the web section's own list (`components/sectionMenu.ts`, now shared), opened where Rune says the right-click was; a note has its bar (title, float or dock, remove) and its resize corner, an image its remove button and corner. **The paper, the face and markdown** (fourth slice, 2026-11-13): Rune's web canvas can't be see-through (its web host never asks WebGPU for an alpha canvas), so `apps/rune/src/paper.rs` draws the web paper's own shader inside Rune's frame, through its compositor hook: the base and its diagonal, the grain stepping twice a second, the dot grid that moves with the camera, the light that follows the pointer, the vignette and the night fade, and a section's own dots (28 px, frost at 13 %). **Hanken Grotesk** is handed to Rune as TrueType (`apps/rune/fonts`, unpacked from the web bundle's WOFF2 by `scripts/rune-fonts.py` and checked in; Latin and Latin Extended, upright and italic), fetched beside the wasm after the first frame. **A note's markdown is rendered** by Rune's own kit, one top-level block at a time so the blocks take the web note's margins: headings, strong and emphasis, inline code, code blocks, lists, quotes, rules and links (a press says where it goes, and the client opens http(s) ones as the web note does). A card's paper is the raised one, as the web's is (Rune had a note on the darker paper since the first slice). The board says it's Rune's: a glass pill in the bottom bar (**Rune**; **Rune off** in amber, with why, when it was asked for and couldn't run, and the web renderer draws instead). **The parity ledger** (`src/rune/parity.ts`, shown in Settings → Renderer) lists every part of the board as *Same*, *Partly* (with the difference), *Web layer* or *Not yet*: of 43 rows, 10 the same, 20 partly, 9 the web layer, 4 not yet | partial: a first slice, off by default | `e2e/rune.spec.ts` (9, headed on this machine's GPU in Edge, the desktop app's engine: `PERF_GPU=1 E2E_CLIENT_PORT=15450 npm run e2e -w @foundation/client -- rune`): the default loads nothing of Rune's; Rune draws the company board with no GPU errors, its sections where the web renderer's are (`docs/evidence/2026-11-12_rune/board-{web,rune}-{day,night}.png`); a press selects a section, the wheel pans and Ctrl+wheel zooms, a note dragged 90 × 60 px floats there (within 3 px), and text typed in Rune's field reaches the store; without WebGPU the board falls back and says so; Settings switches engines both ways; every panel on the seeded board is a live island, each section's shell within 1 px of the rect the store gives, and Rune is handed none of them; a table is drawn by Rune at rest, becomes the web's sheet when pressed, takes `=B3+1` in a cell, and goes back to Rune showing 25 (`islands-rune-night.png`, `table-rune-at-rest.png`, `table-web-in-hand.png`). | **Files** | Upload anything with progress; images and video show instantly, then swap to the shared URL; range streaming; archive-never-delete. **A file can't run as the app** (2026-11-18): every file answer carries `nosniff` and a CSP `sandbox`; a file shows in place only if its type is passive (png, jpeg, gif, webp, avif, mp4, webm, mp3/m4a/wav/ogg, pdf, plain text, 3D models); anything else (HTML, SVG, XML, scripts, stylesheets, unknown) is a download of opaque bytes, and an active or unknown type is stored as `application/octet-stream` at every door into the archive (the name is kept). SVGs therefore no longer show as pictures. **A file answers to its board** (2026-11-18): the board tools and routes that take a file id (`read_file`, `find_images`, `lay_out`, `collection_put`, `stage_attach`, asks, huddle recordings, loading an engine image) refuse a file on a members-only board the caller isn't on | done | `test/hardening.test.ts` (an agent's HTML upload is an attachment with the sandbox; a png stays inline; ranges; a member of one board can't reach another's files through each door). The PDF frame keeps no `sandbox` attribute: Chromium's PDF viewer renders under the CSP header but shows nothing in a sandboxed frame (checked in the browser pane, 2026-11-18) |

A link shows its real host beside it and opens in a new tab with no opener or referrer; only http(s) becomes a link. Every frame showing an address from a card, an app or a person's typing (app cards, web page spaces, the built-in browser, T3) follows one rule (`panels/frameSandbox.ts`): never Foundation's own client or server (any loopback name on their ports), always sandboxed, its own origin (and camera, microphone, clipboard reading, a favicon) only for a saved app or the T3 address; the app bridge names the frame's origin and hears only that frame. Allow / Decline on the card: `POST /api/boards/:id/apps/:panel/asks/:ask/allow|decline`, `requirePerson` and can-edit. Allow records the leave and calls nothing (the agent calls again); a changed definition asks again ("changed"); Decline isn't asked again in that run. `app_tools` says per tool: allowed, not yet allowed, changed since allowed. | **Security** | **The audit log** (2026-11-18, `audit.ts`): one append-only table of who did what, for whom, through which door, where the thing acted on runs, what was decided and who approved it. Arguments are kept only as a hash and a size; no values, results, message text, file contents, tokens or emails. Triggers refuse UPDATE and DELETE; each row carries the one before's hash, and `verifyAudit()` finds the first broken link. Recorded: every board tool call from every door, every person-gate refusal, Allow and Decline of an agent's ask, desktop leave, runs, jobs and T3 threads started and stopped, agent tokens, saved apps, roles and memberships, allowances, dev sign-in. `GET /api/audit` is for an admin, in person | done | `test/audit.test.ts` (4), `test/egress-doors.test.ts`. An agent's send to an external service goes only where a person allowed that kind of send to that service from that board; otherwise nothing is sent, the agent gets a sentence and the log an `asked` row. A person's own call is its own approval. The Slack, GitHub and Flowform clients check the same call before anything leaves, and an agent's request to the read routes that reach them is held the same way. A send that names or copies something from a members-only board the person can't see, or (a message) from another members-only board, is refused whole. | **Desktop updates** (2026-11-08, Josh: "an auto-update feature similar to what we have in Vision") | The desktop app updates itself the way Vision does (its ADR-055): `tauri-plugin-updater` reads `latest.json` on the newest GitHub release (`releases/latest/download/latest.json`; the repo is public), checks each installer's minisign signature against the public key in `tauri.conf.json`, and runs it (passive NSIS on Windows; `tauri-plugin-process` relaunches on macOS and Linux). An installed build checks 8 s after launch and every 6 h while open; `tauri dev` and the browser never do. **Nothing downloads until a person presses Update and restart.** The card (glass, top right; over the sign-in page too, in day tokens there) offers Update and restart · Later (until next launch) · Skip this version; shows MB and a bar while downloading; says what changed once after the restart. Settings → Updates: the running version and Check for updates. Rules are pure (`apps/client/src/update/policy.ts`). **Releasing (2026-11-13): by itself.** Every push to `main` (not one that only touches the plan, evidence or prose) runs `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml` on a Windows runner and a macOS (Apple silicon) runner side by side: `npm ci`, Rune's wasm, `npm test` and typecheck (on Windows only; a failure there cancels the Mac build), then `npm run release -- ci build <run>` on each, which stamps `<major>.<minor>.<patch + run number>` (0.3.0 → 0.3.1, 0.3.2 …; bump `tauri.conf.json` to 0.4.0 and CI goes on 0.4.<run>), writes `docs/blog/<version>.md` from the commit subjects since the last release unless a person wrote one (plan syncs left out; the build carries it, nothing is committed), builds and signs with the repo's secrets; then a third job, `ci publish <run>`, puts both builds' files in one release with one `latest.json` naming every platform (`mergeFeeds`; it refuses builds of different versions), as a draft made live only once every file is up, so if either build fails nothing is published. A Mac build is offered only to its own architecture (`darwin-aarch64`; Intel Macs get no build and no update). **macOS is ad-hoc signed, not with an Apple Developer ID, and not notarized**: the updater installs it without a word, but the first install from the downloaded `.dmg` is stopped by Gatekeeper (System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway). A re-run of a published run does nothing. By hand still works: `npm run release -- bump <v>`, `build`, push, `publish`. **The app icon** is new: an F laid in masonry (`apps/client/assets/`) (replaced 2026-11-10 by Josh's four panels on a grid: `foundation-mark.svg` is the white drawing; the desktop icons and the browser tab's are the white mark on a glass squircle (Josh: "just make it white for the desktop … a glass morphism squircle back"; a still icon can't blur what's behind it, so the glass is drawn: translucent navy, a sheen, a lit rim), the in-app mark is blue on paper and white on fills; `#/design` → The mark, `docs/evidence/2026-11-10_icon/`; the installed app shows it from the next release build) | done on Windows | The whole loop on a real install (`docs/evidence/2026-11-08_desktop-updates/`, `update-check.mjs`): a check app at 0.2.90 offered 0.2.91 from a local feed 8 s after opening, a press downloaded and verified it, NSIS installed it passively and the app came back as 0.2.91 saying what changed, in 16 s. It found three faults, fixed: a second build could load another app's page on the other address family; the card was under the sign-in page; night glass over the day sign-in page. `test/update.test.ts` (7), `test/release.test.ts` (1), `#/design` → Desktop updates day and night. **Not yet:** Linux; Intel Macs; a Developer ID and notarization for macOS; the MSI path. **macOS (2026-11-20):** `ci build` was run on this Mac (Apple silicon) with a throwaway key: a signed `Foundation_<v>_aarch64.app.tar.gz`, a `.dmg`, a feed of `darwin-aarch64` only; `codesign --verify --deep --strict` passes on the ad-hoc bundle, and its bundled Node starts the server (`/api/health`). A machine card's glyph is ▣ (it was ▤, the note's). On the card: pick a distro (Kiri's seven), an image the engine has, or a saved image from the archive (**Load**, `docker load`); **Start** (a person) pulls with progress and runs it confined (no host mounts, not privileged, ports on 127.0.0.1, 2 GB / 2 CPUs / 1024 processes by default). Running: a terminal (xterm.js) shared by everyone on the board, editors type and viewers watch, the screen replayed to late joiners, reconnecting by itself; ports as pills that open the port as a page in a section beside it. Stop keeps the disk; Remove asks twice and sends `confirm`. The card mirrors the engine (a machine removed outside Foundation shows Not started; one whose engine has stopped answering shows **Out of reach**, its disk kept). Containers are Kiri's `machine-<name>` with `dev.kiri.machine`, so `kiri machine shell <name>` reaches them. **Kiri is part of the build** (2026-11-07): `npm run kiri:install` (`scripts/kiri.mjs`) downloads the pinned Kiri release (0.1.18; **0.2.1 from 2026-11-17**), checks its SHA-256 against the release's `SHA256SUMS` (0.2.1 ships none: GitHub's own digest of each asset), and on Windows installs it per user (no administrator) with Kiri's own Linux imported into WSL 2 as `kiri`, then starts it. Elsewhere it checks the download and prints the one install command. `npm run dev` / `npm run desktop` start Kiri if it's installed. In the browser on Kiri: Start from asleep woke Kiri and ran alpine in about 1.5 s. Inside, `Alpine Linux v3.22 on …-microsoft-standard-WSL2`, a port served from the machine answered on 127.0.0.1 and was refused on the Wi-Fi and WSL addresses, and the cgroup held the caps. With Kiri quit, the card said Out of reach; Start brought back the same container, and Remove (twice) woke Kiri and left it empty (`docs/evidence/2026-11-07_kiri/`, and `…_machines/` for Docker and two people). **0.2.1** (2026-11-17, Josh's yes; it fixes three differences HomeForm met on 0.1.18: classic `docker save` archives, a kept-alive client after `/images/load`, and Testcontainers' engine check): an upgrade stops Kiri and its distro before the MSI; the script writes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Kiri\setup.json` as Kiri's setup window does (the headless install never had, and 0.2.x reads the distro from it); and when the engine in WSL is older than the pin it copies the app's `kiri` and `kiri-init` in itself, because 0.2.1's own copy hands its script to `wsl.exe --`, whose shell expands it to nothing (fixed on Kiri's main with `wsl.exe -e`). Kiri 0.2.x answers `Version` with a Docker version (27.5.1) and its own as the `Kiri` component, which the engine status now shows. `npm run machines:smoke` on Kiri 0.2.1: start 1.8 s, the shared terminal, stop and start again in 177 ms with the disk kept, remove (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_kiri-0.2.1/`). 0.2.1 has no full `.deb` (only kiri-lite's), so the Linux .deb path says so. The macOS and Linux paths of `kiri.mjs` haven't been run **Agents' commands** (2026-11-08, `machineasks.ts`): `machine_run` asks to run one command in a running machine; the ask waits on the card in violet with Run and Decline, and only a person who can edit the board answers it, from their browser (a bearer token or a viewer gets 403). Run executes it through the Engine API without a terminal, under `timeout`, and the agent's call gets the output and exit code; declined, unanswered (120 s) or withdrawn, nothing runs and the agent is told so. The ask and its outcome (who ran it, the exit code; who declined it) land in activity. `machines.test.ts` +2 on the stand-in engine; on Docker Desktop with three browsers, the bearer's and the viewer's Run were refused, Ada's Run returned exit 0 and the os-release, Kai's Decline returned declined (`docs/evidence/2026-11-08_crew-next/` 10–16) |

| **Machines: typing, the desktop, agents using it, pipes** (2026-11-15, Josh: "I can't actually type into the VM when focused on the object … a very clear option to see the VM's desktop … the agent council to clearly manipulate the VM and have computer use over it … piping information into a VM and also being clear about its outputs that I can pipe into another application") | **Typing.** A plain click in a running machine's terminal puts the caret there at any zoom (it only worked at exactly 100 %: `scaledMouse.ts`); the card goes in use, wears the 2 px ring and says *Keys go to this machine · Esc Esc to leave*; every key is then the shell's (`lib/keysHeld.ts`: the shell's key handler and the talk sheet stand down), so Ctrl+C, W, U, L and K do what a shell does. One Esc is the shell's; Esc twice, or a click elsewhere, leaves. Ctrl+C works on Kiri where the image has perl or python3 (see §7). **The desktop.** The head carries `Terminal · Desktop`. Desktop shows the machine's graphical desktop: Xvfb (1280×800), the XFCE session and x11vnc installed *inside* the machine by **Add a desktop** (a person's press: `requirePerson`; apt on Ubuntu and Debian, apk on Alpine; the package manager's own progress on the card), or with *With a desktop* at Start. Nothing is published: VNC listens on the machine's own loopback and Foundation reaches it as it reaches the shell, through the engine (an exec of `socat`, relayed over `/machines/:board/:panel/vnc`), where noVNC 1.4.0 draws it in a same-origin frame (`desktop.html`), so the board's zoom scales it like a picture. It works on a machine that is already running and for someone on another computer. A plain click uses it (the ring, *Keys go to this desktop*), Esc Esc or a click elsewhere leaves, and the card's full screen is its full screen. Viewers get a second, view-only VNC and can only watch. **Agents.** `desktop_screenshot`, `desktop_mouse`, `desktop_type`, `desktop_key` (xdotool and scrot inside the machine). They work only where a person pressed **Let agents use this desktop**: a leave on that machine with who and when, off by default, taken back with Stop by anyone who can edit, cleared by Remove; a viewer can't give it, a bearer can't, and a person whose `computer_use` preference is `never` can't (`prefs.ts`: `person_prefs`, `GET/POST /api/prefs`, `computerUseDefault(userId)`). Without it the tool does nothing, says why, and the card offers the person the button (*✦ Name asks to use this desktop*). The screen goes to the model as a picture (MCP image content; an image block for the API runtime). While an agent drives, the card says *✦ Name is using this desktop · what it just did* with its last actions, a crew member's cursor is on the card, and every action is in the activity under the agent's name. **Pipes.** Every machine card has ports `in` and `out`. What is wired to `in` is written to `/foundation/in` inside the machine when it changes (text as text, anything else as a line of JSON); what the machine writes to `/foundation/out` is read back (parsed as JSON when it parses) and stored on the card's `out` by the server, so wires and apps receive it. Besides an app's port, a note (its text) or a table (its rows, or one cell) can be at the other end; `out` wired to a note or table is written there. The card's strip says *In ← source · value · when · /foundation/in* and *Out → where · value · when · /foundation/out*, with a one-line hint and a picker to wire either end. **Fixed 2026-11-17** (Josh: "Add a desktop doesn't work in Kiri … the selection around the window is still weird"). It didn't work because the server he ran had been started two hours before the desktop was merged and never restarted: the route answered 404 and the app showed a toast reading *not found*. A press the server refuses is now said in the well, with Try again; a server from before desktops is named as that, with how to restart it. No network, no recipe (by the distro's name), not root and an interrupted install each say so; an install that says nothing for a minute says for how long, and **Stop** ends it in the machine and puts the card back to *no desktop yet*. A running machine no longer wears an agent's ring (violet, outside its card and its section); the in-use ring is the terminal well's, or round the desktop's screen, 2 px on the screen at any zoom, and the only ring while it shows. Checked on Kiri 0.1.18 with Ubuntu, Debian and Alpine (smoke), the four distros without a recipe (refused in words), a stalled and an unreachable mirror in a real machine (`install-failures.mts`), in the browser by day and night (`e2e/machines-fixes.spec.ts`), and in the packaged app's transport (the production build on another origin, bearer, tickets). `docs/evidence/2026-11-17_machines-fixes/` | done on Kiri; a note → `tr a-z A-Z </foundation/in >/foundation/out` → a note, live as the note changes, JSON out as JSON; Add a desktop to a running machine, the 1280×800 screen in the card, typing into a terminal on the desktop from the board; The switch now recognises a Foundation server started its own way (`node --import tsx apps/server/src/index.ts`, or the built-in `server/node … server/index.mjs`, by the listener's command line: `netstat -ano` and the process's command line; `npm run dev` and anything else is never touched) and offers **Restart** and **Stop** for it (`local_server.rs` `foundation_kind`, `stop_adopted`); Restart stops it and starts the same kind from the folder. Where a machine's Add a desktop finds the server older than the app, the desktop app's well offers **Restart the server** beside Try again. **The tools row** (`MachineTools.tsx`): the same row under the terminal and the desktop: Copy (the terminal's selection; what the machine copied), Copy all (the terminal's scrollback), Paste (into the shell; on the desktop typed where the machine's cursor is), and ⧉ a window of its own, with what a press did said beside them. The terminal's keys (`machineTerminal.ts`): Ctrl+C copies when text is selected and is the interrupt when not; Ctrl+Shift+C copies; Ctrl+V and Ctrl+Shift+V paste. The desktop's (`desktopFrame.ts`): what the machine copies (x11vnc's cut text) goes to this computer's clipboard while the desktop has the keys; Ctrl+V hands the machine this computer's clipboard, then the key. A card's error and an install's line are selectable. **Ctrl+scroll** over a machine is the grid's zoom even while the machine has the keys (`GridViewport.tsx`), and over the desktop too (its frame hands ⌘/Ctrl-wheel to the card, which zooms about the same point); ### 3.1 flowform → Foundation port map

| flowform | Foundation | |
|---|---|---|
| Google OAuth + allowlist, hashed bearers, 30-day sessions | `auth.ts`, `flowform.ts` | federated: the same Google login on the same server (`/auth/google/start?cb=<loopback>`). Cookie + Bearer; Foundation's tokens never go in URLs |
| Yjs rooms, durable log, compaction, presence | `boards.ts`, standard y-protocols wire (not the custom bincode one) | ported |
| Server-side agent writes, `request_id` idempotency, confirm-token deletes, visible agent presence | `tools.ts` | ported |
| MCP endpoint over the canvas | `mcp.ts` (19 tools) | ported, extended |
| Media archive-never-delete; content-addressed bytes | `files.ts` | ported (local disk; S3 later) |
| File search (BM25 FTS) | `search.ts` | ported (lexical). Semantic (e5/SigLIP + RRF k=60) deferred |
| Slack search | `search.messages` with a user token | simplified. flowform's per-user OAuth + mirrored visibility is the target (§8) |
| Messaging (DMs, channels, threads) | `chat.ts`, `chatsock.ts`: ChatForm (§0.11) | done 2026-11-18: one model for channels, DMs, agents and bridged conversations |
| Code tab (T3 gateway, Codex/Claude Code) | Session tabs (subscription runtime, goals + steps); Vision Code threads read-only in the side pane | partial |
| LOD, media residency, viewport culling, iframe snapshots | zoom-gated frames, IO-mounted panels, idle shader, memoised layout | partial (§5) |
| Gantt / Timeline & tasks | — | **dropped** (as asked) |
| Digital twin (factory sim, Kapaʻa 3D, mechanical/electrical benches, deployments, demo scenes) | — | **dropped** (as asked). FactoryForm owns the twin |
| Rooms, room_members, room media (S3) | `flowform-import.ts`, `members.ts`, `files.ts` | imported from Flowform's data backup, re-runnable (`docs/flowform-import.md`). Groups, card arrows, dataflow wires, directives, floor plans, camera feeds and board jumps are counted, not carried |
| Admin (users, audit, crashes, maintenance) | — | deferred |

## 4. The board tool surface

One definition (`apps/server/src/tools.ts`), three doors: the built-in agent, `/mcp`, and `POST /api/boards/:id/tools/:name`.

| Category | Tools |
|---|---|
| Reading | `read_board`, `search`, `company`, `read_file`, `read_repo`, `find_repo`, `app_tools`, `places` (approved folders on this computer: list, look in one, read the start of a text file), `flowform` (the person's Flowform through its own MCP tools, read-only; needs their link) |
| Organizing | `add_section`, `rename_section`, `add_note`, `add_table` (with data), `update_card`, `arrange_section` (a section's flow order), `delete_card` (confirm), `send_to_board` (a card to another board, as a person dropping it on that space's tab does; its search entry now survives the move: the index only drops a board's own entries), `extract_section` (a section becomes a grid of its own inside this one; 2026-11-17, below), `focus`, `set_plan` (the goal and steps a session tab shows; writes nothing) |
| The crew | `point_at` (aims the cursor at a table cell or a panel's port, a laser on the spot; writes nothing), `bring_view` (brings the person who asked to a section or card: their view glides there, or they're asked first if busy; read-only), `call_in_help` (a helper agent for an independent part of the ask: its own name, cursor, run and session; two per ask, none from a helper, six agents per board), `crew_work` (read: the ask, the lead's plan, and what each agent on it is doing and has made, so helpers don't duplicate). Every Foundation run on a board is a crew member (the Shiba on Today isn't: it has no board, so no cursor): its tool calls carry its id (`ToolCtx.crew`, MCP `&crew=`), aim its cursor first and sign its writes |
| Note pages (2026-11-26, docs/notes.md) | `add_note_page` (a note of its own in Notes, inside a page, optionally put on this grid), `place_note` (a page on this grid as a card: the same note), `make_note_page` (a grid's note becomes a page; the card shows it), `archive_note_page` (on the page's own board; confirm; nothing deleted). A page's words are written with `update_card` on its own board (id `body`) |
| Characters | `add_character` (a card for an avatar the council's agents can wear: name, set, form and who it is; drawing, modelling and animating it spend credits, so those are a person's presses on the card) |
| Files from this computer | `bring_file` (one file from an approved place, copied into the archive and put on the board as an image or file card; the original is only read) |
| Apps | `add_panel` (app, link, machine, connector, agent), `connect`, `send_to_port`, `call_app_tool` (a file it returns is kept in the grid's files), `add_cad` and `refresh_cad` (3D cards fed by an app, docs/cad-feed.md) |
| People and studios | `ask_person` (Hermes), `vision_brief`, `reverse_engineer` (REA, person-run) |
| Machines | `propose_machine` (a card waiting for a person's Start), `machines` (read: engines, state, ports as URLs, the last screen of each shared terminal). `machine_run` (asks to run one command in a running machine and waits: it runs only when a person with edit rights presses Run on the card, and returns the output and exit code, or that it was declined or went unanswered). Start, stop, restart, remove, load and Run are a person's, from the card |
| ChatForm (2026-11-18) | `replay_timeline` (a recorded session as words: who said what, what changed on the grid), `add_replay`, `place_file`, `add_chat_card` (2026-11-25: a ChatForm conversation on the grid as a card that refers to it; only someone who can see the conversation can place it); `chat_channels`, `chat_read`, `chat_search`, `chat_post`, `chat_react`: as the person the agent works for, authored as the agent. A post into a bridged portal is a draft |
| Huddles and Slack | `open_huddle`, `huddle_transcript` (read), `add_slack_channel`, `slack_read`. Agents read Slack; posting stays with people on the card |
| Today (personal) | `today` (read), `set_goal`, `set_profile`, `point_today` (lights items on the person's Today; writes nothing). Goals and profiles are the caller's own, not a board's, so viewers may use them |
| Digest | `write_digest` (writes or updates today's "What changed" note in the board's "Daily digest" section from its activity and the company connectors, with no model; the server also writes one each morning on the company board) |
| Design pipelines | `start_design_pipeline`, `pipeline_status`, `stage_attach`, `stage_choose`, `stage_check`, `stage_settings`, `stage_request` (hand-off), `complete_stage` (Review: approved / changes) |

**Invariants**, from flowform and tested in `apps/server/test`:
- Every write names its board.
- `request_id` makes a write idempotent.
- Deletes need `confirm: true`.
- Every write lands in `activity`, under who really did it: a person (their browser, or their own grid edits, coalesced) or the named agent and the person it worked for; an agent's write moves its presence ring.
- Nothing that spends money or runs third-party code starts without a person.
- A viewer can't change a board: not through its sync socket, a tool (HTTP, MCP, the agent) or any other write (`roles.test.ts`).
- Nothing on this computer outside an approved place is read, and only an admin, as a person, approves one (`places.test.ts`).
- Another service's tokens that have to be replayed (a person's Flowform link, and their Flowform session for file downloads) are encrypted at rest, never plain in the database, a URL or a response; the link asks for reading scopes only, and the session is used for one download route only (`flowformlink.test.ts`, `flowformfiles.test.ts`, `flowform.test.ts`).

---

**Machines' desktop and pipes (2026-11-15).** `desktop_screenshot` (the screen as a picture), `desktop_mouse` (move, click, double_click, right_click, middle_click, drag, scroll_up, scroll_down at a pixel), `desktop_type` (text; a newline is Enter, a tab is Tab), `desktop_key` (keys and chords, e.g. `ctrl+c`, `alt+F4`): only on a machine where a person pressed Let agents use this desktop. `machines` now also says whether a machine has a desktop, whether agents may use it, and its pipes (what feeds `in`, where `out` goes, the last value of each). `connect` wires a machine's `in`/`out` to an app's port, a note (`text`) or a table (`rows`, or a cell such as `B2`).

**Collections (2026-11-17).** `add_character_pipeline` `{section, set}` (write): the studio's flow into a section with a collection after Output; `read_collection` `{collection}` (read): its items and where it lays out; `collection_lay_out` `{collection, section | null}`: pipe it into a section (null stops; laid cards stay); `collection_copy` `{collection, item, section, x?, y?}`: one item out as a card, floating at x, y or docked; `collection_put` `{collection, file_id? | character?}`: by hand; `collection_take_out` `{collection, item, confirm}`: cards made from it stay. All but the read take `request_id`.

**Sub-grids (2026-11-17).** `extract_section` `{section, request_id?}` (write): make a section a grid of its own inside this one; returns `{board, name, section, card, moved, sections, stayed[], wires{moved,dropped}, already?}`. Also `POST /api/boards/:id/sections/:section/extract`. `GET /api/boards/:id/outline` returns the grid's tracks, sections' spans and cards for a preview, and its `parent`. `dissolve_grid` `{section, confirm, request_id?}` (write, 2026-11-27): the undo, a grid inside this one put back into the section that stands for it (`section` may also be the grid's card id); returns `{board, name, section, moved, sections, wires}`; the grid is archived. Also `POST /api/boards/:id/sections/:section/dissolve` `{confirm: true}`.

**Reading a card, and code mode (2026-11-24).** `read_card` `{id}` (read): one card in full (a note's whole markdown, a table's cells); `read_board` shows 240 characters of each. `search_tools` `{query, limit?}` (read): the matching tools as TypeScript signatures. `run_code` `{code, request_id?}`: JavaScript in a QuickJS isolate in a worker thread, with `foundation.<tool>(args)` for every tool; each inner call is `runTool` with the caller's own context, so a script can do nothing its caller couldn't do call by call (a viewer reads, `confirm` is still needed, a person's actions stay a person's). 20 s, 32 MB, 40 calls, two scripts a person and four in all. `/mcp?…&mode=code` lists seven tools instead of 82. `docs/agent-harness.md` §3.

## 5. Performance budget

| Surface | Budget | How |
|---|---|---|
| Pan, zoom, drag | 60 fps, no React render on the hot path | Springs write `transform` directly (`physics/spring.ts`). Sync diffs by object identity, so a drag touches one key |
| Grid paper | ≤ 2 draws/s while still | `gl/background.ts` draws only when a uniform changes or the 2 Hz grain ticks (was 60/s) |
| Overlays (wires, rings) | Read-only per frame | `sectionLayout` memoised per state snapshot. DOM written only when a path changes |
| React renders | A gesture renders only the cards it changes | Sections and cards read their own slice (`useSection`); the grid, both wire layers, maps and presentation mode read slices too (`state/slices.ts`, 2026-11-08). Held by `e2e/renders.spec.ts` |
| Live frames | ≤ the frames on screen | IntersectionObserver plus a drawn-scale gate: zoom × the section's fit ≥ 0.3 (`store.drawnScale`, 2026-11-08; was zoom alone); posters otherwise; pointer events only while in use (⌘/Ctrl-click or full screen, 2026-11-11). A page starts only once the camera is calm, one at a time (`lib/calm.ts`, 2026-11-11). A page that has loaded stays mounted off screen (§7) |
| Pictures | Decoded pixels ≈ what's drawn | Levels at 256–2048 px, made once per device in a worker and cached (`lib/imageLod.ts`, 2026-11-11); the original only when the drawn width passes the largest level |
| Videos | Decoders only near the screen | Mounted once the camera is calm within a screen of the viewport, released 5 s after leaving it, `preload="metadata"` (2026-11-11) |
| Connectors | Poll only while visible | 20 s while on screen; server cache 20 s |
| Search | Local < 20 ms; remote ≤ 2.5 s | FTS5 with prefix indexes; remote sources raced against a timeout |
| ASCII wind | 60 fps (every display frame); < 5 ms/frame | WebGL2, two draws a frame on every display frame (2026-11-07; was 25 fps on a timer). Backs off to 30, then 15 fps, then suspends only when *its own* work is behind (the GPU hasn't finished a frame three frames on, or draws cost the main thread > 4 ms), and steps back up after 180 frames of keeping up (6 s at 30 fps); the page's own busy frames no longer count against it. Page hooks draw on the next frame, once. Paused when hidden or unfocused; settles after 3 min until touched |
| Every surface, on a real GPU | p95 frame ≤ 16.7 ms × 1.25, ≤ 5 % of frames over | `e2e/frames.spec.ts` (`PERF_GPU=1`): headed Edge (WebView2's engine) on the machine's GPU at 2×, page frames (rAF), Long Animation Frames, and the wind's own draws. The board's first load behind the sign-in is reported, not held to it (§7) |
| Character cards | A picture at rest; live only in use or drawn large | A card plays under the pointer (and 1.4 s after) or at ≥ 80 % of its size, once the camera is calm (`characters/stagePolicy.ts`, 2026-11-25); otherwise its kept still: a 3D model drawn once per clips file (`portrait.ts`), a mesh avatar's cut-out portrait. A mesh avatar that stops is kept ready (3 at most), so only its first wake pays the runtime's loader (170–555 ms, §7). Measured: panning over the studio's characters 47 → 60 fps, script at rest 1,409 → 381 ms per 2 s (§0.18) |
| The Mac's engine (WebKit) | 30–60 fps on the Character studio | Measured 2026-11-24 with Playwright's WebKit at 2× on a copy of this Mac's data: pan 60, zoom 44–46, pan at 12 % 40 (§0.16; `docs/evidence/2026-11-24_mac-studio-perf/`). A wire's pulse and tunnel mask rest while the board is far or moving; the image and byte caches open once per page. Not yet a spec (plan: *webkit-frames*) |

**Measured** (2026-11-03, `e2e/perf.spec.ts`; 180 notes + 20 live frames, headless Chromium with software WebGL, so upper bounds; `docs/evidence/2026-11-03_perf/`):

| Action | p50 | p95 | Longest | Frames > 33 ms | Long tasks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Pan | 16.7 ms | 16.8 ms | 33.4 ms | 2 of 712 | 1 |
| Pinch zoom | 16.7 ms | 33.4 ms | 33.5 ms | 30 of 664 | 0 |
| Drag a card | 16.7 ms | 16.8 ms | 99.9 ms | 3 of 294 | 2 |
| Pan with 8 live frames running | 16.7 ms | 33.4 ms | 50.1 ms | 38 of 678 | 0 |

Pan and drag hold 60 fps. Live frames and zoom drop about 5 % of frames to 30 fps. ~~Picking up a docked card has one ~100 ms frame~~: that frame was React re-rendering every section and card on each press, not the relayout. Sections and apps now subscribe to their own slice of the store (`useSection`, links-only `usePortLayout`), so a press renders 1–2 cards instead of 38. Measured on a slower cloud box, the drag's longest frame fell from 250 ms to 67 ms (`docs/evidence/2026-11-07_perf-renders/`). Re-run `perf.spec.ts` on the Mac to refresh this table. Next: measure on a real GPU and trackpad, then decide between pausing frames while the camera moves (a still in their place) and leaving them live.

**Renders per gesture** (2026-11-08, `e2e/renders.spec.ts`; dev server, StrictMode counts double; `docs/evidence/2026-11-08_narrow-subscribers/`). The grid, both wire layers, maps and presentation mode used to read the whole store. Typing 30 characters rendered each of them 60 times. A card drag rendered the agent layer about once per pointer move (124–128): your own cursor going out to awareness notified every presence subscriber. Now those gestures, and 10 remote edits elsewhere, render none of them: only the card and its section. On Josh's Windows machine under other sessions' load, `perf.spec`'s frame times were within noise before and after (p50 16.7 ms both; the longest frames were machine stalls on both sides), so the frame-time gain is unmeasured until a real-GPU run.

**Measured on a heavy board** (2026-11-11, `PERF_GPU=1 npm run e2e -w @foundation/client -- heavy`; Windows 11, RTX 5080, Edge 157 headed, 1440×900 at 2×, dev server; the geometry of the Flowform room Josh named: one 15,732 × 5,239 plane, 27 photos (up to 4096 px), 4 videos, 15 live apps, 10 notes, all floating; `docs/evidence/2026-11-11_heavy-board/`). Share of frames over 20.8 ms:

| Action | Before (main) | Picture levels only | Levels + calm mounting (now) | Calm mounting, originals (`?nolod`) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fitted (12 %): the board loading, 6 s | 1.7 % | 2.0 % | 2.3 % | 1.7 % |
| Fitted: pan / pinch | 0 / 4.3 % | 0 / 4.5 % | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| Photos at ~35 %: pan / pinch | **21.1 %** / 5.8 % | 0.2 % / 5.3 % | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| Photos at 100 %: pan / pinch | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| Glide: photos → fitted → apps (framing keys) | 5.9 % | 36.8 %, then 15.7 % (videos started mid-glide) | 0–0.6 % (three glides, two runs) | 1.2 %, 0, 0 |
| Apps framed: pan / pinch | 2.0 % / 0 | 1.9 % / 0 | 0 / 0–0.6 % | 0 / 0 |
| Pictures decoded (Mpx), settled, at 35 % / 100 % / apps / fitted | 99.4 everywhere | — | 6.2 / 45.6 / 9.6 / 7.6 (at 100 % the 1417 px cards need their 4096 px originals) | 99.4 everywhere |
| Pictures drawn from fewer pixels than they cover, settled | 0 | — | 0 (only two originals smaller than their cards); in the 1.5 s after a glide stops, the level before shows until the next has decoded | 0 |
| Live pages running at the apps' framing | 11 | 11 | 2 | 0–1 |

What it was: on this GPU the frames lost while panning over photos (21 %) and gliding came from **work started mid-gesture**: live pages mounting as cards crossed the 0.3 drawn-scale gate (a whole document each), videos starting their decoders, and full-size photos decoding and uploading as they came into view. Calm mounting removes the first two; levels remove the third and cut decoded pictures from 99 Mpx (about 400 MB of textures) to under 10. With calm mounting alone, originals also hold 60 fps on an RTX 5080, so on this machine the levels' gain is memory and bandwidth: a revisit loads a few kilobytes per picture instead of 110 MB. A weaker GPU (the integrated Radeon here, a MacBook Air) is where that should show in frames; unmeasured. The first visit still downloads every original once to make its levels (§7).

**Measured on a real GPU** (2026-11-07, `PERF_GPU=1 npm run e2e -w @foundation/client -- frames`; Windows 11, RTX 5080, 60 Hz, Edge 157 headed, 2× pixels; the same 200-card board; `docs/evidence/2026-11-07_frames/`). The sign-in was never measured before: headless Chromium is SwiftShader, and the wind refuses software renderers.

| Surface | Before (dev server) | After, dev server | After, production build, 1440×900 | After, production build, 2560×1400 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sign-in wind at rest / stirred / pressed / typed into | **10 / 10 / 10 / 1 fps** (page 60) | 60 / 60 / 60 / 60 fps | 60 / 60 / 60 / 60 fps | 60 / 60 / 60 / 60 fps |
| Sign-in dissolving into the board | p95 66.7 ms, 9 % of frames over (with the board's load; measured apart after) | p95 16.7 ms, 0 over | p95 16.8 ms, 0 over | p95 16.7 ms, 0 over |
| Side pane, search, ⌘K open and close | p95 49.9 ms, **28 % over**, one 388 ms task | p95 16.8 ms, 2.6 % over | p95 16.7 ms, 0 over | p95 16.7 ms, 0.8 % over |
| Pan / pinch zoom | 0.1 % / 0.3 % over | 0.1 % / 0.3 % over | 0 / 0 over | 0 / 0 over |
| Drag a card | 0.6 % over, longest 133 ms | 1.2 % over, longest 815 ms (once, in the test page's iframe) | 0 over, longest 16.8 ms | 0 over, longest 19.1 ms |
| Pan with 20 live frames | 0 over | 0.5 % over | 0 over | 0 over |
| Framing glides (0, ⇧1, F) | 2.7 % over | 1.8 % over | 0 over | 1.9 % over, longest 33 ms |
| First wind frame after navigation | — | 1.5–2.4 s (Vite's dev modules) | 0.9–1.1 s | 1.3 s |

What it was: **(1)** the wind drew at 25 fps on a timer, and a burst of page work at boot (React mounting, shaders linking) dropped it to a 12 fps backoff it never left, then suspended it (it faded out) at the next burst, so on this machine it ran at 10 fps and vanished while typing. **(2)** `publishCamera` read the viewport's rect on every camera frame, forcing a layout of the whole board each frame (≈ 390 ms of a pane opening on 200 cards); it's now measured on resize. **(3)** The floor's edges were inherited custom properties transitioning on `.grid-viewport`, so every card restyled on each frame of a pane opening; they're now non-inherited and set on `.world-mask` alone. **(4)** The dissolve now waits for the board to be live and the page to draw on time before it starts (the wind keeps turning meanwhile). Also: the wind reveals in 1.6 s (was 4 s, which read as a slow load). Next: a slower GPU (the integrated Radeon here, a MacBook Air) and a real trackpad.

## 6. Hookups a person needs to do

Every integration is off until its key exists; the server always starts. Copy `.env.example` to `.env`.

| # | Hookup | Why | How |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Repo visibility** | `joshleong/Foundation` is **public**; company plans and connector names are in it | Make it private, or move it to the Form Industries org |
| 2 | **Google sign-in** | Real accounts | Works out of the box through flowform-server (`FLOWFORM_URL`). flowform's client secret stays in AWS SSM; Foundation never needs it. Dev login is off unless `FOUNDATION_DEV_LOGIN=1`; leave it off in shared deployments. A hosted Foundation (not on loopback) signs in with its own `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` / `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` (built 2026-11-20, `docs/hosting.md`: an Internal consent screen on the company's Workspace, redirect `<address>/auth/google/callback`); when both are set every Google button uses them, on a local server too. Flowform widening `native_loopback_callback` is the other way, not built. The desktop app's page (http://localhost:31440) is admitted by CORS already; a web client on another origin needs `FOUNDATION_CORS_ORIGINS` |
| 3 | **Allowlist** | Who can enter | `FOUNDATION_ALLOWLIST=@formindustries.com,@normallab.co,…` |
| 0 | **Node 22.16 or newer** | The server, run from the repo (the installed desktop app carries its own Node 22.22 and needs none) | Older Node 22 has no FTS5 in `node:sqlite` and the server dies at boot. Since 2026-11-09 `npm run dev` / `npm run desktop` check first and say so (`scripts/node-check.mjs`), and the server's own error names the fix. Josh's Windows machine has 22.13.1 at `C:\Program Files\nodejs`: `winget install OpenJS.NodeJS.LTS` |
| 4 | **Anthropic API credits** | API runtime | `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. The subscription runtime needs only `claude` installed and signed in. On Josh's Windows machine (2026-11-07) Claude Code's own sign-in had expired ("OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed"). Signing in to the Claude desktop app doesn't sign in the CLI: `claude auth status` said `loggedIn: false` until **`claude auth login`** was run in a terminal. Since then real crew turns run |
| 5 | **BomForm** | The connector | Now reads the **Airform Data Room's daily copy** through its read-only `GET /api/foundation/bomform` digest (branch `foundation-bomform-read` in AirformDataRoom): set `FOUNDATION_READ_TOKEN` there and `DATAROOM_BOMFORM_URL` + `DATAROOM_READ_TOKEN` here. Locally the launcher runs that branch's worktree on :3010 (`node scripts/sibling.mjs dataroom AirformDataRoom-foundation`). **For production:** merge the branch, set the token on Vercel, deploy, and point `DATAROOM_BOMFORM_URL` at airformdata.com. **The copy is stale:** last synced 2026-09-05; the Data Room's daily sync has stopped (check `BOMFORM_API_KEY` / `CRON_SECRET` on Vercel). A direct BomForm key (`BOMFORM_MCP_URL` + `BOMFORM_API_KEY` + `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`) still takes precedence when present |
| 6 | **FactoryForm, Creation** | Live factory data; the studio and its Hub | `npm run services` starts FactoryForm (:3001), Creation (:5173 — its own config says 1420, Vision's port) and the Creation Hub (:4317) from the sibling folders (or `FOUNDATION_SIBLINGS`); each is also a launch config. FactoryForm is followed live through its `/api/events` stream. For FactoryForm writes over MCP, `FACTORY_MCP_TOKEN` |
| 7 | **Slack** | Channel cards, links, huddle invites, Hermes DMs, search | Create the app from **`docs/slack-app-manifest.yml`** (api.slack.com/apps → From a manifest), install it, and set the bot token as `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN`. That covers channel cards (read, post as the person, links, invites, huddle notes) and Hermes DMs. For ⌘K search, set the user token as `SLACK_USER_TOKEN` (`search:read`). For private channels, `/invite @Foundation` first. For the agent to lay out Slack images: the user token's search:read finds them by full text; the bot token needs `files:read` (in the manifest now), and then finds them by name |
| 8 | **GitHub** | Repo discovery across the org | `GITHUB_TOKEN` (falls back to the `gh` login) and `FOUNDATION_GITHUB_ORG` |
| 10 | **REA** | Reverse engineering | `npx rea-agents@6.9.0 setup` once (registers REA's MCP with Claude Code; MIT; verify it's `morluto/rea`). Then use Run on a reverse-engineering card |
| 11 | **Lufga** | Display face | Install the licensed files locally; the stack falls back to Hanken Grotesk. Don't commit them to a public repo |
| 13 | **Kiri or Docker** | Machines in sections | **`npm run kiri:install`** installs Kiri: the pinned release from GitHub, SHA-256 checked. On Windows (needs WSL 2) it's per user, into `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Kiri`, with the WSL distro `kiri`; it changes no docker context or PATH, so Docker Desktop stays docker's default. On macOS and Linux it prints the `.dmg` / `sudo apt install ./….deb` step. `npm run kiri` starts it, `npm run kiri -- status` says what's there, and `npm run dev` starts it too (`FOUNDATION_KIRI=0` leaves it alone). To move to a new Kiri, change `VERSION` and the hashes in `scripts/kiri.mjs` from that release's `SHA256SUMS`. Or install Docker Desktop. Foundation finds `\\.\pipe\kiri`, `~/.kiri/run/docker.sock`, `/run/kiri/docker.sock`, then Docker's socket (or `DOCKER_HOST`); `FOUNDATION_ENGINES=kiri=<socket>,docker=<socket>` names them instead (and wakes nothing). `FOUNDATION_MACHINE_MEMORY_MB` / `FOUNDATION_MACHINE_CPUS` change the caps; both engines honour them. Check with `npm run machines:smoke -w @foundation/server [-- kiri]` (it runs alpine) |
| 14 | **Flowform boards and files** | Reading your Flowform from Foundation | Settings → Services → **Connect Flowform**, then approve on Flowform's page (it asks to read your boards and search your files, nothing else). Each person connects their own. `FLOWFORM_URL` names the server (default server.formresearch.ai). Foundation's callback is `<FOUNDATION_PUBLIC_URL>/auth/flowform/link/callback`, registered with Flowform the first time someone connects; a hosted Foundation needs Flowform to accept that address |
| 15 | **Places** | Files on this computer | Settings → Places, as an admin: the folder's full path. Nothing is approved by default |
| 16 | **A minimum client version** | Making everyone take a release the server can't work without | `FOUNDATION_MIN_CLIENT=0.4.0` in the server's environment, **after** that version is on the update feed (`npm run release -- publish`). Older apps are told, can't put it off, and get no board socket until they update. Unset it to let everyone back in |
| 12 | **Vision ↔ Foundation** | Hand-offs to Vision Code | Works with Vision Code running on this machine (T3 :3783). Foundation looks for its CLI in a Vision checkout (`~/Development/Vision`, `~/Dev/Vision`, `C:\Dev\Vision`: `.tools/code-server/t3`, `t3.exe` on Windows), then an installed Vision's `code-server/`, then `t3` on PATH. It reads Vision's T3CODE_HOME from `~/Library/Application Support/co.normallab.vision/code` (macOS), `%APPDATA%\co.normallab.vision\code` (Windows) or `~/.local/share/co.normallab.vision/code` (Linux) (`t3paths.ts`). If nothing is found, the side pane and the hand-off card list where it looked. Without `T3_URL`, Foundation reads Vision Code's address from `<T3CODE_HOME>/userdata/server-runtime.json`, which T3 writes when it starts (Vision takes the next free port if :3783 is taken), and uses `http://127.0.0.1:3783` if that file is missing or unusable. Set `T3_CLI`, `T3CODE_HOME` and `T3_URL` if they live elsewhere; when set, they're the only places looked. A hosted Foundation needs its own route to Vision Code (a pairing link instead of the local CLI). Approve a thread's file writes in Vision Code |
| 17 | **Chrome extension** | Every employee's new tab is Today | The whole route is in `apps/extension/README.md`. (1) Confirm the placeholders in `apps/extension/release.json` (the hosted Foundation's address, the company's domains, the support email, the privacy URL); it needs a hosted Foundation on HTTPS (§8 *Ship*). (2) `npm run release -w @foundation/extension`. (3) A person registers a Chrome Web Store developer account under a company Google account (a one-time fee) and uploads the zip as **Private** to the company's Workspace domain, with the listing from `STORE.md` and the images in `store/`; then puts the item's id in `release.json`. (4) A Workspace admin force-installs it (Devices → Chrome → Apps & extensions → Users & browsers → add by id → Force install) and pastes `dist/policy/admin-console-policy.json` as its policy; optionally Today as home and startup page. Machines outside Workspace: `dist/policy/windows.reg` and the macOS plists |
| 18 | **App addresses** | Today's apps | Since 2026-11-19 the four on HyperForm are linked where they are hosted with nothing set (§0.10); `HYPERFORM_URL`, `PARTFORM_URL` (or the older `BOMFORM_URL`), `DEPLOYFORM_URL`, `FACTORYFORM_APP_URL` move one on this machine. Still to set: `FLEETFORM_URL` (and optionally `VISION_URL`, `CREATION_URL`, `DATAROOM_URL`). To see them inside Foundation a person signs in to HyperForm once in the built-in browser (top of this file). The list itself is `packages/protocol/src/apps.ts`; apps added on boards (⌘L) join it automatically |
| 19 | **Data Room goals** | People's projects and goals from the Airform Data Room on Today | Not built: needs a read endpoint in AirformDataRoom (proposed: `GET /api/foundation/goals?email=` → `[{id, title, detail, progress, dueAt, project}]`, bearer `FOUNDATION_READ_TOKEN`, like the BomForm digest). Then set `DATAROOM_GOALS_URL` here; Today shows them read-only with source "dataroom" (the goals table already has `source` and `external_id`) |
| 20 | **Meshy** | The Character studio draws, models, rigs and animates through it | `MESHY_API_KEY` in `.env` (set here 2026-11-14; a Pro plan or higher for `nano-banana-pro`, else set `MESHY_IMAGE_MODEL=nano-banana-2-1`). Each stage is a person's press and says its cost first: about 86 credits for a 3D character, 18 for a mesh avatar's pictures. Without the key the cards say Meshy isn't connected and nothing can be pressed |

## 7. Known issues

- **The release history, as built 2026-11-27: Review** (§0.24). (1) **A checkout's committed `docs/releases.json` goes stale** as CI publishes: a dev client whose tag is newer than the file says *not in the release history* until someone runs `git fetch --tags && node scripts/release.mjs history` (installed apps are always whole: each CI build writes it). (2) Release titles and changes are commit subjects, as the CI notes are: a merge commit's subject is dropped, so a release that is only a merge shows *Small fixes*. (3) Settings → Releases still lists the hand-written posts only. (4) Two checks were already red and are not this change: e2e `bottom-bar` *the side menu's foot* (the orb 213 px from the Settings icon) and the tools test's built-in browser step (`[data-browser-frame]` never shows); and the server's `agent-kinds` *a lost sign-in is said…* timing (under 700 ms) fails under the full parallel suite and passes alone, 3 of 3.

- **Note pages, as built 2026-11-26: Review** (docs/notes.md). (1) **Every grid note is listed under its grid** (Josh's call, 2026-11-26). The list is read when Notes is shown, again when grids change and every 20 s while it's open, so a note made a moment ago can take that long to appear; a grid note's title can't be renamed from its full-size view (rename it on its card). (2) **A new page is open to everyone on the server**, like a new grid; members-only is the space's People, as for any grid (also §5). (3) **A card showing a page has no Markdown view and no carets**: its sync is the page's words only (no awareness), so others' carets and the CodeMirror markdown view are on the page and on grid notes, not on a page's card; ⌘Z there is the browser's own. (4) **A slash or a shortcut is an edit like typing**: the "/" goes out to others while the menu is open and is taken back when a block is picked. (5) **Toggles open and shut for everyone** who can write (kept as `<details open>`); a reader folds on their own screen. (6) Search, Rune, wires and the timeline read a page on the page's own board; on the grid a page card is a card with no words (`read_board` says which page it shows). (7) **Checked in Chromium on the web client** (two people, day and night); not in the desktop app's WebView2 or on the Mac. The note e2e checks around it pass again (section-agents 6/6, together 7/7, timeline-live's live band, pointer 1–2b, renders); four were already red on main (c57e89b) and still are: card-chrome 2 and timeline-live 2 (a writing note's toolbar sits ~10 px under the card's foot), grid-undo 2 (Delete on a selected image), pointer 3 (the section menu's Split column). A person checks: make a note in Notes, write in it with the slash menu, put it on a grid, edit it there and see it on the page.

- **Today's notifications and the bell beside Today, as built 2026-11-26: Review.** (1) **Checked on Foundation's own chat server only**, in Chromium on the web client: not against the Rust chat server (where Today falls back to its per-conversation items), not in the desktop app. (2) **A grid tag has no seen state**: a note that tags you stays under Notifications while it tags you, with no dot; only chat rows are marked seen (this device's, as the bell's are). (3) **Grid notifications are tags in notes only**: an ask or a stage you own stays a todo, and a comment on a card isn't one (there are none yet). (4) The grids Today reads tags from are every grid on the server, as Today's focus always has (not filtered by who may open a grid).

- **A huddle's devices, as built 2026-11-26: Review.** Josh: "Voice and video doesn't work, it should probably also ask for permissions for the mic and the camera and have a small device icon to indicate that it's connected and to choose another." **Found:** a huddle asked for the camera and the microphone in one `getUserMedia`; on a computer with a microphone and no camera (or a camera another app holds) the browser failed it with *Requested device not found* before any permission prompt, and the call ended there ("No camera or mic found"). Reproduced in the app's browser pane (a microphone, no camera). **Built:** `lib/media.ts` opens each device on its own terms: one request (one prompt) when both exist, an absent camera never asked for, a busy or missing one costing only itself, a refusal not asked again for the device refused; the call goes ahead with whatever works, muted or camera-off for what didn't. A **device chip** (headset, `panels/DeviceChip.tsx`) in every call's controls: a dot (ok / warn: no camera / bad: no microphone), a picker for the microphone, camera and speaker (`setSinkId`, where the browser allows), the microphone's level, and for a blocked, busy or missing device why and **Allow / Ask again** (the browser's prompt, on a person's press). It opens by itself on joining when the microphone didn't come on. Unmuting or turning on a camera you don't have yet asks for it. A device switched mid-call replaces the track for everyone (`replaceTrack`, no renegotiation); one unplugged says so. Choices are remembered in this browser. **Checked:** `test/media-devices.test.ts` (10), `e2e/huddle-devices.spec.ts` (3: no camera, joined and heard by a second person; another microphone mid-call, heard on the same connection, the old track stopped; a blocked microphone, joined muted, the chip open with *Allow the microphone*, then heard), `huddle`, `chatform-huddle` and `media-notes` specs, typecheck. Evidence `docs/evidence/2026-11-26_huddle-devices/` (night and day). **Not checked:** real cameras and microphones, the desktop app's WebView2 prompt (Tauri leaves it to WebView2's own dialog; a refusal there is remembered by WebView2 and *Allow* can't undo it: Windows Settings or clearing the app's WebView data can), a Mac. (1) A device switched while recording ends that person's file and starts another. (2) Captions (Web Speech) listen to the system's default microphone, not the one picked. (3) `#/design` shows the chip and its picker only in the huddle row; this machine's own `:4410` server was busy, so the dev server wasn't driven by hand.

- **The bar's Ask pill, as built 2026-11-26: Review.**
  - (1) **The session column has no button on the bar.** Ask was its button; it is now the line to the grid's agents. ⌘J opens and puts away the column, and ⌘K's "Ask the agent" starts one, but nothing on screen says so.
  - (2) **One line starts a run for every connected agent on the grid.** Six agents are six runs, each on its own credentials, from one press of ↵, with no count asked back first (the placeholder says how many: "Ask all 6 agents…"). A person starts it, so the invariant holds; the cost is the thing to watch.
  - (3) **With a session column open, a typed line still goes to the grid's agents** (the column has its own composer); a spoken one goes to the session, as before. The two differ on purpose, and it may read as inconsistent.
  - (4) **Checked with the stand-in agent and recogniser only**, in Chromium, on the web client: not with a real model, a real voice, several real agents at once, or in the desktop app.
  - (5) **Two browser checks were already red on main (241c555) and still are; each stops the serial tests after it in its file.** `bottom-bar.spec.ts` "the side menu's foot" (the day/night orb is 213 px from the Settings icon it expects to be in line with), which keeps "day and night reach the grid's paper" and "the tools" from running; and `section-crew.spec.ts` "this computer's agents" (it expects "writes the board", the pane says "writes the grid"). `machines.spec.ts` was changed for the Ask pill and not run. `council.spec.ts` "a 3D character" failed once in three runs (the expressions picture never showed) and passed on the others.

- **The timeline live and whole, a note and a chat card as one box, as built 2026-11-26 (§0.22): Review.** (1) **Recording is unverified with real devices** and in the desktop app (WebView2 asks for the microphone and camera itself; never pressed here). (2) **A long stretch is thinned** to 400 steps: an event's moment then draws a step that may hold its neighbours' changes too. (3) The nudge reaches windows joined to that grid; a section that is another grid reads every 20 s. (4) The right-click menu's Paste needs the browser's clipboard permission; refused, it says to press ⌘/Ctrl+V. (5) While a note is written its bar and toolbar add about 36 px above and 40–80 px below it; a note in a section's top or bottom row is drawn over its section's edge then. (6) Where the edits behind the 2026-11-26 report went is not known (§0.22).
- **The timeline, voice and video notes, and menus that rise, as built 2026-11-25 (§0.21): Review.**
  - (1) **History begins when this server first opens a grid.** Nothing before that can be shown, and the band says so. Past the budget (256 MB a grid) the oldest history is dropped and the beginning moves; events are kept.
  - (2) **The past is looked at, not restored.** A removed card can be seen and its voice or video note played, but not brought back. Removing a card does not remove it from history, and there is no purge yet: say so before anyone treats delete as erase.
  - (3) **Not drawn in the past:** a hosted app (it stays live), a section that has since gone (listed under *Sections that were here*), a child grid inside its card (its bar says to open the grid), a chat card's conversation (it is in the moment column), maps, apps and machines (a mark and a name).
  - (4) **A chat server of its own** (the desktop app's default) pages by message, not time: the moment reads back 200 messages and then offers the conversation. Never exercised: the checks have no such server.
  - (5) **Behind the scenes is thinner than the ask.** Tool calls, scripts and machine commands are recorded; a place's harness prompt and its own terminal are not built (their kinds are reserved), T3 runs leave no answer, a desktop action shows as its tool call, and a pointer in the past does not say what the agent was doing.
  - (6) **Recording is checked in Chromium only.** WebKit and the Mac app were not run. `src-tauri/Info.plist` now declares the microphone and camera; whether the signed app also needs entitlements for them is unchecked, and huddles depend on the same answer.
  - (7) **Not measured:** scrubbing a busy grid, or with `PERF_GPU=1`; the `:has` rules that let a risen bar and toolbar out of a section's body on a heavy board. A history answer is built whole in memory, capped at 24 MB (413 past it).
  - (8) **Small:** a risen bar covers about 14 px of the card above it while it shows, and a toolbar part of the card below; a catch-up after a restart cannot see changes that only deleted things; a section's bar scales with zoom and is tiny zoomed out; `describeBoard` in the protocol still calls every media card *media* (the server rewrites note lines after it); plain sound files dropped on a grid are still file cards.
  - (9) **The full suites are sensitive to load on this machine.** With other work running, `wires.test.ts` (a 400 ms budget), `chatagents`, `pagecard`, `codemode` (its deadline test), `t3host` and `chathuddle` each failed once and passed alone; on a quiet machine all 382 client and 695 server tests passed.
  - (10) **Real-browser specs, as last run:** `timeline` 8/8, `media-notes` 5/5, `chatform-scenario` 2/2 (Replay, whose past cards changed), `card-chrome`, `section-agents`, `sections` and `sub-grid` passing after three fixes this work needed (the section clock had borrowed the *opens as a grid* button’s class; the section menu’s list and key walk gained *Voice or video note…*; a design-page class was shared). Two checks were changed rather than the app: `sections.spec.ts` now opens the folded layout button first (the 2026-11-24 header folded it; the check predated that), and `card-chrome`’s scroll-line check restyles before reading (Chromium answered a stale style once a hover changed nothing else on the card; the line was seen lit by hand). **`pointer.spec.ts` test 3 still fails** at its last step: a note made from the section menu lands under the pointer, and the check’s next right-click there opens that note’s menu instead of the section’s. A menu-made note has landed there since before this work and the 2026-11-24 commit says this spec was not seen passing, but it was not run against the previous commit to prove it. The whole e2e suite was not run.

(4) Not tried with a real T3 agent or a real Claude turn, only the stand-ins. (5) The desktop app's server on :4410 serves none of it until it restarts.

Until it serves a `part_model` tool (docs/cad-feed.md), "pull the CAD out of Airform BOM" ends in a plain sentence saying so, now shown in the trace as *Didn't finish*. (4) A timed CAD refresh runs under the name of the person who set the card up; if they lose write access the refresh is refused (logged on the server) and the card keeps its model without saying why.

That is a change of behaviour from 2026-11-15 ("every card draws itself"), made for the frames; say if the studio should move more at rest. The chat server also needs `CHATFORM_TRUSTED_CLIENT_IDS`.

Not checked: the app against the Rust server, day, a second person on a grid, a huddle from the card, desktop notifications in the installed app.

Then ask an agent, or call the tool, to lay the examples on the company board. Its existing BomForm and FactoryForm cards are wired from where they are.

- **Images and video, and the grid's undo, as built 2026-11-24: Review.** (1) The undo stack lasts while the board is open in this window (a `Y.UndoManager` isn't persisted): a reload starts it empty. (2) An undo restores what *you* last wrote; if someone has since changed the same card, Yjs keeps theirs and your step does less than you'd expect. (3) Undo doesn't move the camera: taking back a change off screen happens off screen. (4) The selection and the section in use aren't part of a step (a card brought back isn't selected again). (5) What isn't the board's document isn't undone: a sub-grid made from a section (*Sub-grids: no undo*, below), a card sent to another board, a section's agents and huddles. (6) Delete removes the selected card of any kind, not only images; a card that asks first (a character) still asks, and Backspace never reaches a card whose content has the caret. (7) **Rune** draws images itself and still offers its own remove button there, and ⌘Z in its note field is Rune's; the grid's undo keys are the web renderer's. (8) `e2e/pointer.spec.ts` test 1 stopped in this checkout at selecting a note's words (other sessions' uncommitted note and wheel work is in it); 1b and day/night pass.
- **One note, as built 2026-11-24: Review.** (1) **Rune** still draws a plain note itself and opens it in its own field, as markdown: no toolbar, no Markdown or Background button, no text size there. A note with a size of its own, without its paper, or made as a text box before this is an island (the web's), which is how those two show at all under Rune; not looked at in a browser under Rune. The ledger says so (`rune/parity.ts`). (2) **The formatted surface keeps what the note's markdown can hold** (as the text box did): once someone writes in a note, the whole of it is kept as the surface reads it back, so `*x*` becomes `_x_`, `*` bullets become `-`, a list inside a list comes back flat, and a heading below the fourth becomes a fourth. Until then it is untouched; the Markdown side never rewrites anything but what you type. (3) **Text size is the card's, not a word's**, and the markdown side is always the editor's own size. (4) `rich` is still on older cards and still in the schema (nothing reads it but Rune's island rule); no board was migrated and the schema number didn't move, so an older desktop build reads the same boards, shows new notes as markdown notes, and ignores size and background. (5) A note without its paper is found by its dashed edge under the pointer; an empty one at rest shows only *Type here* (to a writer) and nothing to a viewer. (6) The toolbar is a little wider than a 420 px note (an agent's): it scrolls sideways there. (7) `e2e/pointer.spec.ts` check 3 is out of date with the menu (two Live entries it doesn't expect, a Split column icon that moved to the section header), so its key walk, which names the one Note, hasn't run.

- **Whole-app usability pass (2026-11-22, §0.15): Review.** Checked in Chromium (`e2e/usability.spec.ts` 5/5, `docs/evidence/2026-11-22_usability-pass/`), `test/usability.test.ts` (11/11), `test/design.test.ts` (4/4), `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, and `npm run check:security`. - **ChatForm's Rust server (2026-11-21)** is deployed on Vercel since 2026-11-24 (production deploys from every push to `main`); its Neon store is connected with a prefix, read through `CHATFORM_DATABASE_PREFIX`. On Vercel a socket closes at the function's time limit (300 s on Hobby) and the client reconnects; Rust functions and WebSockets are both Vercel betas. Files, pins, starring, notify settings, archiving, agents, huddles, portals, bridges and the Slack import aren't on it.
- **ChatForm on a chat server (2026-11-22)**: checked only in a browser on this computer against a local Rust server and a stand-in Google, not in the desktop app's window, on a deployed server or with real Google. The server must list the app's page in `CHATFORM_CORS_ORIGINS` (`http://localhost:31440` for the desktop app) or the sign-in says it can't be reached. While ChatForm is on a chat server: who is where on the grids is read once and no longer kept live (it rode Foundation's chat socket, which isn't open); the Agents, Inbox and Huddles tabs aren't shown at all, though Foundation's server still has them; a message's `@agent` starts nothing; conversations kept in the side menu are kept by id on Foundation's server, so ones from the other server show as gone; people who have signed in but hold no socket appear in the people list only after a reload; the setting is per device. **Since 2026-11-24 the desktop app defaults to the team's server** (`foundation-chatserver.vercel.app`; `builtChatServer`, `vite build --mode desktop` only, so the dev server and the e2e build stay on Foundation's), and Settings lists it with the servers the device has used, a **Use** each; a device that chose another or Foundation's keeps its choice. Google sign-in there is in testing mode: only the project's test users (josh@formindustries.co so far) can sign in.

- **Who is where, as built 2026-11-21**: a person counts as on a grid while a window of theirs holds its sync socket, which Today and ChatForm keep open for the last grid opened, so "on Form Industries" can mean "had it open last"; a person's ChatForm dot (chat socket) and where they are (sync socket) can disagree; it reaches only windows that hold the chat socket (every signed-in window does); restricted grids are filtered, but who is on an open grid is visible to everyone who can see that grid.
- **A conversation on a grid, as built 2026-11-25 (unit-tested only; nothing below was seen in a browser)**: the card, its drop, its picker, the in-card thread, the docked call and the `#/design` specimens have not been opened in a browser in day or night, at 360 × 480, zoomed, or under Rune. **Across computers**: a grid's document syncs only between people on the same Foundation server; two people on different Foundation servers who both open "the same" grid are on two different documents, and a chat card helps them only if both cards point at the same chat server and conversation. What crosses computers is the conversation (the chat server's) and, on a chat server with `where`, who has which grid id open; cursors, cards and edits do not. The chat server's people are matched to Foundation's by id, then by name (two servers, two ids for one person): two people with the same name read as one face, and someone whose names differ between the servers is drawn twice in the side menu's count. The window sends the grid's id and never its name, because the client can't tell a members-only grid (`/api/boards` carries no such mark): the id of a members-only grid, with who is on it, still reaches the chat server and every window signed in to it (which drop a grid they don't list). The Rust server keeps and relays a `name` for people without the grid; this client never sends one and ignores it. On a chat server Foundation's own who-is-where is re-read every 20 s rather than pushed. **Voice** is the channel's huddle and exists only when ChatForm is on Foundation's own server; the desktop app defaults to the team's chat server, so there the card has text and a line saying voice needs Foundation's own. **Carrying** a conversation out of ChatForm's list is off on a chat server (as keeping it in the side menu is), so there a card is added from the section's menu. A card is not told apart from another card of the same conversation in another section (each is its own view of the same store). Reading marks the conversation read only while the card is in use. Alt-wheel now treats a pane that rests at its foot (`column-reverse`) correctly in `lib/cardScroll.ts`; that path is unit-tested, not tried with a wheel. A chat card on a board an agent reads (`read_board`) shows as a card named `#name` or *Conversation* with a channel id; agents read the conversation itself only through the chat tools, under their person's access.

<!-- security-notes -->
- **The security pass of 2026-11-18** (ADR-001 slices 1 and 2 and what followed; rules in `docs/SECURITY.md`; evidence in `docs/evidence/2026-11-18_*`).
  - **Closed:** sign-in by email alone on by default; work only a person should start being startable with an agent's token; app tools called at an address taken from a card with no person asked; programs the server starts inheriting its keys, and one fetched unpinned; uploaded files able to run as the app; machine terminals and desktops taking input from an agent's connection; other local pages able to use the signed-in session; repo reads outside a repository's name; boards' files and settings reachable without membership; sends to Slack under a person's name without the person; agents sending to outside services with no one's say; no record of who did what; remote pictures in rendered text fetched on sight; frames able to show Foundation itself; the Slack agent running with a shell.
  - **Still open, in general terms** (the detail is kept off this public repository until each is closed; `docs/SECURITY.md`): some actions are not yet in the audit log, and its chain is held only by the process that writes it; the model call itself is a send that is not gated; the shared service token is broader than it should be; some programs a person installed are used at whatever version is there, and one keeps its home under the data folder; machine images are pulled by tag; a pinned program's own dependencies are resolved when it runs; the content security policy is report-only, and off in the packaged desktop app; the private-content rule for outgoing sends is a text rule with known limits.
  - **What changed for people:**
    - Sign-in by email alone is off unless turned on in the server's settings, for your own computer only.
    - Agents send to outside services (search, reads, messages) only where someone who can edit the board has turned that on, in the board's pane. Every board starts with nothing on.
    - An app's tools can be called only for an app a person saved, and each tool needs a person's press the first time. There is no button in the app to save an app yet, so only seeded and API-saved apps can be called.
    - SVG uploads are downloads now, not pictures on a card.
    - A picture from elsewhere in rendered text waits for a press; loading it does go out, with no referrer.
    - In a frame, an address on this computer that is not a saved app runs without an origin of its own, so its sign-in and storage do not work there. A web site elsewhere keeps its own.
    - Another local page that called Foundation with the signed-in cookie, and the dev client opened at 127.0.0.1 in place of localhost, are refused for writes unless listed in the server's allowed origins.
    - Agent conversations made before this date may start afresh: the agent's working folder moved out of the data folder.
    - Which GitHub owners the repo tools may read is the director's call; unset, any owner the server's GitHub login can see.
