  - **Not checked with a real run:** the packaged desktop app against any of these rules; a real agent turn and a real T3 start with the trimmed environment; a real job; a real Slack, GitHub or Flowform; the built-in terminal; most of the e2e suite (the specs that sign in, start T3, and use frames were run). The asks and switches were seen on `#/design`, day and night, not on a live board against a running server.
<!-- /security-notes -->
- **The feature branch met the security pass on 2026-11-20** (`feat/edges-places-flowform` merged with main; Josh: "Merge main into it"). What the merge changed: git for Foundation's avatars, and cloudflared and its lookup for a machine's links, now start through `child.ts` (the allowlisted environment, no keys; git signs in from this computer's own credential store, which was not tried after the change); the machine Access view reads agents' leave from the board's `desktopLeave`; a write to a relayed machine port (`/m/…`) goes through the same forgery check as the rest; the wheel is main's (`lib/wheel.ts`, with its setting), and the branch's `lib/wheelKind.ts` is left unused with its test. **Not reviewed against `docs/SECURITY.md`:** Kubernetes clusters, machine functions, the uploader's timer, and above all the relay itself: a machine's web app is served on Foundation's own origin, so its page's scripts run as a page of Foundation's with the person's sign-in. That wants a person's decision (a separate origin, or no cookie on that path) before a machine runs anything not trusted.

- **Themes, the browser, the release page, sheets and the card menu, as built 2026-11-18** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-18_themes/`, whose `NOTES.md` says how each part was checked).
  - **Every section a person adds starts with a pinned table.** Josh confirmed it on 2026-11-19 ("Keep it"). It also means a new section is never the *Bring work here* empty state. An app opened into the untouched table takes the section; anything else lands over the sheet. If it should be a choice instead, it is one function (`startWithSheet`, `state/store.ts`).
  - **Only tables pin**, one to a section, and a pinned table can't be minimized or floated. Cards in the flow lie over it and can hide cells. Its columns don't follow the section when it's resized: the table is scaled to the width instead (a much wider section draws bigger cells) until it is unpinned and pinned again.
  - **The section adjuster on older grids was not reproduced.** The grab areas are now one per section edge (tested), but the checks ran on fresh data; Josh's older grids weren't opened, so what was wrong there may be something else.
  - **The wheel no longer tells a mouse from a trackpad** (2026-11-24; the guess made a wheel on a scaled display, or a high-resolution one, pan). Every wheel event zooms, so two fingers on a trackpad zoom too, and a trackpad has no two-finger pan while the setting is *Zooms* (drag the paper, or Settings → General → *The wheel on a grid* → *Pans*). Not tried on a real trackpad. The e2e specs written when the wheel panned keep that setting (`e2e/helpers.ts`); only `look.spec.ts` runs the default. Those older specs were not re-run today.
  - **Page cards (2026-11-19, §0.10): not verified against the real HyperForm.** Everything was checked against a stand-in page. Not known: (1) **whether Google's sign-in works in this browser**: it is a headless Chrome driven over the DevTools pipe, and Google refuses some automated browsers ("this browser or app may not be secure"); if it does, the way in is HyperForm's own token or machine certificate, not built; (2) how PartForm, DeployForm and FactoryForm look at a card's size (a new section is 1520 × 1745 CSS px, drawn at about 40 % when framed); (3) whether HyperForm's session outlives a browser restart (the profile is kept, so it should). **The kept picture is a signed-in page that anyone who can see the board is served**: DeployForm's is customers. That is the point of the far view, but it means a page card on a board shares what its page shows with that board's people, admins or not; keep such a card on a board whose people may see it. **A picture is never refreshed in the background**, and one that fails to load shows the card's name instead. **An agent's "did" line shows on every open page card**, not only the one it acted in. **`via` and `seenAt` are new optional fields of a panel**: an older client ignores them and shows such a card as a frame (which stays blank for these sites).
  - **The built-in browser runs on the server's computer, not in the app.** It is sent as pictures: no remote cursor shapes, no IME, text selection only through the page; sound is muted; downloads and file choosers are refused. On a dense screen a sharp picture follows 140 ms after the page stops moving. Reopening it right after closing takes 2–3 s.
  - **What you sign into in it stays signed in** (`work/host-browser`, a Chrome profile, not encrypted beyond what Chrome does), and it sits on the server's network (it can open `localhost` and LAN addresses, Foundation's own included). Agents get in only with the browser open and *Let agents use this browser* on, and only the agents of the person who turned it on; they then act with those sign-ins. Pages are other people's text: an agent is told to read them as content.
  - **Merged with the security pass on 2026-11-19.** The built-in browser now starts through the one door (`child.ts`: the allowlisted environment, none of the server's keys), keeps its profile in the work folder (`work/host-browser`, not `data/`), and dev sign-in is refused while it is open, as it is for the terminal. Its framed fallback (a non-admin) and T3's frame follow the frame rules (`frameSandbox.ts`). A browser whose profile had been used before sometimes drew no first picture (the one picture a still page sends came at the size before, and nothing followed): a picture at the size now is owed, and the cast is begun again until it comes (checked 12 times on a reused profile, 3 on a fresh one; it failed about one time in three before).
  - **Not exercised in a browser**: the browser's clipboard write-back, AltGr, multi-click selection, the non-admin framed fallback beyond its empty state, the 30 s dialog timeout; macOS and Linux launch paths (unit-tested only). No e2e spec drives the browser tool; its server test does.
  - **T3's page is still T3's own.** In the pane its frame is told light or dark (`color-scheme`); T3 was not started here, so whether its client follows was not seen. Its tab mints a new sign-in link when opened; closing the tab drops the page.
  - **A grid's pinned look is the whole window's while that grid is open** (the top bar and panes too), and the orb can't override it there; its tip says so.
  - **The Changes pane's counts are read from the post's lists**: a release written as prose with no lists counts one thing per part.
  - **Rune** draws a pinned table as a card in the flow and knows nothing of minimized cards.
  - **Not checked:** the desktop window, a viewer in a browser (the menu's viewer branch is by reading), two people on a pinned table at once, night on Today for the release page (day was shot; the tokens are the room's either way).
  - **The running server serves none of the server side until it restarts** (the desktop app's, on :4410): the browser, the tidied terminal, apps as tabs and a grid's look need it.
- **Councils, named seats and Ins & outs, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_council-seats/`).
  - **The circle on the monkey** was read as the round violet glow under an agent in its pane (removed). If it meant something else, it wasn't found: in the council itself no circle showed on a copy of the live data.
  - **An agent asked from a council stays on that council only on the asker's screen**: the tie is the asker's session, which only their browser knows. Everyone else sees it on the council of the section it works in.
  - **Your live machine's pipes weren't touched.** Its Out is wired to the note "How it works", and a test line would overwrite that note's text; the pipe was proven on a fresh machine instead. Wire Out somewhere else first (Ins & outs → Out to a new note on the right).
  - **Talking to the second Claude (Garnet)** goes to T3's Claude, which isn't connected on this copy; nothing was asked of a real agent.
  - **Adding the same agent again** was checked on the server; the Add sheet's *Add another* wasn't run in a browser.
  - **Not checked:** the desktop window, Rune, voice in the white line.
- **Collections, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_collections/`, whose `NOTES.md` says how each part was checked).
  - **Nothing goes into mesh-avatar-studio itself.** The Mesh avatar node still asks for a `.mavatar` built by hand in mesh-avatar-studio; a collection gathers the portraits it is cut from and the finished characters, not the studio's own work.
  - **Rune doesn't draw a collection** (`apps/rune`): under the GPU renderer the card is blank and there is no drag-out.
  - **No wire is drawn to the section a collection lays out into.** The card's foot says where (*Lay out in*, with *n laid ›* to go there); the section itself doesn't say what feeds it.
  - **Opened, a collection covers the cards beside it.** A floating one at a section's right (after a flow's Output) moves left to fit while it's open, over Output and Moods, and goes back when it's folded; the wire into it routes round it meanwhile.
  - **A copy is a snapshot.** A character's copy shows it as it was when copied; a stage redone on the original doesn't reach it, and its row stays when the card is removed (like any character's).
  - **Gathering follows a board while the server has it loaded** (a change to its wires, cards or characters gathers 60 ms later). A collection on a board nobody has opened since the server started catches up when someone opens it.
  - **Not run**: the live Character studio (its Output nodes get their output when it's next opened on a server running this), Rune, the desktop window, a viewer in a browser (the tools' refusal is tested on the server; the card hides drag-out and × for a viewer), a trackpad.
  - **The running server serves none of it until it restarts** (the desktop app's, on :4410; top of this file).
- **The bottom bar, the tools and the councils at the right, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_bottom-bar/`).
  - **The add-agent flow didn't work on the running app because that server is old** (top of this file): on this code the T3 host starts and lists agents, and the *Turn it on* step fixes the one real dead end (an agent T3 has switched off). It reaches the desktop app only after :4410 restarts.
  - **The terminal is line-mode.** There is no pseudo-terminal (no native module): a line is edited in the browser and the shell runs it. Ctrl+C can't interrupt a command; on a busy shell it starts the shell again. Programs that want a real terminal (an editor, a pager, `claude`'s sign-in) don't work in it, so the Connect step still says to use the computer's own terminal for that (plan: *agent-host-terminal*).
  - ~~The browser is an iframe.~~ Since 2026-11-18 it is a real browser on the server's computer for an admin (above); anyone else still gets the framed page. The Connect step's key links still open the system browser (plan: *in-app-browser*).
  - **T3's page in the frame is T3's own UI**, not Foundation's style, and closing the tools pane drops it (plan: *t3-on-the-grid*). It frames as `localhost` when Foundation is on `localhost` so its sign-in cookie is same-site; from any other host it was not tried.
  - **Headless captures with a frame open show a black or white strip in the top bar's right half.** Read back from the page, those pixels are the paper; it is the capture, not the page (`11_browser.png`, `13_…`). Not seen in the app's own browser.
  - **The section council's ＋ mark floats above a plain silhouette** (the e2e server has no characters); with real characters it sits over a head.
  - **Which way "left of the profile" meant**: the orb is the foot's first thing, on the Settings icon's edge, and the avatar follows it, so the avatar itself is no longer on that edge.
  - **Not checked:** the desktop window, Rune, a real GPU frame trace with the new bar (`PERF_GPU=1 … frames`), a real voice.
- **The council on its section's edge, the guided agent setup and the chrome, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_council-setup-chrome/`, 14 shots; there is no NOTES.md there, this is the list).
  - **Claude can't be mounted from inside Foundation.** The ask was to mount Claude through the inbuilt terminal. Foundation has no terminal on the computer it runs on: a machine card's terminal is a separate computer and can't sign the host in. The third way (*Claude Code on this computer*) tells the person to use the computer's own terminal (plan: *agent-host-terminal*, Next).
  - **Links to key pages open in the system browser**, not in a browser inside Foundation (plan: *in-app-browser*).
  - **The Claude API-key way was never run with a real key.** The new T3 preset `claude` (id `claude-cloud`, cloud only) hands T3 the key as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; whether T3's Claude driver accepts it this way is unverified.
  - **The account way was built from Foundation's own T3 host.** The ask pointed at Vision's implementation; it was not read.
  - **The Council tab is not the agents' replies.** It shows what you said and each agent's live line; their full replies are in their sessions. What you said is kept for that window only: not across a reload, not shared with anyone (plan: *council-replies-in-panel*, Next).
  - **Voice in the council's line was not exercised in a browser test** (no microphone in headless).
  - **Offline is judged from the agents list only** (`/api/agents/available`: not ready, or gone). A dropped socket to the server doesn't mark the agents offline.
  - **A council standing over its neighbour can cover that neighbour's lower cards while it's open.** Meant: it stands over whatever is above its section. Close it (Esc) to reach them.
  - **A card's ✕ at 26 px has no test.** It was measured in a browser (a 10.5 px button, a 26.0 px press at the Character studio's fit); no e2e check holds it, and it was not tried under Rune or in the desktop window.
  - **What was run**: typecheck; `e2e/council.spec.ts` (8), `e2e/section-agents.spec.ts` (5), `e2e/today.spec.ts` and `e2e/one-interface.spec.ts` in Chromium on ports above 15000; day and night looked at. Nothing else: not the desktop window, not Rune, no real agent.
  - **The running server serves none of it until it restarts** (the desktop app's, on :4410; top of this file).
- **The pointer, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_pointer/`, whose `NOTES.md` says how each part was checked).
  - **Alt-wheel through a card** (2026-11-17) is checked in Chromium only: not on a Mac (⌥), a trackpad, Firefox or the desktop window; ⇧ sideways has a unit test and no browser check. The mark that teaches it is drawn in the zoomed world, so below about 0.6 it is too small to read.
  - **A card's ✕ was small when the card was: fixed 2026-11-17.** At a drawn scale of 0.3 a bar's ✕ was 7.8 px, with the drag handle all round it: a near miss picked the card up. No press on a character's ✕ was found to be swallowed (2026-11-16 and again 2026-11-17, real mouse, 1.0 / 0.6 / 0.3), so this was the likelier reason a card "couldn't be removed". The ✕ now keeps a 26 px press area on the screen at any size (measured in a browser; no e2e test yet, above). The bar's other buttons still scale with the card.
  - **Under Rune a note or an image is still grabbed from anywhere on it** (`apps/rune/src/view.rs`): the 2026-11-17 grab rule is the web renderer's only. Rune's e2e was not run against the new menu.
  - **`#/design` → Using a card**: its paragraph still describes ⌘/Ctrl-wheel scrolling a card, and its older "On call" image specimen has no grip (DesignPage.tsx was left to another session; the replacement sentence is in `docs/evidence/2026-11-17_pointer/NOTES.md`).
  - **Not run**: touch, the desktop window (WebView2), a Mac; a real video file was not played (the check reads the `autoplay` flag a tap sets); machine cards were not opened in the pointer check.
  - **The section menu's icons are mostly the glyphs the list had** (⤢ ← → ↑ ↓ ◫ ⬓ ◱ ◳); only Remove got a drawn bin. A disabled Remove (the last section) was not checked in the browser. No type-ahead; Tab walks the buttons in DOM order.
- **Sub-grids, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_sub-grids/`). Not run: the Rune renderer and the packaged desktop app.
  - **Sub-grids: a card edited offline can come back.** Extract deletes the moved cards from the parent's document. A device that was offline and changed one of them (moved it, renamed it) can bring it back onto the parent when it reconnects, so it would exist on both grids. Text typed into a moved note in the instant of the move can be lost. Not seen in tests; not guarded.
  - **Sub-grids: put back (2026-11-27)** is the undo, and is all-or-nothing: a grid holding a machine, a huddle or a pipeline stage is refused until they're taken off (they stay on the grid they were made on). An archived grid has no restore press yet (its row, history and files are kept). Card ids aren't checked against the parent's on the way back (they're random, so a clash isn't expected). Not run in e2e or the desktop app; checked by `subgrids.test.ts` and by hand in the browser (`docs/evidence/2026-11-27_sub-grid-put-back/`).
  - **Sub-grids: Vision hand-offs and running agent sessions** that named the section keep pointing at the parent grid; what they bring back lands there, not in the new grid.
  - **Sub-grids: a leftover card's section keeps the name** it had only while the card fills it; a person who renamed that section keeps their name.
  - **Rune**: ⤢ still opens the in-window level; the question isn't drawn.
  - **Sub-grids: the question can be off screen.** It sits at the section's top right (where the remove question does). Asked from the right-click menu low in a section taller than the window, it is above the view; the same is true of *Remove section…*.
  - **Preview cost**: every grid card on screen asks its outline every 4 s. Fine for a handful; a grid of many child cards would want one request or a change signal.
- **Machines: Add a desktop and the in-use ring, as fixed 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_machines-fixes/`).
  - **A server started from a checkout runs what it was started with.** The app updates itself; the server does not until someone restarts it. On 2026-11-17 that was why Add a desktop "didn't work", twice: the second time because the server had been started by the app's run before an update, and the switch offered no Stop for a server it hadn't started. Now the switch offers Restart and Stop for any Foundation server started its way (recognised by command line), and the desktop's well offers Restart the server when the route is missing; that half needs the next desktop release, which carries the switch's Rust. From 2026-11-27 the server says the code it runs (`GET /api/server` → `build`, its `git describe` read once at start, or `FOUNDATION_BUILD` where there is no git) and the server panel shows it as *Code*; when that is an earlier release, or the same release with fewer commits, than the app's own, the panel says so in yellow with **Restart it** (a server this app started or adopted) or *stop and start it where it runs*. Limits: it's seen only in the server panel (nothing pops up by itself); two builds on different branches with the same count since a release say nothing; the app's built-in server (no git) says nothing until CI sets `FOUNDATION_BUILD` (it doesn't yet); a server from before this change sends no `build`, so it says nothing either. Checked by `test/serverInfo.test.ts` and on `#/design` in day and night (`docs/evidence/2026-11-27_server-older-than-app/`); not yet seen in the desktop app against a really older server.
  - **A machine's window of its own was opened in Chromium, not in the Tauri window.** `e2e/machines-tools.spec.ts` opens `machine.html` as a tab; the desktop app opens it as a `WebviewWindow` (capability `core:webview:allow-create-webview-window`), which nobody has pressed yet. An app from before this falls back to `window.open`. The keyboard lock in its full screen is Chromium's (WebView2 should have it; not checked).
  - **A cluster's nodes run privileged.** k3s won't start without it on Kiri (`failed to evacuate root cgroup … read-only file system`, tried 2026-11-17). On a shared kernel a privileged machine can reach the kernel every machine uses; a cluster starts only on a person's press and its cards and Access say so. Kiri only so far: Docker Desktop not tried.
  - **A public link needs cloudflared on this computer**, which Foundation doesn't install (a person runs `winget install --id Cloudflare.cloudflared`). Checked with a stand-in that prints a link as cloudflared does; a real tunnel has not been opened.
  - **The Foundation link rewrites only HTML attributes.** An app that builds root-relative URLs in its own script (fetch("/api/…")) needs to be told its base (it gets X-Forwarded-Prefix). It reaches only people who can reach this Foundation; the desktop app's server is on 127.0.0.1 unless FOUNDATION_HOST says otherwise.
  - **A function's run is a person's press**: nothing runs it when new pictures arrive, and agents can't call one yet.
  - **The window's buttons on Today were checked by their CSS in the browser**, not in the desktop app's own window.
  - **What the machine copies reaches this computer's clipboard only while the desktop has the keys** (a browser lets only a focused page write it). Otherwise the card says the machine copied text and Copy puts it there. x11vnc sends the X selection, so selecting text in the machine copies it, as on Linux. Viewers get no clipboard (their VNC is view-only).
  - **A desktop installs on Ubuntu, Debian and Alpine only.** Fedora, Rocky, Arch and openSUSE, four of the Start picker's seven, are refused at once with the distro's name (tried on Kiri).
  - **A stalled install is only reported, not ended**: the card says how long the package manager has been quiet; a person presses Stop, or it is ended after 25 minutes.
  - **Stopping an install leaves zombies in a Kiri machine** (killed apt and dpkg processes that Kiri's init doesn't reap). They hold nothing and go when the machine restarts. Kiri's to fix.
  - **The in-use ring's edges are antialiased** when the card isn't drawn at a whole-pixel scale: 2 px wide to within about a tenth of a pixel, not pixel-sharp.
  - **The owner's server was not restarted and his machine was not touched.** The fix is verified on a server of this code with a machine of the same image, not on `box-a70f` (top of this file).
  - **A non-root image was not started on Kiri**: the words for an engine that ignores the install's request for root are tested on the stand-in only.
- **The character sheet, the expressions stage and the studio's nodes, as built 2026-11-17** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-17_character-studio/`).
  - **The animals' expression sheets were not generated** (nor anyone else's). The stage exists; each is one press, 9 credits. `node scripts/characters-expressions.mjs --set animals` lists them and the cost; `--press` with `FOUNDATION_SESSION` spends them.
  - **The expressions prompt has never met the real image model.** The eight-panel grid, the labels, the mesh form's "same place in every panel" and `aspect_ratio: "4:3"` on Meshy's `image-to-image` are all unverified. The first real press should be looked at before the rest are spent.
  - **Mesh avatars still have no drawn variants.** The expressions sheet is drawn to be their source (BLINK, TALKING), but nothing cuts or registers them, and mesh-avatar-studio's rig format hasn't been checked for taking them.
  - **The live studio hasn't been upgraded.** It happens the next time someone opens the studio (⌘K) on a server running this code: the flow card becomes the Inspiration node, eight more nodes and nine wires per set are added, each set's column grows to 2,060 px and the pipeline row to 1,490 px. Tested on the server; not watched in a browser on a studio with twenty-two characters.
  - **A redrawn expressions sheet doesn't redraw the views.** By design (it clears nothing); a portrait drawn before it won't match it until *Views* is pressed again.
  - **The studio grid has 27 more cards and 27 wires.** Its frame rate wasn't measured.
  - **Two cards of the same picture in an Inspiration section show as two picks that toggle together** on a character card (they are one file). Seen while testing; there before this work.
  - **`council.spec.ts` "the card plays it live" failed twice in about ten runs** when run after `character-studio.spec.ts` on one server (the stage stayed a still). The spec now points at the stage first; the cause wasn't established.
  - **A viewer on the pipeline's nodes** (no Generate, no ＋ New) is written and was not checked in a browser; the card's viewer state is on `#/design`.
- **Removing a character, as built 2026-11-16** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-16_card-chrome/`): what was reproduced and fixed is that a removed card's character stayed on every council until a reload (nothing told the browsers). A ✕ that does nothing was not reproduced in Chromium; if it happens again, note where it was pressed. A removed character's row and files stay and there is no way in the app to bring one back. 2026-11-17: looked for again at three drawn scales and still not reproduced; the ✕ was 7.8 px on a card drawn at 30 %, and now keeps a 26 px press (above).
- **A card's bar slides a page, a terminal or a file down** rather than resizing it, so the bottom 38 px of those cards are under the card's edge while the pointer is on them (a terminal's prompt line, often). Notes, lists and characters get shorter instead and lose nothing.
- **Today, the band, adding agents and the pipeline, as built 2026-11-16** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-16_today-council-agents/`).
  - **Signing an agent in is not done inside Foundation.** The add flow's Connect step says how (a terminal on the computer: `codex login`, `claude`…) and waits; there is no built-in VM with a browser for it, and no browser tool. A machine's desktop exists (2026-11-15) but has no browser in it. Since 2026-11-17 Connect walks through it as numbered steps with the button, link or command for each; the signing in itself is still done in T3 or in the computer's own terminal (above).
  - **The agents panel is not Claude Code's set of panes.** Three faces: at work, add an agent, background. An agent's session has no changes, files, terminal or browser pane of its own, and "keep the computer awake" isn't there.
  - **T3 "mini" is a list.** Background shows T3's sessions (title, agent, model, state) with Open and Stop, and Open T3 puts the whole of T3 in a browser tab. T3's own interface isn't redrawn in Foundation, and a session can't be read or continued from the panel.
  - **Only Claude was added end to end**, with the script runtime. ChatGPT, Gemini, Kimi, DeepSeek and a local model were opened as far as *this computer's agents are off* in the checks; starting T3, adding a key, and the named session were not run.
  - **A named session costs a turn.** Adding one of this computer's agents with the box ticked sends it a first message ("read the grid, say what you see, wait"), on that agent's plan or key. It's a person's press and the box says so, but it isn't free.
  - **A section's band misplaced that section's wires while it was open** (they were drawn 236 px above their ports): **gone 2026-11-17**, with the band. A section's council now stands over its top edge and moves nothing in the section.
  - **A section's council is in the grid's space** (the band of 2026-11-16, and the figures on its edge since 2026-11-17). It scales with the view: at overview zoom it's too small to read (the grid's council is the one for that).
  - **Pictures, plans and folds are on Today only.** The session column (✦ Ask) and a council's replies still draw text as before.
  - **The badge counts, it doesn't notify.** It's what Today's Todos hold plus a ready update, asked once a minute while the window is in front; nothing is pushed, and nothing marks an item as seen.
  - **White over the Shiba relies on the shade.** One 36 % shade lies over the whole room so white reads on its lightest mood; the Shiba is dimmer for it by day.
  - **An older studio's upgrade moves sections down a row** and adds three; cards a person had laid across rows in their own arrangement are not re-flowed. On this computer the live studio gets its pipeline row the first time the Character studio is opened after the server restarts.
  - **Approval is not retroactive.** Characters from before 2026-11-16 (and the starter set) read as approved; characters agents add (`add_character`) are pending like a person's.
- **Machines' typing, desktop, agents and pipes, as built 2026-11-15** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-15_machines-desktop/`, whose `NOTES.md` says how each was checked on Kiri 0.1.18).
  - **Ctrl+C in an Alpine machine on Kiri does nothing.** Kiri 0.1.18 starts a machine's processes with SIGINT blocked; Foundation clears that through perl or python3, and Alpine's base image has neither. The terminal says so when it opens; `apk add perl` and reopening it fixes it. Ubuntu and Debian are fine. Not looked at: Fedora, Arch, Rocky, openSUSE (they need python3 or perl in the image). Kiri's to fix.
  - **Kiri leaves an exec's stream open after the program exits.** Foundation now asks the engine whether the exec still runs (a request a few times a second at first, then every 700 ms, for as long as a command runs). Kiri's to fix.
  - **In a browser tab, Ctrl+W, Ctrl+T and Ctrl+N are the browser's**, whatever has focus: no page can take them. In the terminal they reach the shell in the checks only because Playwright's keys don't pass through the browser's own shortcuts. **Not verified in the packaged desktop app** (WebView2 has no tabs, so they should reach the shell; nobody pressed them there). The Keyboard Lock API in full screen would give them to the shell in a browser; not done.
  - **The desktop was opened in the packaged app's transport, not in its window.** The production build served from another origin with a bearer and socket tickets shows and uses a desktop (`e2e/machines-fixes.spec.ts`, 2026-11-17), and the installed app serves `desktop.html` and its script. Nobody has pressed it inside the Tauri window (WebView2).
  - **Not tried on Docker, macOS or Linux hosts.** The desktop and pipes use only the Engine API calls the terminal already uses (exec, a hijacked stream), and the stand-in tests are Docker-shaped, but no real Docker ran them: this computer's Docker pipe is Kiri.
  - **No live model has driven a desktop.** The checks drive the tools with an agent's token (REST and MCP) and with the crew's script runtime (a fixed playbook, no model). The API runtime's image block (`agent.ts`) is typed and unexercised; a subscription run goes through MCP, whose picture was checked at the door, not inside Claude Code.
  - **The desktop is 1280×800, fixed**, with no clipboard between it and the board, no sound, and no browser inside it (Ubuntu's Firefox is a snap). It is ~250 packages (1 to 3 minutes on this connection, about 700 MB of the machine's disk) and its processes keep running in the machine until it stops (they start again when someone opens the Desktop view).
  - **One desktop, shared.** A person and an agent can both act on it at once; nothing takes turns between them (agents take turns among themselves).
  - **The leave agents have doesn't expire.** It lasts until someone presses Stop or the machine is removed, across the machine's stops and restarts.
  - **A busy agent fills the activity.** Every action, a look included, is a line, and the board keeps the last 400.
  - **Wires to and from notes and tables aren't drawn.** They carry values (the server reads and writes those ends) and are listed, made and removed on the machine's strip, but the wire layer draws card-to-card wires only. `in` takes one source (the first wire).
  - **Pipes move only while watched**: the machine's card on someone's screen, or the machine wired and the board open somewhere. `in` is capped at 64 KB and `out` at 256 KB (the strip says so when cut). `out` is polled every 2 s (one exec each time).
  - **A table cell wired in is the cell as typed** (a formula arrives as its text, not its value): the server doesn't compute tables.
  - **The council doesn't hand a machine to an agent by itself.** An agent on a section's council can use that section's machine's desktop once a person has pressed Let agents use this desktop on the card; the council has no button of its own for it, and no real model has done it.
- **One interface, as built 2026-11-15** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-15_one-interface/`).
  - **A server that was already running has none of it.** The new routes (`/api/prefs`, `/api/system-grids`, `/api/agents/available`, `/api/boards/:id/agents`, `…/outline`, a grid's `parent`) and the `agents` map are the server's. This computer's Foundation server (the desktop app's, from this checkout, on :4410) was left running through the work, so it serves the old code until it's restarted: until then the new client shows Settings → Plan / Design as an error toast and the council can't add an agent.
  - **Nothing was checked with a real model or a real T3 agent.** The council's line reaches the script runtime in the checks. Sending to one of this computer's agents goes through the same `/api/t3host/threads` the agents pane uses, which SPEC already lists as unproven with a real thread.
  - **The council has no conversation.** A line starts a new ask for each agent it goes to; what comes back is the agent's work on the grid and its lines in the agent's pane (*Lately, here*), not a thread in the council. The session column (✦ Ask) still holds the server's own agent's conversations. Since 2026-11-17 the agents panel's Council tab lists what you said and each agent's live line; it still doesn't show what they answered (above).
  - **An assignment is a seat, not a brief.** It carries who, by whom and what the agent is; no standing instructions, no model choice, no budget (plan: *posted-agents* is now about those).
  - **What an assigned agent is, is as it was when assigned.** If the agent is later removed from this computer or re-pointed (a Kimi moved from cloud to local), its seat keeps the old words until it's taken off and added again; speaking to it then fails in a toast.
  - **Whose model it is, is a monogram.** The marks are drawn here in the ink (Claude's rays, a knot for ChatGPT, K, a whale's back…), not the vendors' logos; a provider nobody listed is the agent's spark.
  - **The design language was grouped, not redrawn.** Each chapter's card draws the old page's sections for that chapter, at the card's width. Chapters are taller than their section (1680 px), so they scroll inside their card; a few specimens made for a 1,100 px page are tight at 1,240. "Cleaned up" so far means the twelve chapters and their order.
  - **Foundation's own grids can be changed by any member.** The plan's and the design language's cards can be moved or removed like any card; nothing puts them back (delete the board's row and it is laid out again).
  - **The kanban card is the plan only.** `panel: "kanban"` with another `source` says it has nothing to show; a kanban of a grid's own cards isn't built.
  - **A grid's old folder is ignored, not converted.** `boards.folder` stays in the database and nothing shows it; this server had no folders, so none were turned into parent grids.
  - **Live character cards were counted in a headless browser only.** There, software rendering shed the budget to three of thirteen; on this computer's GPU the number wasn't measured.
  - **A still's ground is cut in the browser** (384 px, flood from the border at an RGB distance of 30): a pale figure on a pale ground, or a ground that isn't flat, is left as it is, so its silhouette is a box.
  - **Rune**: the new cards (a grid inside this one, kanban, design) and the port labels are the web's; none was opened under the Rune renderer.
  - **Not rerun on this computer's GPU**: the frame-time checks (`PERF_GPU=1 … frames`).
- **The council and the Character studio, as built 2026-11-14** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-14_council-characters/`).
  - **Not seen with a real agent turn.** Seats, colours and moods were checked with the script runtime in two-browser-style e2e and with the bench on real characters; a real Claude Code turn hasn't sat in the council yet.
  - **The mesh stage is not a button.** A mesh avatar's rig is coordinates read off zoomed grids by an agent with vision, then Python (uv, OpenCV) and Chromium to build and review it: tens of minutes of mesh-avatar-studio work per avatar, done in a Claude Code session and brought in as a `.mavatar` (`docs/characters.md`). All five of the anime set were done that way on 2026-11-14 (their projects are in `C:\Dev\mesh-avatar-studio\projects`, outside this repo); the next character someone adds waits for a session. Making this an agent job on the board is on the plan (*mesh-stage-on-the-board*).
  - **The mesh avatars have no drawn variants.** Their closed eyes and open mouths are the mesh deforming, not drawings: faint crease lines or a soft patch stay above a closed eye, and expressions change the brows and lids, not the drawing. mesh-avatar-studio's drawn variants need an image model that edits inside a mask; Meshy's doesn't take one. Each was traced in one pass and judged on its rendered poses, not tuned in the editor. Earrings that aren't red move with the head instead of swinging (the builder only isolates red ones: Aoi's swing, Hana's and Midori's don't). Rin's glasses are part of her face: each eye's region was kept inside its lens so a blink leaves the frames alone, and the frames don't shift when her head turns. Hana and Midori keep small off-white wedges between locks of hair that the cut-out didn't reach.
  - **A portrait's background is cut away by hand too.** Asking Meshy for a clear background (`remove_background`) took Sora's white hair and white blouse with it (tried 2026-11-14), so the portrait is drawn on a flat mid-grey ground instead and cut out when the avatar is built (a flood fill of that grey, from the border and from enclosed gaps). Aoi's, Hana's, Midori's and Rin's portraits predate that and are on off-white, which cut cleanly only because none of them is white at the edges.
  - **A mesh avatar is its portrait.** Head to toe, an anime character is its sheet standing still (it breathes in CSS). mesh-avatar's full-body rig (legs, arms, cloth) is experimental and wasn't tried.
  - **3D moods are the body only.** Meshy's skeleton has no face, finger or cloth bones: a 3D character can't blink, and its expression never changes. "Working" and "talking" are two talking gestures; the library has no typing or writing clip that faces the camera. A beat is cut at its hold time, not at the clip's end.
  - **Moods are read from words.** Thinking or working is decided by how the cursor's line starts (read, look, search, find … → thinking). There is no speech: "talking" only means the agent is asking its person to come and look.
  - **Meshy's rigging is for bipeds.** Every character here was drawn standing on two legs in an A-pose for that reason; a four-legged animal needs its own rig (WildChild built those in Blender).
  - **Costs are a table in the code** (`stageCost`), from Meshy's pricing on 2026-11-14; if Meshy changes its prices the buttons are wrong until it's edited. What a stage really cost is what Meshy reported, shown after. The balance is read at most every 30 s.
  - **The starter set is a copy, not a history.** Its characters arrive with every stage marked done but no Meshy tasks behind them, no bare model and no lineage: *Again* on a starter's skeleton or moods is refused ("Make the model first") until its model is made again here, which draws a new character. Each starter character is brought in once per install (`character_starter`, keyed by form, set and name): at boot the server brings in the ones this install hasn't had, making the studio for them if there isn't one (2026-11-17), so a fresh install's council has them without anyone opening the studio and an install whose studio came from an older set gets the newer ones. A studio from before that counts what it holds (retired cards too) as had, and deleting a starter's card doesn't bring it back. A character re-exported with new files (a re-rig, an expressions sheet) doesn't replace the copy an install already has. Foundation's avatars (§0.12) upload to that set from the studio: a character main stops listing is archived on each install at its next boot, and one main lists again comes back; an install that skips from a build before 2026-11-17's uploader straight to a set that dropped a character doesn't withdraw it (its rows weren't marked as listed yet). An upload pushes to main, which publishes a desktop release: the timer only pushes when the set changed, but a day's timer with a changed set is a release a day. Uploaded pictures are PNG as Meshy drew them (the export script made JPEGs): a new character adds a few MB. The wire router gives a section's wires no lanes, so the three into the uploader share the gutter at its edge for their last stretch (they still land apart). It adds 41 MB to the repo and the installer. The inspiration pictures are not in it: they're other people's work, and the repo is public.
  - **Doing a stage again doesn't ask.** *Again · 9* on a finished sheet clears the views, model and clips after it at once (the files stay in the archive; the card forgets them).
  - **Meshy forgets tasks.** The skeleton and the clips are made from Meshy's own copy of the model, by task id. After Meshy's retention runs out those presses fail ("Meshy no longer has this task") and the model has to be made again.
  - **Characters are the company's, not a board's.** Any signed-in person can read a character's files by its id (`/api/characters/:id/files/:role`), whoever may see the studio board. Deleting a character's card takes it off the council; its files stay archived.
  - **Weight.** A character's clips are a 3.5 MB GLB (30,000 triangles, a 2K texture), fetched when a council first shows it and kept for the page's life; nothing is compressed or simplified for a 124 px seat. Frame time with six seats wasn't measured (`PERF_GPU … frames` has no council case); the e2e runs on a software renderer.
  - **Under Rune** a section's toolbar has no council mark of its own (its icons are drawn in Rust): its ✦ opens the section's council since 2026-11-15, as do the menu, ⌘K and the top bar. The cards and the council are web islands and DOM, so they work over either renderer.
  - **On this machine** the studio board was made by a dev-login "Josh Leong" (`josh.leong@gmail.com`), not the Flowform account `josh@normallab.co`: the dev login refuses that address (it isn't on the allowlist). Its activity is signed by that name.

- **The checkout the switch starts by default (`C:\Dev\Foundation`) was on a branch behind `main`** (2026-11-13): its server lacked `/api/today`, so the installed app (built from `main`) showed Today as offline ("not found") against it. Resolved the same day by merging everyone's work into `main` and `main` back into that branch; the server a checkout runs is still whatever that checkout has, so keep it in step with `main`. Also seen once that day: two `vision.test.ts` cases failed in a full `npm test` on Node 26 and passed alone and in two full server runs (192/192): a timing flake under load.

- **Agents in the section and the text box, as built 2026-11-13.** The marks at rest (✦ Agents, ⤢) sit at the header's right end, so **on a section wider than the window they are off screen** until you pan (the tab above the section follows the view; the chip doesn't); the panel itself is always kept on the open paper. **Under Rune** the header is Rune's drawing and has no chip or ⤢ at rest (its toolbar's ✦ and the menu open the panel, and a text box and the agents card are web islands, unchecked there in a browser); the parity ledger says so. **The text box** rides the browser's own editing (`contenteditable` and `execCommand`): it keeps what the note's markdown can hold, so a list inside a list comes back flat, a line break inside a paragraph becomes a new paragraph, pasted text arrives as plain words, and there is no font, size, colour or alignment. Two people in **one paragraph** at once merge through the note's text (the smallest edit of the whole markdown), which is coarser than the markdown editor's per-keystroke binding: fine across paragraphs, and in one line the later writer's caret can land a character off. ⌘Z after someone else's edit lands undoes less than you'd expect (the surface was redrawn). *Made here by agents* reads the board's activity by the card each line names, so a line about a card that was since removed drops out, and its *just now* is only refreshed when the panel next draws. An ask from the panel reaches the agent as text (*In the “Name” section: …*), not as a section id, so two sections with one name are ambiguous to it. The agents card is one to a section and shows that section only; on a grid section it sits in the first inner section and shows that one.

- **Rune's motion rules and the new icon, as built 2026-11-19** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-19_rune-motion-icon/`).
  - **The rules were checked by what each board was told, not by measuring pixels in flight.** The e2e reads the word and the rule each scene went to Rune with, and one screenshot 300 ms into a pane opening shows the cut already at the pane's edge. No frame-by-frame capture proves nothing eases.
  - **Rune now differs from the web renderer in two places on purpose**: the web settles a section's place over 0.5 s when it arrives from the store and eases the floor's edge with the pane; Rune lands both. The parity ledger says so.
  - **A settle is by timing**: the board that arrives within 250 ms of a let-go or a tool press eases. Someone else's edit landing in that window eases with it.
  - **The two Rune checks that failed on 2026-11-19, looked into the same day.** *A link in a note*, two causes, both fixed. (1) The web note has shown a link's real host beside it since the security pass (`link ↗ foundation.invalid`), which wraps the link, and Rune drew no host, so the check pressed where no link was. Rune now draws it (`apps/rune/src/view.rs` `host_mark`, `host_of`: dim, in the code face, held to the link so they wrap together; nothing when the link's words already say the host; the name before an `@` is never taken for the host), and the two notes differ by 3.5/255 where they differed by 6.4. (2) **A press on a link's letters never reached Rune.** Rune mirrors what it draws into the page for screen readers (`data-rune-a11y`), and on a canvas that fills the viewport it leaves each link's `<a href target="_blank">` taking the pointer, over the letters and above the canvas. So the browser opened the address itself, round Foundation's own handling of a link (http(s) only, `openExternal`), and Rune reported nothing; only the blank top and bottom rows of the line reached the canvas. `rune.css` now keeps the pointer off the whole mirror; `e2e/rune.spec.ts` *a press on a note's link* holds it. **Not checked:** the desktop window (where a `_blank` anchor may have done nothing at all), a screen reader's own activation of a mirrored link (it still goes to the browser's `<a>`, not through `openExternal`), a `mailto:` link. *A section dragged onto another's cell*: failed twice, then passed eight times running on the same code, alone and in the full suite; Rune reported the drag and the drop at the right cell each time it was watched. Cause not found (a cancelled pointer in the headed window is a guess); nothing was changed for it.
  - **The icon's tile colour is a choice of mine**: night navy `#111a2c`, kept from the icon before it. One line in `assets/foundation-icon.svg`, then regenerate.
  - **Not checked:** the icon in a real taskbar, dock or installer (the PNG, ICO and ICNS were generated and looked at as pictures; the desktop app wasn't rebuilt); the extension's and the Slack agent's icons are their own and unchanged; the mark at 13 px in the app's chrome (the 52-unit gap is under a pixel there); Rune in the desktop window; `PERF_GPU=1 … frames`.
- **The Rune renderer, as built 2026-11-13** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-12_rune/`, `2026-11-13_rune-frames/`; off by default). Settings → Renderer lists what's missing row by row. **It is slower than the web renderer where it draws the most itself.** On 300 markdown notes the web renderer holds 60 fps and Rune runs long frames on a third to a half of them (p95 83 to 150 ms panning, pinching or dragging; above). What a profile of the fitted pan showed: Rune sets every glyph of every card again on each frame it draws, on or off screen (no culling here, and `El::cache` on a card's content changed nothing); it mirrors the text to a hidden DOM tree for screen readers and resyncs it on each of those frames (12 % of the time, in its own `syncA11y`); and **its view runs again on about four frames in ten of a pan**, and on every grain tick, though only live values moved (190 to 206 runs for 180 wheel notches; not our scene, which isn't resent, nor the wheel, the pointer, the paper's slots, a card's chrome or markdown, each ruled out in turn; it looks like one run for each live value set from outside a handler). Markdown makes each of those runs dearer (the same pan with plain text notes ran at about twice the frame rate). Building for speed rather than size (`opt-level = 3`) took a seventh off and made the wasm smaller. Next: find what commits (Rune's `CommitWhy`), cull or cache off-screen cards, and ask Rune for a cheaper mirror. The other gaps: **islands are always over what Rune draws** (a note can't float over an app or a map), their use (Ctrl+click) and full screen are unchecked under Rune, and a section's fit lands at once for them where the web's glides; **a table's first press picks it up, not a cell**, and at rest it has no wire handle and shows a cell's image or link as its text; **a section's tools hide while the pointer is on one of its cards** (the web's stay), have no roll-over labels, and an app section lacks its app's buttons; a dragged section doesn't ease into place, and dragging past the board's edge is unchecked in a browser; a grid section shows its count, not its miniature; an empty section has no welcome, no file drops, no + in the gaps and no gutters; **cards can't be carried** to another section or tab; Rune's web layer swallows a right-click unless one of its elements claims it, so every right-click on the board is claimed (a section opens the menu; elsewhere nothing opens, where the web has an empty cell's menu); **a note's markdown** has smaller bullets than the web's, a task's box that doesn't tick, IBM Plex Mono for code where the web has the system's code face, and tables and pictures unchecked; writing a note has no shared carets (the field sends its whole text on each keystroke, so two people typing in one note at once through Rune can overwrite each other: use the web renderer to write together). **The paper is drawn twice**: Rune's canvas is opaque, so it draws the paper itself and the WebGL paper still runs under it unseen; a grid section's body gets the dots where the web leaves it plain. **Type**: Cyrillic and Vietnamese fall back to Inter (the web bundle has them; Rune keeps one face per family, weight and style, so each script needs its own family name), the first moments of a load are Inter until the face arrives, and Rune still asks for `Inter-*.ttf` beside its module, which isn't there. `apps/rune/fonts` is a copy: run `scripts/rune-fonts.py` when `@fontsource-variable/hanken-grotesk` is bumped. **The whole board is sent as JSON on every store change** (coalesced per frame), and each one runs Rune's view. The night fade is Rune's own colour spring between three samples, not the tokens' 1.4 s mix. The mark turns green when the module has mounted, a moment before the GPU's first frame. **The desktop app with Rune on is unchecked**: `build:desktop` now runs `rune:build` (`dist/rune/` checked here), but no installed app has been run with it, on Windows, macOS or Linux; on a machine without the Rust toolchain the build says so and that app has no Rune. Building needs rustup, the pinned toolchain and `wasm-bindgen-cli 0.2.129` (both installed on this PC 2026-11-12). Rune is pre-release and takes no pull requests: gaps in Rune itself (a see-through web canvas, the web host's cursors, the view running on live-only frames) go to its issues.
- **Desktop updates, as they stand before anyone else installs it** (2026-11-12). The loop is built and was run end to end on a real install against a local feed (2026-11-08). From 2026-11-13 CI publishes a release on every push to `main` (§3); until a person installs one, no copy has taken an update from GitHub itself. A failed CI run publishes nothing and the next push tries again; its version numbers skip, which is harmless. Compared with Flowform's client (read from the installed 1.x binary's own strings, not its source, which isn't on this machine): Flowform asks its own server for the feed (`/update/latest`, behind its sign-in), checks **at startup before the window is usable** and installs without asking (`--skip-startup-update` after the hand-off), and its server refuses clients under a minimum. Foundation asks a public GitHub release, checks 8 s after launch and every 6 h, and **never installs without a press** (an invariant here: nothing that runs third-party code starts without a person), so an ordinary update can be put off for ever with Later or Skip; the minimum (`FOUNDATION_MIN_CLIENT`) is the one way to insist. Still open: a Mac taking an update from GitHub is unrun (CI builds Apple silicon from 2026-11-20, ad-hoc signed, so its first install needs Open Anyway); Linux is unbuilt; the MSI path is unrun (the feed prefers NSIS); the Windows installer isn't code-signed (SmartScreen will warn on first install); the feed's address is baked into each build, so moving it needs one release that still answers at the old address.
- **A result in the hand, as built 2026-11-12**: the held card is drawn at a fixed size, not the size the card will land at, so a big image settles into a card several times larger; a waiting card is pinned by its first card's spot only; a stack waits as a whole for its slowest file before any of it is dealt. Dropping onto an empty cell or past the board's edge (which a carried card can do) isn't offered from search: it lands in the section under it or not at all. The clipboard is per device, not per person across devices.
- **Sections in sections, as built 2026-11-11** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-11_grids-in-sections/`). Wires are drawn only between cards in the grid you're looking at: a wire to a card inside a miniature (or in another level) doesn't show. Agents' cursors and `bring_view` aim with the board's own tracks, so they point at the wrong place for a section inside another; `arrange_section` and `lay_out` work on any section but agents don't yet open levels. A miniature draws its cards live (a page or machine in it still runs when large enough); a big inner grid could cost frames on a busy board (not traced with `PERF_GPU`). Presentation steps through the sections of the grid you're in, not into nested ones. Nothing collapses a grid section back into a section of cards when only one inner section is left. The opened view's sections never reach the server until something changes, by design, so a second person sees a section turn into a grid only once it's split or added to.
- **Carrying cards, as built 2026-11-10** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-10_carry/`): checked in a browser with synthetic and real pointer drags, not on a trackpad and not in the Playwright suite. The carried copy is a DOM clone: a map, a CAD still turning, a terminal or a live page shows as it last drew (canvas and iframes don't clone), which is fine for a 300 ms carry but reads as blank for a map. A board can be dropped on only while it has a tab or the spaces pane is open. A table's formulas that name tables on the board it left stop resolving there; a map whose table stays behind loses its source. Night's data-viz slots and wire type tones (json blue, data green…) are unchanged: they're the work's colours, not chrome; say if they should go grey too.
- **Spaces, as built 2026-11-10** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-10_spaces/`): checked in a browser against a test server, day and night; the Playwright e2e suite passes on the new shell (2026-11-10: 40 passed, 2 skipped: the GPU frames check, which needs `PERF_GPU=1`, and a drop that had no empty cell). **Not re-run:** the GPU frames check and the desktop window. Known gaps: a presentation is a seeded grid, not a canvas of its own; a web page space shows nothing (and says nothing) for a site that forbids framing; a new page's address is asked with the browser's own prompt; folders can't be renamed or removed in one step and spaces can't be deleted; agent sessions are still listed per space, so the column's history changes when you change tabs.

- **The frameless desktop window is checked on Windows only** (2026-11-10). Josh ran it (`tauri dev`): moving it by the bar, minimize, maximize and close, and resizing by the edges all work. A Mac (overlay title bar, the lights at the left, the bar starting 84 px in) and Linux have not been opened. If moving or closing fails there, the window's frame is set in one place: `decorations(…)` / `title_bar_style(…)` in `apps/client/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`.
- **`[::1]:1430` can refuse a bind on this Windows machine with nothing listening on it** (2026-11-10): `npm run desktop` then fails with *Port 1430 is already in use*. Until it clears (a reboot), start the window on another port: `FOUNDATION_CLIENT_PORT=15430 npx tauri dev --config '{"build":{"devUrl":"http://localhost:15430"}}'` in `apps/client`, with the server running.
- **Middle and right pan stop at an embedded page in use**: a press inside a live iframe that's in use (⌘/Ctrl-clicked) or full screen belongs to that page, so the grid doesn't see it. At rest a frame takes no pointer, so they pan over it.
- **Using a card, as built 2026-11-11** (`docs/evidence/2026-11-11_heavy-board/`): checked in Chromium by `e2e/card-use.spec.ts` and on the heavy bench, not yet in the packaged desktop window, on a Mac (⌘-click, ⌘↵) or with a trackpad. A list (Slack, a huddle's lines) and a terminal's scrollback now need ⌘/Ctrl-click before the wheel scrolls them; typing into a terminal or clicking a Slack channel still works without it. Tables have no full screen yet. Tooltips and menus drawn outside a full-screen card don't show over it. An app in use still gets ⌘/Ctrl-wheel itself (the browser may zoom the page inside it).
- **Picture levels (upgraded 2026-11-21):** JPEG and PNG images now generate quantized server tiers (`64, 256, 512, 1024, 2048` px, `ETag`/`304`, disk LRU cache outside `data/files/`) and every client surface (grid cards, ChatForm pictures + Lightbox, file cards, search tiles, clipboard, collections, characters) shares one ref-counted L1 RAM + L2 Cache Storage cache (`foundation-image-cache-v2`) across cookie and bearer transports, with `98.4%` measured cold-load byte reduction and off-screen VRAM shedding (`docs/evidence/2026-11-21_images-hosted-chat/`). WebP and AVIF originals still fall back cleanly to the client Web Worker (`imageLodWorker.ts`) for local tier generation; heavy GPU frame traces (`PERF_GPU=1`) were not re-run in this slice.
- **Calm mounting delays a page** by about a quarter of a second after the camera stops, and pages that come on screen together start one after another (≤ ~120 ms apart).

- **"Nothing connects" was the server not running** (2026-11-09, Josh: "several fetch issues with connecting things to claude, running the agents, and … data sources"). On this Windows machine the system Node is 22.13.1, whose SQLite has no FTS5: the server died at boot with `no such module: fts5`, the launcher went with it, and every request the app made then failed to fetch. The installed desktop app has the same symptom for another reason: **it doesn't start a server**; it expects one at http://localhost:4410. With the server up on Node 22.22 everything asked about worked here: an agent turn on the subscription ran (`run` → `crew` → text → `done`), `POST /api/agent/token` answered, connectors answered (offline ones saying why: the sibling apps weren't running). Fixed: the launcher checks Node before starting anything and prints the fix; the server's own error says the same; the client says *Can't reach the Foundation server (host)…* instead of the browser's "Failed to fetch", for requests, agent turns and a proxy's 500. **Still open:** Node on this machine has to be upgraded by a person (§6 #0); ~~the packaged app should start (or bundle) its server~~ (it carries one since 2026-11-09, started from the server switch: §3 Desktop); `node-check.mjs` has no test (its message was read from the launcher on the system Node); the client's message was seen from `reach` against a dead port in the browser, not with the whole app's server down.
- **Flowform rooms: what doesn't come across** (2026-11-11). A 3D model card Flowform names no file for, and video notes, stay notes naming them (Flowform's tools give no file for them). Flowform's older table kind (`grid`) returns no rows through its tools. Directive cards (an agent instruction on a board, e.g. *LessCAD*) aren't carried. A room on Flowform's older grid layout can't be read (`room_catalog_v2` says `legacy`) and is refused with that said. A re-read after the backup import treats the importer's cards as edited here (its ledger hashes differ), so it leaves them. A file Flowform wouldn't give isn't asked for again until the server restarts. Files download one at a time; a big room's first read takes a while (Engineering Room has 265). Downloads declare Flowform client 1.9.0; when Flowform raises its minimum they stop with that said, until `FLOWFORM_CLIENT_VERSION` is raised. Link sign-ins in flight live in memory. Fixed on the way: a card whose kind changed (a note that became an image) showed twice until a reload (`sync/board.ts` `pullObjects`).
- **Places are folders, not screen control** (2026-11-09). "Computer use" was built as Foundation reading approved folders on the server's computer. It doesn't see the screen or drive other apps. Approval is server-wide (one list, admins only), not per board or per person: anyone signed in can browse an approved place, and anyone who can edit a board can bring a file onto it. The path is typed (no folder picker in the desktop app yet). The deny-list of secret names is by name only: a key saved as `notes.txt` in an approved folder is readable. A file is read whole into memory to be brought (512 MB cap). On a hosted server, places would be the host's folders, which is a different question (as machines are). `#/design` doesn't show the browser or the Places tab yet (DESIGN.md §5 describes them).
- **The floor's edge is a clip now, not a mask** (2026-11-09). The 56 px fade is gone; `e2e/frames.spec.ts` (`PERF_GPU=1`) and the rest of the e2e suite haven't been re-run since, so the pane-opening numbers in §5 are from the mask. A clip should cost less than a full-screen mask; that's unmeasured. Cards cut by a pane's edge show a hard edge mid-card, by design.
- **Two people editing the same cell** still resolve last-writer-wins for that cell (Yjs picks one). Different cells merge.
- **A card added into a section that hosts an app** used to land behind the app. Fixed for agents (`cardSection`, tools.ts) and, since 2026-11-07, for people: every client add path makes the app a card in the flow beside the new one (`unhostFor` in store.ts, `test/hosting.test.ts`). The app stays a card until someone chooses "Let it fill the section" again.
- **Roles are per board or per account, with no per-section or per-card permissions.** Admins are global (`FOUNDATION_ADMIN_EMAILS`, or flowform admins). A viewer can still start agent sessions (read-only) and join huddles. A viewer's presence (cursor) still shows to others.
- **Offline is per board and per device.** Only boards opened on this device are kept. The copy isn't encrypted at rest (sign-out deletes it). Card positions and other per-card fields are last-writer-wins when two devices edited the same card offline (notes and cells merge).
- **A build from before epochs can read but not write** until it reloads (by design, so an old open tab can't pour a stale board into a reset one).
- **The desktop app's own server** (2026-11-09, Desktop in §3). What's open: **(1)** the NSIS installer was run on the machine it was built on, over an installed 0.3.0 (whose page kept a session for :4410; people sign in once more on :31441, seen); the MSI, a machine that has never had Foundation or Node, and the updater replacing a running `node.exe` are unchecked; nothing is signed, and an unsigned `node.exe` inside an unsigned installer may draw SmartScreen or antivirus attention. **(2)** Whether a dev server on :4410 is used is decided at launch: the same installed app shows the dev server's data when `npm run dev` is running and its own otherwise (two servers, two sets of boards; the sign-in page's Server line says which). **(3)** If :31441 is held by another program the server moves to the next free port; the session is kept per server address, so that launch asks for sign-in again. **(4)** The server's settings come from `.env` in the app's folder; there's no screen for them, and no way in the app to read `logs/server.log`. **(5)** The installed app has no `docs/plan.json` (Settings → Plan is empty) and no `scripts/plan.mjs` (plan sync is refused). **(6)** Agents need `claude` on the person's PATH, as before; the app doesn't carry it. **(7)** The installer is 23 MB larger for Node. **(8)** macOS and Linux builds of this haven't been made; the kill-on-close job is Windows only (elsewhere the stdin pipe and the quit handler do it). **(9)** The window waits for the server before it opens (0.3 s here; up to 25 s on a machine where it never answers, then the message).
- **Sockets and the bearer transport** (2026-11-08). Same-origin pages use the cookie, unchanged. A client on another origin holds an `app` bearer (localStorage, keyed by the server's origin; sign-out drops it there and on the server) and opens each socket with a one-time ticket: hashed, 30 s, deleted as it's read, valid only for the socket path it was issued for and only while its session lives. The ticket is the one credential that goes in a URL. Open: the bearer is readable by script on the app's page (a cookie isn't); files on another origin load as blobs (a video downloads whole before it plays); the packaged exe hasn't been run against a hosted server.
- **Flowform sign-in attempts are kept in SQLite** (`flowform_attempts`, 2026-11-07), with only hashes of the nonce, browser cookie and poll secret. They live 10 minutes, are spent once and are swept, so a server restart mid-sign-in no longer loses them (`test/flowform.test.ts`, the restart simulated by re-importing the module). If the server dies while `/done` is waiting on flowform, the window gets "interrupted, try again" after a minute, and a web sign-in cut off that way needs a second click. Not yet run against the real flowform.
- **Linking accounts is a command, not a screen.** One person can end up with two Foundation users: a dev-login address, and the address flowform signs them in with (flowform keeps the sign-up address, josh@normallab.co, for the Google identity josh@formindustries.co). `npm run link-accounts -w apps/server -- <keep> <other>` folds one into the other: sessions, files, runs and asks move, and the flowform link moves, so Google signs in as the kept user. Josh's accounts were linked on 2026-11-02 (kept josh.leong@gmail.com). There's no admin UI for this yet.
- **The REA job runner and the Vision brief are unexercised end to end.**
- **The app floorplan is a snapshot** (`apps/client/public/floorplan.html`, 2.3 MB). It doesn't update itself; refresh it after a release (`docs/floorplan.md`). The map's sources (`floorplan/`) stay out of git, so a fresh clone redraws them.
- **The ASCII wind shows nothing under reduced motion** (and since 2026-11-09 its "Move the cursor" line hides then too), on machines without WebGL2, or on software renderers — the Details original's choice; the sign-in page is plain paper there. It also fades out while the window is unfocused.
- **Hidden windows pause `requestAnimationFrame`.** Overlays catch up when shown. `?rafshim` (dev) drives frames from a timer for headless capture.
- **Panel iframes**: since 2026-11-09 (#80) a page served from Foundation's own origin (the app's, or its server's, where uploaded files live) runs without `allow-same-origin`, as an opaque origin, so it can't lift its sandbox and act with the person's session (`panels/frameSandbox.ts`). A page from anywhere else keeps its own origin (its sign-in and storage need it); that origin can't reach the board, but it can still do whatever its own site allows, so only add apps you trust. Serving our own tools from their own origins would let even those keep theirs.
- **A section narrower than its content** now fits it (2026-11-07): Rim-drive fan v3's 1,500 px of stages in a 1,080 px column should show whole at about 70 %. Not checked on that board (it isn't in this machine's data); the pipeline itself still doesn't reflow, it's scaled.
- **Fitted cards can be small.** At 40 % on a zoomed-out board a note's text is unreadable until you zoom in or Expand. The floor (`MIN_FIT`) is a judgement call; tune it on a real display.
- **An expanded section's wires**: wires between the sections that stepped back now fade with them (2026-11-07, `wireBackstage`); a wire with one end in the open section stays at full strength and can cross the open section's paper on its way out.
- **Documents are searched by their text layer only.** A scanned PDF (no text layer) is found by its name; there's no OCR. Legacy binary Office (`.doc`, `.ppt`, `.xls`), RTF, Keynote and Pages aren't read. An encrypted or zip64 document is found by name. Text past 200 k characters isn't indexed. A document that fails or times out isn't tried again until the extractor's version (`TEXT_V` in filetext.ts) is bumped.
- **A dropped folder over 400 files brings the first 400** and says so; the rest have to be dropped again. Folders dropped on empty paper (not a section) do nothing, as files there always have. A file whose upload fails stays as a waiting card until Retry or Close in the tray.
- **An expanded section keeps its floating cards where they were**, so a card dropped far right in the fitted view makes the open section wide, and the camera frames it smaller. Docked cards reflow; floaters don't.
- **The open section's rect eases with its card** (2026-11-09, #68): `sectionCardRect` follows the card's own CSS transition (`getAnimations`) while it opens or closes, so wires and landings travel with it. A section that changes size for another reason (a track resized by a peer) still snaps its wires.
- **Backward wires into the first column** have room to turn since 2026-11-09 (#66): new pipelines start 28 px further from the left wall (`LEFT_ROOM`). Pipelines made before keep their places; moving the first column right by hand gives the same room. (Was: the input sat 28 px from the wall, so the U-turn into it had about 14 px. A wider content pad on the left of pipeline sections would give it room.
- **Landings on one edge** stay on it at both ends since 2026-11-09 (#67, `spreadOnEdge`), and share a too-short edge evenly. A crowd still pushes a landing off the spot facing its other end, so its wire takes a short diagonal.
- **The "+" add-section buttons sit in the gaps between sections.** Since 2026-11-07 a press waits out the double-click window (300 ms, armed in blue at once) and a second click cancels it, so a double-click in the gap zooms instead of adding. A slower double-click than 300 ms (Windows allows up to 500) still adds; tune on a real mouse.
- **Throw momentum is generous** (velocity × 125 ms); tune on a real trackpad.
- **Raw colours left in components (12, allowlisted).** The old toolbar CSS and other dead rules are gone (2026-11-07), no component transitions `background`/`color` any more, and `--color-on-fill` and `--color-highlight` took white text on fills and the port gloss off the list; all enforced by `apps/client/test/design.test.ts`. What remains: the photo-thumbnail scrim, the orb artwork, the iframe canvas, Drive's brand green and four script-side colours. Hovers on table cells, gutters and top-bar buttons now change colour at once instead of fading.
- **Snap framing to a section** at 1440×900: an empty section frames its middle (at most 1000×700), so its welcome reads at about 105 %; sparse sections come up at the 105 % cap.
- **Huddles are a mesh**: each person sends one stream per other person, so beyond about six it strains uplinks. An SFU (LiveKit, self-hosted) is the step up. Only STUN by default, so people behind strict NATs won't connect until `FOUNDATION_ICE_SERVERS` names a TURN server.
- **Transcription uses the browser's speech service.** In Chrome it sends mic audio to Google's servers while you're in a huddle. Firefox has none (type notes instead). A local path (Whisper on the server, or Vision's `@pacific/voice`) would keep audio in-house. It also hasn't been run with a real voice (§3).
- **Opting out of recording is per person and on their word** (2026-11-18: *Don't record me*; their browser never records them, everyone sees *not recorded*, the session lists who opted out). Before that: anyone in a huddle can start it and everyone sees REC, but nobody could stay out while it runs except by leaving. Chrome's WebM recordings are written live with no duration; since 2026-11-07 the client writes Segment → Info → Duration (wall-clock start to stop) into each file before it uploads, so the archive's player shows the length and can seek (a 3 s recording in Chromium: `Infinity` → 2.98 s), and a file it can't safely patch uploads unchanged. Not yet run through a two-person huddle into the archive player.
- **Huddle state is the server's alone** (schema 6, 2026-11-07): who's in a huddle and whether it records live in the board's `huddles` map, not on the card, so moving the card while someone joins keeps both (`huddle.test.ts`, `e2e/huddle.spec.ts`, two people in Chromium). A stale `huddle` an older build writes onto a card is ignored, and stays there until the card is rewritten. After a crash, the live state still keeps the people who were in it until someone joins or leaves.
- **Slack is polled, not pushed, on a Slack card**: 5 s while a card is on screen. Threads show a reply count but can't be opened. The Slack board (2026-11-12) is pushed, through the Slack app's Socket Mode process; cards don't use that line yet.
- **The Slack board has not met real Slack** (2026-11-12): everything was checked against a stand-in. Unknown until it runs: rate limits on a workspace with many channels (the first read takes three channels at a time, 60 messages each; polling reads one channel every 5 s), HEIC and other image types Slack serves (only PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP and AVIF come in), and whether the linked app's manifest keeps the added scopes. Edits and deletions in Slack re-read the channel, but an image already brought in stays (cards aren't removed by the server). The Summary a real agent writes is only as good as `slack_read`'s 60 messages: threads aren't read. Agents-on has no expiry: it runs as the person who pressed ✦ until someone presses it again, within the caps (`FOUNDATION_SLACK_CURATE_MIN_MS`, `FOUNDATION_SLACK_CURATE_PER_HOUR`). The Slack app's check-in lends Foundation its bot token over loopback (kept in memory, never stored) so nothing has to be copied into `.env`; a hosted Foundation needs `SLACK_BOT_TOKEN` set instead. `apps/slack-agent` is the template as Slack ships it (its agent, Casey, answers DMs and mentions with demo data) plus `foundation/bridge.js`; its `npm run lint` fails on `.slack/config.json`, which the CLI writes.
- **Opening the side pane by hand beside a session on a narrow window** used to leave the grid a sliver. Since 2026-11-07 the pane stays (it was asked for) and the session narrows to make room (to its 360 px least), then search closes, then the pane narrows (`test/floor.test.ts`; at 1100 px a 460 px session went to 388 and the grid kept 440). The narrowed width is remembered.
- **Notes are CodeMirror on the note's Y.Text** (2026-11-08; the textarea that could interleave two people's letters in one word is gone, §3). What's left: a note's undo history lasts while the board is open (not across reloads; a Y.UndoManager isn't persisted). The editor is a lazy chunk (~100 kB gzipped), fetched when a pointer rests on a note or at idle once a board shows one; a page that never fetched it and then loses its network can't open a note until it's back (it says so). Enter types a plain newline: list markers aren't continued. Not yet seen in the packaged desktop window or on a real trackpad and IME (composition input is CodeMirror's, untested here).
- ~~**The floor's fade is a mask over the whole world layer.**~~ Replaced by a clip on 2026-11-09 (above). Its edges used to restyle the whole board on every frame of a pane opening; scoped to the mask on 2026-11-07, and measured at 0–0.8 % of frames over on a 200-card board (§5). It still re-rasterises a full-screen mask while a pane slides; profile on a weaker GPU.
- **The board's first load is one burst of main-thread work** (100–150 ms in the production build on a 200-card board: the sync message, then React rendering every section at once). Signing in now waits for it before dissolving, so the dissolve holds 60 fps, but the wind stutters for that moment, and switching boards has the same burst. A first render that yields (a transition, or sections mounted a few per frame) would remove it.
- **The dev server costs more than the build**: React's development build makes a card pick-up 120–190 ms and a framing key 55–70 ms there, against 0 frames over in the production build (§5). The section-scroll commit (500 ms after a scroll stops) is one ~65 ms frame in dev. Judge feel on `E2E_BUILD=1` or the packaged app.
- **The frames check needs a GPU and a display**: `e2e/frames.spec.ts` skips without `PERF_GPU=1`, so CI can't hold the 60 fps line yet. A self-hosted runner with a GPU could.
- **Live frames and zoom cost frames** in headless Chromium (about 5 % drop to 30 fps, §5). On a real GPU (RTX 5080, 2026-11-07) 20 live frames cost nothing measurable; a weaker GPU is still unmeasured.
- **Touched-card rings** come from the ids a session's tools named and, since 2026-11-07, from the board's activity its agents signed during each turn (helpers included), so a card a tool changed without naming it is ringed too. Checked on the scripted runtime (section, table and note ringed), which names its ids anyway; the unnamed case is covered by reading, not yet seen with a real model.
